# Codex combined COUNCIL + GROUNDED REVIEW — EG-1 English retrain #1 plan

You are the sole reviewer for this plan (council is deliberately replaced by you because this plan hinges on live-web facts — dataset licenses — that a no-repo, no-web council cannot check). You have repo read access and WEB SEARCH is ON. The plan text is at the END of this prompt.

# THE PLAN FOLLOWS
# Issue #1570 — EG-1 English retrain #1: lists, formatting, restraint — 2026-07-16

Status: DRAFT skeleton. Data-sourcing section (§3.2) PENDING web-research agent. Stable sections filled from Codex overnight research (read, not re-derived) + eg1-model-provenance + Fluid-1 teardown.

## Preface — Lane + Live UAT declaration
- Primary lane: **Eval-harness** (data generation + training + scoring under `scripts/eval/`), gitignored training data (`Docs/dev-tooling`). Final artifact swap is a separate **distribution** step (manifest bump / R2), NOT in this plan's first PR.
- Live UAT: **Y** at the artifact-swap step only (dictate real spoken lists through the running app, verify bullet output + no meaning damage). The data/training/eval phase is graded on the held-out corpora, not the app.
- User Rubric: English-primary power user who dictates lists/emails/notes. (Full rubric to fill.)

## 0. TL;DR
Fix EG-1's three measured English weaknesses — under-activation of genuine lists (esp. two-item/scoped), leftover spoken-command residue, without breaking its currently-strong restraint — by adding ~1,000 real-web-grounded, balanced English training pairs to the existing 5,656 and retraining the SAME base (Qwen3-4B) on the SAME shipped prompt via the documented QLoRA pipeline. Grade the quantized artifact on the existing held-out English sets. Ship as a weights-only EG-1 update if it clears the bar.

## 1. Problem
Users report EG-1 is weak at exactly what makes cloud polishers popular: turning spoken lists into clean bullets and formatting email/notes structure. Codex's overnight structural scan proved the cause is a starved training diet, not a base-model ceiling.

## 2. Goals & non-goals
### 2.1 Goals
- Raise genuine-list activation (esp. two-item + scoped-implicit) measurably.
- Remove leftover spoken formatting commands ("make a bullet list of…").
- Hold restraint (do not convert ordinary prose to lists) — currently ~100%.
- Keep core English polish non-inferior (no worse than -2pp).
- Keep the same base + same shipped prompt + same download size (~2.7GB) → clean weights-only hot-swap.

### 2.2 Non-goals
- Multilingual (that is retrain #2, on top of this).
- Base-model switch (Gemma etc. stays benched unless this plateaus).
- Prompt-contract change (behavior goes in weights; prompt template id unchanged).
- Building the full public-speech benchmark lab (rejected as over-engineering).

## 2.5 Grounding brief (evidence, read not re-derived)
**Weakness diagnosis (Codex overnight, OVERNIGHT-LOG DATA-004 / EVAL-005/006/007):**
- 259/5,656 training outputs are lists; item-count skew: 2-item=**3**, 3-item=179, 4=25, 5=30, 6=2, 7=19, 8=1.
- 80 `LF-*` + 80 `LFT-*` rows leaked from the old benchmark into training → old 94-95% headline is contaminated (82% train/eval family overlap), NOT held-out quality.
- Current EG-1 + shipped prompt: positive-list activation 72/100 (behavior), 71/100 strict; two-item activation 11/20. UNDER-activates.
- Restraint currently strong: 100/100 restraint, 98/100 strict on the 100-trap set.
- Prompt-only "list-v2" boosts activation (86/100) but damages meaning (split "picked up dinner"), loses scope on 5 explicit-list cases, leaves spoken-command headers, and REGRESSES restraint. → behavior must be trained into weights at prompt parity, not prompted.

**Training pipeline (eg1-model-provenance, settled #1265):**
- Base Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507; QLoRA (Unsloth, r16/a32, lr 5e-5, 2 epochs, response-only loss) on the RTX 4090 (AlienSV, $0); merge → GGUF → Q5_K_M → judge the QUANTIZED artifact.
- Training prompt MUST equal shipped prompt (drift = ±18pp). Weights-only update keeps promptTemplateID → hot-swap per eg1-operations.

**Reusable assets that already exist (Explore inventory + NOVEL-LIST-CORPUS-RUN):**
- Working English list+restraint generator (built & audited the 75+75 corpus).
- Held-out English dev eval sets: 75 positive-list + 75 restraint (`eg1_english_list_pilot75_v2_bundle`, leakage-clean, gitignored), plus two-item 20-case and positive-100/restraint-100 materialized prompt sets.
- Intact v2 training data (5,656 pairs).

**Ceiling context (Fluid-1 teardown):** competitor trained on ~100k real dictations; our realistic internal target is 20-50k; per-behavior we are near-zero on lists → high buffer, cheap first win.

### Grounding: single-authority / negative claims
- Eval harness: `scripts/eval/` is the ONLY polish eval harness (never fork). New data + scorers extend it in place.
- Leakage: all new training pairs must be family-disjoint from the held-out eval sets (exact + token + n-gram + embedding screen) — reuse the overnight leakage screen, not a new one.

## 3. Design
### 3.1 Approach
Add ~1,000 new balanced English pairs to the 5,656 (dropping the ~160 leaked `LF-*`/`LFT-*` rows so we don't re-contaminate the eval), retrain per the documented recipe under the CURRENT shipped prompt, quantize, grade on held-out sets.

New English 1,000 stratification (from TRAINING-DATA-DESIGN-V2, adopted):
- 400 positive lists — item counts 2/3/5/7 balanced; list types (explicit bullets, explicit numbering, scoped-implicit, bare-label, correction/format-command) balanced; domains (work, personal, technical, medical, legal) balanced; lengths balanced.
- 400 matched prose-restraint — each paired to a positive family, same domain/length/difficulty; restraint types: incidental enumerations, narrative action sequences, compound nouns ("laptop charger"), quoted content, alternatives/contrasts, shared modifiers, two-item policy-boundary prose.
- 200 core polish — filler removal, self-correction, punctuation, entity/number/date preservation, minimal edit.

### 3.2 Data sourcing — license-verified (round #1 is GREEN-license ONLY)
Real content grounds one side of every pair; the other side is synthesized. Two paradigms:
- **(A) Degrade:** real clean structured text = GOLD output → synthesize the messy spoken input (flatten bullets to run-on speech, inject disfluencies + spoken commands).
- **(B) Minimal-clean:** real spoken transcript = INPUT → minimally-edited output that STAYS prose.

**Round #1 sources — all GREEN (permit commercial ML + derivative-model distribution):**
| Source | License (verified) | Side / role | Weakness |
|---|---|---|---|
| OpenAssistant oasst1/oasst2 | Apache-2.0 | GOLD — real bullet/numbered answers, drafted emails, matched prose answers | W1,W2,W3,W4 (primary gold pillar) |
| AMI Meeting Corpus | CC-BY-4.0 | INPUT — spontaneous meeting speech; incidental enumerations, "first…then" narratives, compound nouns | W4 (primary restraint pillar), W2 |
| VoxPopuli (en) | CC0-1.0 | INPUT — formal spoken monologue, some spoken lists | W1, W4, cleanup |
| Common Voice CV11–CV16 | CC0 (older releases direct-download) | INPUT — clean single-sentence prose | W4, cleanup |
| FLEURS (en) | CC-BY-4.0 | INPUT — read sentences | cleanup, W4 |
| US federal gov docs (GovInfo/Federal Register) | Public domain (17 U.S.C. §105) | GOLD — agendas, minutes, checklists, plain-language procedures | W1, W2 |

**Attribution to carry on the model card:** AMI, FLEURS (CC-BY-4.0) require attribution; VoxPopuli (CC0) requests acknowledging the European Parliament; OASST (Apache-2.0) and Common Voice (CC0) need none. Screen GovInfo docs for embedded third-party copyrighted excerpts.

**DEFERRED / founder call (NOT in round #1):**
- **Enron / AESLC emails** — YELLOW: FERC public record, no clean license grant. Would add real corporate-email density for W2, but round #1 covers W2 adequately via OASST drafted-email answers + GovInfo memos. Excluded from round #1; founder can approve as a round-#2 add.
- **Stack Exchange** — YELLOW: pre-July-2024 dumps only (CC-BY-SA, share-alike + attribution); 2024+ dumps forbid commercial/LLM use. Low value here; skip round #1.

**RED — excluded, do not re-investigate:** WikiHow (CC-BY-NC-SA, non-commercial), TED-LIUM (NC-ND), Spotify podcasts (research-only), post-2024 Stack Exchange.

**Slice → source map (round #1, ~1,000 pairs):**
| Slice | Count | Paradigm | Input | Gold |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| "Make a list" | 400 | A degrade | synthesized spoken | OASST bullet/numbered answers + GovInfo agendas/checklists |
| "Keep as prose" (matched restraint) | 400 | B + some A | AMI + VoxPopuli real transcripts (incidental/narrative/compound) + OASST prose answers | minimally-cleaned prose |
| General cleanup | 200 | B | Common Voice / VoxPopuli | punctuation + caps normalized |

**W4 design guard:** the 400 restraint cases must be *matched* to the 400 list cases on surface trigger words ("and", "then", commas, "first/second") so the model learns the list boundary, not a keyword. AMI is the highest-value negative source (natural "laptop, charger, and cables" compounds + "first we…then we…" narratives that must stay prose).

### 3.3 Generation method
Real content grounds one side; a strong teacher model (different author/critic models, per design) synthesizes the paired side into ASR-style raw input / minimally-edited gold output. Automated quality + leakage screen before acceptance. English needs NO native-human roster (that was a multilingual concern — the overnight custody/registry machinery is out of scope here).

### 3.4 Prompt parity — SETTLED (founder 2026-07-16)
Train + infer against the CURRENT shipped prompt. Behavior lives in weights; promptTemplateID unchanged; clean hot-swap. (Rejected: adopting list-v2 as a new prompt contract — the overnight A/B showed prompt-only changes damage restraint and add residue, and it would force a code-lane prompt-family change.)

## 3a/3b/3c — placement / ownership / single-authority
- No new app module or coordinator. No runtime ownership change. The model artifact is the only shipped surface, swapped via the existing hot-swap contract. Single-authority: eval harness stays the sole scorer; leakage screen stays the sole gate.

## 4-9 Runtime state / lifecycle / consumer / failure-mode / signals / fallback audits
**N/A — no app-runtime state change in this plan.** This is a training-data + model-weights change. The pipeline (generate → screen → train → merge → quantize → judge) is an offline dev-tooling flow with no in-app FSM, no async runtime edges, no caller-visible signal change. The eventual artifact swap rides the ALREADY-AUDITED EG-1 hot-swap + model-delivery contract, whose lifecycle audits are owned there. Any prompt/template change would trigger those audits — this plan makes none.

## 10. Mechanism — reuse what exists, verify what's assumed
**PROVEN reusable (overnight branch, built + audited the 75+75 corpus):**
- English-list data: `generate_eg1_english_list_benchmark.py`, `eg1_english_list_contract.py`, `assemble_eg1_english_list_pilot.py`, `make_eg1_english_list_pilot_manifest.py`. Currently produce EVAL corpus by synthesis → EXTEND to (a) produce TRAINING pairs and (b) ground the gold/input side in the new web sources (OASST/AMI/GovInfo) instead of pure synthesis.
- Scorers: `score_eg1_english_list_ab.py`, `score_two_item_lists.py`, `score_list_structure.py`, `score_eg1_english_list_novel.py`, `build_eg1_english_list_blind_review.py`, `unblind_eg1_english_list_semantic_review.py`.
- Leakage screen: reuse the overnight scanner (do NOT build a new one) to keep new training pairs family-disjoint from the held-out eval sets.
- Held-out eval corpus + training data v2: already present.

**ASSUMED — must verify before build (Codex, repo-grounded + possibly rig check):**
- **Training script.** The SHIPPED EG-1 was trained by the #1265 Unsloth recipe (r16/a32, lr 5e-5, 2 epochs, response-only loss) on the AlienSV 4090 — that pipeline is NOT committed in-repo; only `train_sft_v2.jsonl` is. The only committed trainer is the overnight `train_eg1_multilingual_qlora.py`, which is UNPROVEN for our production recipe (it trained the rejected Gemma checkpoints). Round #1 must train via the PROVEN #1265 recipe. OPEN: confirm the exact proven training script on the rig, or verify the committed overnight trainer reproduces the #1265 config byte-for-byte before trusting it. Do not assume.
- `generate_eg1_multilingual_sft.py` exists (overnight SFT generator) — assess whether it's the input-degradation tool to extend, or whether the English-list generator is the better base.

**New/edited files (round #1):**
- Extended English data generator + a web-source ingest step (OASST/AMI/GovInfo → strata) — under `scripts/eval/`.
- New gitignored training jsonl: the ~1,000 new English pairs + the merged augmented corpus (5,656 − ~160 leaked + 1,000 new).
- Leakage receipt (reuse screen).
- Training invocation (proven recipe, rig).
- Scoring invocation on held-out sets (reuse scorers).
- At SHIP only: manifest/version bump for the weights-only artifact swap (separate PR, distribution lane).

## 11. Testing / eval — the go/no-go bar
Grade the QUANTIZED artifact on the held-out English sets, report four axes (Codex-defined):
1. Positive-list activation ↑ (esp. two-item + scoped) vs current EG-1.
2. False-list restraint held (no worse than current 98/100 strict).
3. Meaning damage: ZERO new S3/S4 (scope/entity/number/timing) damage.
4. Core English polish non-inferior (≥ -2pp).
Paired per-case strict outcomes + exact McNemar + bootstrap CI. Three training seeds before any ship claim (report each, don't average away a bad run).
Live UAT at swap: dictate real spoken lists through the app, confirm bullet output + no damage + latency < budget.

## 12. Blast radius & rollback
Weights-only artifact swap; rollback = repoint manifest to the current EG-1 shard set. No code path change. Same download size class.

## 13. Ship criteria
All four eval axes pass on the quantized artifact across 3 seeds → build the ship PR (manifest/version bump + Live UAT). If restraint breaks or activation doesn't move, DO NOT ship: adjust the data balance and re-run (one smoke + one full dev run per recipe, per stop rules).

## 14. Open questions
- **Training script (grounding, for Codex):** confirm the proven #1265 Unsloth recipe/script on the rig vs the committed-but-unproven overnight trainer. Round #1 uses the proven recipe. (§10.)
- Drop vs replace the ~160 leaked `LF-*`/`LFT-*` rows? Lean: drop for round #1 (simpler; corpus 5,656 → ~5,496 + 1,000 new ≈ 6,496).
- [DEFERRED — founder call, not blocking] Add YELLOW Enron/AESLC emails for extra W2 email density in round #2? Excluded from round #1.
- Teacher model for synthesizing the messy-input side: reuse the v2 teacher (gemini-2.5-flash) or a stronger current model? Negligible API cost either way (existing credits).

## Rules

Global. Every project. Project CLAUDE.md must not restate — extend or scope only.

1. **Validate, don't guess.** Run the command, read the file, test the path. No assumptions stated as facts. Production-grade from day one.
2. **Empirical debugging.** Hypothesis → falsifiable test → run → fix. No sweeping changes based on theory.
3. **chrome-devtools-mcp first.** Default driver for any browser work. → [browser-automation.md](/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/browser/browser-automation.md)
4. **DRY.** Every fact lives in one place. Reference, don't restate.
5. **Council when unsure.** Canonical invocation → [llm-council.md](/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/ai/llm-council.md).
6. **No em-dashes or en-dashes in human-facing content.** Marketing, in-app strings, email, Discord, release notes. Use periods, commas, colons, semicolons, parens, or restructure.
7. **Edit in place, never delete-and-recreate.** File ID is a primary key. Deleting breaks share links, permissions, comments, history.
8. **Never ask if done.** Keep working. Saurabh decides when to stop.
9. **Session wind-down.** Local session-log + Discord post via canonical script. Promote persistent infra to CLAUDE.md/knowledge before logging. → [session-wind-down.md](/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/session/session-wind-down.md)
10. **Write for machine reading.** Every token taxes every future session. Optimize for grep retrieval. → [writing-style.md](/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/dev/writing-style.md)
11. **Match existing style; diff every edit.** If diff is larger than the change, stop and investigate.
12. **Discord webhooks: use canonical script; read webhook contract first.** → [discord-webhooks.md](/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/session/discord-webhooks.md)
13. **Plan before non-trivial code.** Don't jump to code on non-trivial work — plan it and get an explicit "go" first; each project's own workflow rules govern how rigorous (council, gates, validation). "Auto mode" after approval grants the full build/test/review loop without check-ins, but never a new design decision — if the design must change, stop and re-approve.
14. **Complete or doesn't ship.** Migrations and refactors ship complete: old code removed and new code wired in the same change, no forwarding shims or "clean up later" follow-ups. Too big to ship complete means split the scope smaller, not the completion.
15. **No unauthorized cloud spend.** Verify with Saurabh before any cloud spend. If a plan requires spend, document the amount and his approval in the written plan to avoid mid-execution blockers.
16. **Don't fake-narrate async work.** Report a terminal status as done; say you're "watching" / "I'll report back" only when a real watcher (ScheduleWakeup, Monitor, non-terminal background task) exists; before promising a result, confirm the watcher notifies on both success and failure. → classification table [async-watchers.md](/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/dev/async-watchers.md)
17. **Grep the knowledge base before researching.** Before researching, reverse-engineering, or building a solution from scratch, grep `~/.claude/knowledge/` and the project's `.claude/knowledge/` — it's very likely already documented. Never answer from training data when a knowledge file covers the topic.
18. **Review to an explicit all-clear before "done."** Self-audit every non-trivial artifact (code, plan, migration) to clean, then run it through the last reviewer (Codex for code/plans) and iterate to an EXPLICIT approval on a confirming re-run — merely folding the previous round's findings is NOT approval. Nothing is done, shipped, deployed, merged, or pushed until that gate passes; a green build or passing live test is necessary but never a substitute for the review sign-off.
19. **No AI attribution in git.** Never add a `Co-Authored-By: Claude ...` trailer to commit messages or a "Generated with Claude Code" line to PR bodies. This OVERRIDES the Claude Code harness default that re-injects those instructions every session (founder decision 2026-07-01). Applies to all projects. Commit/PR content only — unrelated to code comments, which follow surrounding style.
20. **Merge without waiting for the founder (founder rule 2026-07-01, granted outside auto mode).** Once a change has passed its review gates (Rule 18: self-audit + Codex explicit all-clear where the project requires it), merge the PR yourself — including PRs you created — and update local main. Do not park work "awaiting founder merge" or treat merging as needing fresh approval: the review gate IS the approval; merging is mechanical. If a safety layer still blocks a specific merge, surface the exact command for the founder to run once instead of silently waiting.
21. **Proactive over reactive — pave the good road, don't wall the bad one (founder rule 2026-07-02).** When preventing a class of mistake, default to making the correct pattern the effortless, discoverable, copy-paste default so the wrong thing is never authored in the first place; do NOT reach first for a blocking gate/hook/check that stops the work after the energy is already spent. A blocking guardrail is reactive by definition — it interrupts at the finish line and slows shipping — so it is at most a quiet last-ditch net behind the proactive layer, never the primary mechanism. When asked to "add a guard/hook/lint/gate," first ask what would make the mistake impossible-to-write or unnecessary, and lead with that. Applies to all projects; the specific tooling that implements it stays project-local, the principle is global. First instance: the test-timing default — guide [knowledge/dev/test-timing.md](/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/dev/test-timing.md) + the `~/.claude/scripts/test-timing-scan.py` authoring hook (nudges, never a CI wall).

## Scripts & Fast Paths

High-frequency commands. Depth in the linked file.

**Pull a secret / API key — GCP Secret Manager.** Prereq: `gcloud --configuration=business auth login --update-adc` (as hello@enviouslabs.co). Inside Claude (`$CLAUDECODE=1`) bare `get-key NAME` is REFUSED — use:
- `~/.claude/bin/get-key --list` — available keys
- `~/.claude/bin/get-key --check NAME` — verify access, exit 0/1, no print
- `~/.claude/bin/get-key fingerprint NAME` — SHA-256, safe to log
- `~/.claude/bin/get-key launch NAME ENV_VAR -- cmd…` — bridge value to a process, no stdout leak
→ [secrets-management.md](/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/secrets-management.md)

**Cloud credits — AWS Bedrock ($1,120, exp 2028-04) / Azure OpenAI ($5K, exp 2027-01).** Route here before direct OpenAI/Anthropic APIs. Keys via `get-key launch`:
- AWS: `aws-bedrock-access-key-id` + `aws-bedrock-secret-access-key`, region `us-east-1`, model IDs `us.anthropic.*`
- Azure: `azure-openai-endpoint` + `azure-openai-key`; `az` CLI needs `--tenant dcf062e4-4887-4190-b873-579cea214f6c`
→ [cloud-llm-credits.md](/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/ai/cloud-llm-credits.md)

**Council — GPT + Gemini second opinion.** Always background; one call fires both providers; reuse the `--session` name across rounds (never `-v2`). Absolute paths required. Use for **strategic questions only (1-2 rounds)** — pure reasoning, no repo access. Then ground the strategy against real code with Codex.
- `Bash run_in_background:true ~/.claude/bin/get-key launch gemini-api-key GEMINI_API_KEY -- ~/.claude/bin/get-key launch openai-api-key OPENAI_API_KEY -- ~/.local/bin/council ask --reasoning medium --session <kebab-topic-YYYY-MM-DD> "PROMPT"`
→ [llm-council.md](/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/ai/llm-council.md)

**Codex — GPT-5.x grounding against real code.** Unlike council (pure reasoning), Codex reads the actual codebase, runs commands, and validates strategy against what's really there. Read [codex-cli.md](/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/ai/codex-cli.md) before the first Codex call in a task, including a resume. Flow: council converges on a strategy → hand Codex the plan → it checks feasibility against real files (require file:line citations) → implement. **Default path: run `~/.claude/bin/codex-5.6-sol exec` directly via `Bash run_in_background:true`** (fallback `codex-5.3-spark` if usage-capped — never bare `codex`, never hand-edit `~/.codex/config.toml`'s model default: [codex-cli.md](/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/ai/codex-cli.md) RULE: two-named-scripts-not-one-mutable-default) — leaner than the subagent, the output file is yours to read for live progress, and the harness re-invokes you on exit either way (same notification). Reserve the `codex:rescue` subagent for interactive multi-round rescue where you want an agent to iterate with Codex for you. EnviousWispr uses the shell path exclusively. Effort is pinned by the script (sol → medium), prompt via stdin:
- `Bash run_in_background:true` → `cat <prompt-file> | ~/.claude/bin/codex-5.6-sol exec --sandbox read-only --skip-git-repo-check > <out> 2>&1`
- Multi-round: capture `thread_id` from line 1, then `~/.claude/bin/codex-5.6-sol exec resume <UUID> "delta"` (never fresh `exec` for round 2+). If overriding sandbox, the exec-level flag goes before the subcommand: `exec --sandbox read-only resume <UUID> "delta"`. NEVER shell `&` / `</dev/null`.
→ [codex-cli.md](/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/ai/codex-cli.md)

**Browser / DevTools — chrome-devtools-mcp.** Session startup: Cmd+Q Chrome fully → reopen → verify `chrome://inspect/#remote-debugging` toggle ON → start Claude. Recovery on `about:blank`: `pkill -9 -f "chrome-devtools-mcp"`.
→ [browser-automation.md](/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/browser/browser-automation.md)

# Agent Instructions

## Mandatory Claude-Brain Bootstrap

`AGENTS.md` is the Codex adapter. It must route Codex into the existing Claude Code brain, not copy or replace it.

Before the first meaningful repo action in every new task, Codex must complete this sequence:

1. Complete the global bootstrap owned by `/Users/m4pro_sv/.codex/AGENTS.md`: read `/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/CLAUDE.md` in full. If the current runner did not load the global Codex adapter, read the global Claude file directly now.
2. Read this repo's `CLAUDE.md` in full. It is the EnviousWispr master router and project source of truth.
3. Read every Tier 1 file in full:
   - `.claude/knowledge/gotchas.md`
   - `.claude/knowledge/conventions.md`
   - `.claude/knowledge/github-light.md`
4. Classify the task using the pathways in both Claude files, then read every matching Tier 2 rule or knowledge file before planning, editing, or operating a tool. Important always-on pathways:
   - Shipped code or implementation work: `.claude/rules/workflow-process.md`, `.claude/rules/validation-discipline.md`, `.claude/rules/tools-and-apps.md`, and `.claude/rules/grounding-discipline.md`.
   - Git or GitHub mutation: `.claude/knowledge/github.md` in addition to Tier 1 `github-light.md`.
   - Workflow, gate, hook, plan-template, or agent-instruction changes: `.claude/knowledge/workflow-effectiveness.md` before proposing the change.
   - GCP, Google Workspace, OAuth, API keys, secrets, or cloud spend: `/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/secrets-management.md`, the relevant file under `/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/google/`, and cost guidance routed from the global `CLAUDE.md`. Never print a secret; use the approved `get-key` interfaces.
5. Use Tier 3 only when a Tier 1 or Tier 2 file points to it or the task needs historical evidence. Start from `.claude/knowledge/tier-3-directory.md`; never bulk-load Tier 3.

Reading rules:

- Re-read the live files for the current task. Memory or a prior task's read is navigation help, not proof.
- Read selected files through EOF. If tool output truncates, continue from the truncation point.
- When a pathway file is large, use its `## FACT:` / `## RULE:` headings to find the task-relevant entry, then read that complete entry and any owner it links to. Do not treat opening the first lines as compliance.
- Before substantive work, give Saurabh one short receipt: `Knowledge bootstrap complete. T2: <files>. T3: <files or none>.`
- Claude-only hooks and path loaders are not assumed to run under Codex. Follow the policies they encode and run the repo's canonical validation scripts explicitly when the workflow requires them.
- If `AGENTS.md` conflicts with a Claude file only because Codex has a provider-specific guardrail, `AGENTS.md` wins for Codex. Otherwise the global and project Claude files remain authoritative.

## Working Relationship

Saurabh is the founder, product owner, and final decision-maker for EnviousWispr.
Claude Code is the CTO / lead engineering authority for the project.
Codex acts as a senior developer working under that Claude Code CTO model.

Codex should help in two main modes:

1. **Implementation work** - investigate, plan, edit code, run validation, commit, push, and submit PRs when asked.
2. **Miscellaneous founder support** - answer questions, inspect the repo, debug local issues, summarize tradeoffs, review plans, and give practical recommendations.

Codex may submit PRs, but must not merge PRs unless Saurabh explicitly asks for that specific merge. A finished PR handoff should make the branch, PR link, validation, and remaining risks clear enough for Saurabh and Claude Code to decide the next step.

Keep chat with Saurabh in simple English. Use technical detail in code, commits, tool calls, and PR notes when needed, but do not make Saurabh translate engineering jargon just to understand status or decisions.

## Codex PR Merge Guardrail

Default rule: Codex submits work; Saurabh / Claude Code decide whether to merge.

Codex must not run any PR merge path unless Saurabh explicitly asks for that exact merge in the current task. This includes:

- `gh pr merge`
- `gh api .../pulls/<N>/merge`
- GitHub GraphQL auto-merge mutations
- GitHub connector merge tools such as `merge_pull_request` or `enable_auto_merge`

There is no local script guardrail for this — the rule above is the guardrail. `.codex/scripts/block-pr-merge.sh` is absent from the current checkout and has no tracked Git history. Do not look for it, and do not treat its absence as permission to merge.

## Source of Truth

`CLAUDE.md` is the project brain. `AGENTS.md` is the Codex adapter.

Do not duplicate the full Claude workflow here. Before starting meaningful repo work, read `CLAUDE.md` and the relevant files under `.claude/knowledge/` and `.claude/rules/`. Current issue comments are also source of truth for active work.

The goal is to reuse what Saurabh and Claude Code have already built. Prefer existing project decisions, scripts, templates, issue comments, validation gates, and coding patterns over inventing a new process. If the repo already has a way to do something, follow it. Propose a new approach only when the existing project material is missing, stale, or clearly does not fit the task, and say why.

This project uses **GitHub Issues** for issue tracking via the `gh` CLI.

## Project Knowledge First

Before starting issue work, Codex should use the existing EnviousWispr project folder as the source of truth for repo knowledge: read `CLAUDE.md`, relevant `.claude/knowledge/*`, `.claude/rules/*`, and current GitHub issue comments instead of relying on memory or guessing.

Default stance: verify from the repo first, then answer or act. Memory is only a shortcut to the right files, not proof.

## Codex Issue Preflight

Before claiming or implementing any GitHub issue, Codex must run a short preflight and write down the result in its working notes or issue comment if useful:

1. Refresh the exact issue with comments: `gh issue view <N> --json title,body,state,labels,comments` (on a non-TTY pipe, bare `--comments` prints only the comments and prints nothing when the issue has zero comments — deliberate gh behavior since v1.4.0, not a regression; use JSON to return the body and comments together).
2. Check current open PRs: `gh pr list --state open --limit 40 --json number,title,headRefName,url`.
3. Check whether the work already landed on `main`: search recent commits and grep the named files.
4. Decide the issue shape before editing:
   - direct code PR
   - tracker cleanup / child issue creation
   - already complete and ready to close
   - research experiment only
5. Only add `in-progress` after the issue still clearly needs Codex work.

For experiment issues, use a hard stop rule: one smoke run, one full benchmark, then stop unless the result improves the target metric without meaningful regressions. Do not hand-tune repeatedly in the same PR lane after the benchmark says the shape is weak.

When a PR is opened and Codex is no longer actively editing that issue, remove the `in-progress` label or leave a clear issue comment saying it is waiting on PR review.

## Codex Review Defaults

For Codex review work, match Claude Code's Codex setup — two named, model-pinned scripts, never the bare `codex` binary and never a hand-edited `~/.codex/config.toml` default (`~/.claude/knowledge/ai/codex-cli.md` RULE: two-named-scripts-not-one-mutable-default):

```bash
~/.claude/bin/codex-5.6-sol review --base origin/main
```

Fallback if `codex-5.6-sol` errors with a usage-limit message: `~/.claude/bin/codex-5.3-spark review --base origin/main` (pins high effort). Both scripts already pin their own reasoning effort — do not add a competing `-c model_reasoning_effort=...`.

The canonical EW flow commits the work first, then reviews the committed diff (`workflow-process.md` RULE: local-codex-iteration-before-push). A PreToolUse gate (`.claude/scripts/check-selfreview-before-codex.sh`) blocks `codex review` until the HEAD commit message carries the single-authority self-audit (`Self-audit-grep:` / `Self-audit-owner:`). For an ad-hoc uncommitted review (`--uncommitted`) the self-audit is not yet in a commit, so either commit first (preferred) or bypass with `SKIP_SELFAUDIT=1` (audit-logged).

If `codex-5.6-sol` errors on the model itself (not a usage-limit message), treat that as a CLI/version mismatch and report it plainly — do not silently fall back to Spark for a non-capacity reason.

**Every finding you report ships its fix.** Give the exact replacement text — real code in the file's language, or verbatim prose for a doc — not a description of the change. If you genuinely cannot write it, mark the finding `DIAGNOSIS-ONLY` and say why in one line. The author adjudicates: adopts, modifies (and tells you what changed), or rejects with evidence. A finding without proposed text is incomplete.

**If your current task is reviewing a git diff, read `docs/codex-diff-review.md` first.** It is short, and it is the protocol. Do not apply it while writing code, answering a question, or reviewing a plan — the default there is the smallest correct change, not consolidation.

A scoped `codex review` (`--base` / `--uncommitted` / `--commit`) cannot accept a custom prompt; the target is an enum and the prompt is one of its variants. This file is the only channel that reaches the diff reviewer, which is why the pointer lives here.

For grounded reviews, the canonical Q1/Q2/Q3/Q4 review contract lives in `.claude/rules/workflow-process.md` (§5 and the grounded-review mechanics). Model + reasoning effort are pinned by `~/.claude/bin/codex-5.6-sol` / `codex-5.3-spark`, not by this repo's `.codex/config.toml` (that file only sets `sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"` — it has no model/effort keys; do not assume it can override the two-script default).

(Historical note: earlier drafts referenced standalone template files `~/.claude/grounded-review-template.md` and `.codex/{grounded-review-template,audit-prompt,plan-review-prompt}.md`, and a `codex-companion.mjs` wrapper. None of those exist anymore — the contract is the `workflow-process.md` rules, and Codex is invoked via the direct CLI, not a wrapper.)

## Quick Reference

```bash
gh issue list --state open            # Find available work
gh issue view <number>                # View issue details
gh issue edit <N> --add-label "in-progress"  # Claim work
gh issue close <N> --comment "reason" # Complete work
```

## Non-Interactive Shell Commands

**ALWAYS use non-interactive flags** with file operations to avoid hanging on confirmation prompts.

Shell commands like `cp`, `mv`, and `rm` may be aliased to include `-i` (interactive) mode on some systems, causing the agent to hang indefinitely waiting for y/n input.

**Use these forms instead:**
```bash
# Force overwrite without prompting
cp -f source dest           # NOT: cp source dest
mv -f source dest           # NOT: mv source dest
rm -f file                  # NOT: rm file

# For recursive operations
rm -rf directory            # NOT: rm -r directory
cp -rf source dest          # NOT: cp -r source dest
```

**Other commands that may prompt:**
- `scp` - use `-o BatchMode=yes` for non-interactive
- `ssh` - use `-o BatchMode=yes` to fail instead of prompting
- `apt-get` - use `-y` flag
- `brew` - use `HOMEBREW_NO_AUTO_UPDATE=1` env var

## Issue Tracking with GitHub Issues

**IMPORTANT**: This project uses **GitHub Issues** for ALL issue tracking. Do NOT use markdown TODOs, task lists, or other tracking methods for persistent work.

### Labels

**Priority:** P0-critical, P1-high, P2-medium, P3-low, P4-backlog
**Type:** epic, feature, bug, task, research
**Status:** in-progress

### Workflow for AI Agents

1. **Check ready work**: `gh issue list --state open`
2. **Claim your task**: `gh issue edit <N> --add-label "in-progress"`
3. **Work on it**: Implement, test, document
4. **Discover new work?** Create linked issue:
   - `gh issue create --title "Found bug" --body "Details" --label "P1-high,bug"`
5. **Submit work**: push the branch and open a PR when the task needs review.
6. **Complete**: close the issue only when the requested work is truly done, or leave a clear issue comment if Claude Code / Saurabh still need to review or merge.

### Important Rules

- Use GitHub Issues for ALL persistent task tracking
- Always assign priority and type labels to new issues
- Use `gh issue comment` for audit trail
- Check `gh issue list` before asking "what should I work on?"
- TaskCreate/TaskList are for intra-session steps only

## Landing the Plane (Session Completion)

**When ending an implementation work session**, you MUST complete ALL steps below. Local work is NOT complete until `git push` succeeds.

**MANDATORY WORKFLOW:**

1. **File issues for remaining work** - Create GitHub Issues for anything that needs follow-up
2. **Run quality gates** (if code changed) - Tests, linters, builds
3. **Update issue status** - Close finished work, or comment on in-progress items with exact next steps
4. **PUSH TO REMOTE** - This is MANDATORY:
   ```bash
   git pull --rebase
   git push
   git status  # MUST show "up to date with origin"
   ```
5. **Open or update PR when appropriate** - Submit the PR, but do not merge it unless Saurabh explicitly asked for that merge
6. **Clean up** - Clear stashes, prune remote branches
7. **Verify** - All changes committed AND pushed
8. **Hand off** - Provide context for Saurabh, Claude Code, or the next session

**CRITICAL RULES:**
- Work is NOT complete until `git push` succeeds
- NEVER stop before pushing - that leaves work stranded locally
- NEVER say "ready to push when you are" - YOU must push
- If push fails, resolve and retry until it succeeds
- Do NOT merge a PR unless Saurabh explicitly asks you to merge that PR
# EnviousWispr

Fastest commercial-grade voice-to-text on macOS. Record, transcribe, AI polish, clipboard in under a second. The bar is WisprFlow: match or beat at better speed. **Launched** and shipping.

## 🚧 WORK IN PROGRESS — Heartpath Refactor (started 2026-07-12)

**Current focus. The release is HELD for it. Read before touching audio, capture, recording, recovery, or pill/overlay code.**

Goal: make dictation boringly, simply, robustly work — by DELETING the architecture that makes it fragile, not by handling its failures better. ~22 issues (#1511) are ONE chain reaction, not 22 bugs: three ways to open a mic → one can abort the process → so capture runs in its own process → so every call is a message that can arrive late or twice → so we need identity/staleness/reconnect machinery → so we need detectors → so we need six different components writing sentences to the user. Pull the first link and the rest collapse.

| Read | For |
|---|---|
| [`docs/heartpath-refactor/README.md`](docs/heartpath-refactor/README.md) | **START HERE.** Next action, the settled-questions table, PR status, scoreboard |
| [`docs/heartpath-refactor/BIBLE.md`](docs/heartpath-refactor/BIBLE.md) | **Governs the WHY/WHAT.** Doctrine, audited counts, phase order, and the **13 stances Claude already got WRONG** and must not re-adopt |
| [`docs/heartpath-refactor/IMPLEMENTATION.md`](docs/heartpath-refactor/IMPLEMENTATION.md) | **Governs the HOW.** PROVEN-vs-ASSUMED table, PR ladder, seams, telemetry, tests, CI tripwires, pitfalls. **Never plan against an ASSUMED row.** |
| [`docs/heartpath-refactor/DECISIONS.md`](docs/heartpath-refactor/DECISIONS.md) | Every decision, what it overturned, the evidence. **Do not re-litigate a settled entry** — 21 of them |

**State (2026-07-12):** plan complete · keystone **proven on hardware, 5/5 device classes** · Codex grounded review **PROCEED-AS-PLANNED** after 6 rounds · **no code written.** **Next action: build A1 (#1521)** — plan written and reviewed.

**Council is deliberately SKIPPED** on this refactor (founder, D-021): Codex has repo AND web access; council has neither. Its absence is a decision, not a gap.

**Binding priority order (founder, 2026-07-12):** (1) dictation works 100% of the time it physically can; (2) simple honest handling for real-world reasons it cannot — no mic, muted, no permission; (3) simple reliable handling for the rare genuine crash — *"you always over-index here."*

**The success test is subtraction.** A phase that only adds is not a phase. If the system does not shrink, we reorganised instead of simplifying, and we failed.

## Objective: Reach, Not Revenue

**North Star:** 100,000 users, notoriety, and market disruption. Free is the core strategy, not a gap.

- **Free Forever (BYOK):** No paywalls. Users can bring their own API keys to use the entire product for free.
- **Monetization:** Deferred until massive scale. The only allowed mechanism is an optional, close-to-at-cost cloud transcription convenience tier that never gatekeeps core software utility.
- **AI Guardrail:** Evaluate plans purely on traction, credibility, and word-of-mouth toward 100k. Never apply a SaaS revenue rubric, optimize for monetization, or treat "free" as a weakness.

Marketing-side detail (audience, pains, WisprFlow comparison, messaging) lives in the EnviousMarketing engine (see § Website & Content).

## Compatibility (know this cold)

**Runs on macOS 14 (Sonoma) or later, Apple Silicon only (M1+). No Intel.**

- **Core dictation** (record, transcribe, paste) works on the entire supported range. It never requires macOS 26.
- **The built-in Apple Intelligence polish option requires macOS 26+.** It calls Apple's FoundationModels framework (`SystemLanguageModel`), which only exists on macOS 26. This is NOT the same thing as "Apple Intelligence the consumer feature," which shipped in macOS 15.1: third-party apps had no way to invoke the on-device model until FoundationModels arrived in 26. Do not "correct" our macOS-26 lines to 15.1.
- **Below macOS 26**, Apple Intelligence polish is unavailable and the text falls back to raw SILENTLY. `frameworkUnavailable` IS in the silent-skip set (the `isSilentPolishSkip` switch in `Sources/EnviousWisprPipeline/TextProcessingRunner.swift`, alongside `.unsupportedInputLanguage` / `.outputLanguageDrift`), so a genuine pre-26 user gets raw text with no "polish failed" pill (fixed; #1080 closed 2026-06). The user can pick Ollama (on-device) or bring an OpenAI / Gemini key. Cloud polish and Ollama work on the full range.
- **Proof:** the code is compile- and run-time gated to macOS 26 (`AppleIntelligenceConnector.swift`); telemetry confirms 100% of genuine Apple Intelligence polish users run macOS 26.x (90d). Engineering detail: `.claude/knowledge/llm-contract.md`.

## How this project works

Saurabh is founder; Claude is CTO and sole implementer. Every update is exec-level, plain English, one question at a time. No class/file names, paths, or engineering acronyms in chat — they belong in tool calls and commits.

## Principles

- **Heart & Limbs.** Critical path (audio, ASR, paste) must never fail. Features (Custom Words, LLM polish, filler removal) are limbs: enhance with timeouts and fallback. If a limb fails, the user gets raw text.
- **Full functionality at maximum speed.** Sub-second pipeline latency target. 60-minute graceful cap (#1060), but designed for dictation, not long-form recording.
- **Production-grade from day one.** No band-aid fixes, no shortcuts that trade today's speed for tomorrow's debt.

## Rules

Four headline triggers — mechanics live in the linked files. Read before acting in the pathway.

1. **Code change workflow** — Plan → Coverage Review → Grounded Review → Build → Codex on every ship-path change. → [workflow-process](.claude/rules/workflow-process.md), [validation-discipline](.claude/rules/validation-discipline.md), [tools-and-apps](.claude/rules/tools-and-apps.md), [grounding-discipline](.claude/rules/grounding-discipline.md)
2. **Git and GitHub operations** → [github](.claude/knowledge/github.md)
3. **Session behavior** — wind-down, pacing, never-ask-if-done, overnight bounds → [session-behavior](.claude/rules/session-behavior.md)
4. **Knowledge and rules authoring** — when drafting, updating, or revising any `.claude/knowledge/` or `.claude/rules/` file, these are grep-retrieval assets, not prose: write the fewest words that still trigger the correct action, keep every `## RULE:`/`## FACT:` anchor and load-bearing path/ID/command verbatim, name one canonical owner and cross-link instead of restating a fact, verify each claim against live code before it lands, and delete any sentence that would not change what a future session does. → [writing-style](/Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/dev/writing-style.md)

- [xcode-build-tooling](.claude/knowledge/xcode-build-tooling.md) — Xcode-engine / Tuist / XcodeBuildMCP toolchain (#913 migration); read before build/packaging work.
- [gotchas-release](.claude/knowledge/gotchas-release.md) — release tag, signing/notarization, Sparkle, workflow edits.
- [gotchas-audio](.claude/knowledge/gotchas-audio.md) — AVAudioEngine, audio capture, VAD, streaming ASR, BT, overlay.
- [llm-contract](.claude/knowledge/llm-contract.md) — `LLMError` / `LLMResult` / `LLMPolishStep` / polish providers / prompt mode.
- [polish-prompt-architecture](.claude/knowledge/polish-prompt-architecture.md) — the six polish prompt paths (Apple Intelligence / OpenAI / Gemini / EG-1 / Ollama-Gemma / Ollama-nonGemma), routing, dynamic mode selector, framing matrix, and the shipped cloud→one-fixed-prompt collapse (#1255) + test protocol (thinking OFF, subset-first, 99.9% target). Read before touching any prompt builder or benchmarking provider polish quality. Codex-validated 2026-07-01, EG-1 path added 2026-07-10.
- [deterministic-features](.claude/knowledge/deterministic-features.md) — complete inventory of non-model text features (numbers/dates/money/emails ITN, vocab correction, emoji, and the full AFM guard/repair stack that catches strip/drop/execute). Read when interpreting any polish/AFM benchmark, asking "does the app already handle X," or touching `EnviousOutputFilter` / `InverseTextNormalizer` / `WordCorrector` / `EmojiRestorer` / `LeadingMarkerRepair`.
- [ollama-operations](.claude/knowledge/ollama-operations.md) — Ollama local polish: shipped catalog, thinking-model gotcha (#272, omit `think` + 2048 budget), connector mechanics, latency tax, how to benchmark a local model faithfully. Read before any Ollama/local-model work or benchmark.
- [eg1-operations](.claude/knowledge/eg1-operations.md) — EG-1 native polish (our own model, #1271): artifact identity + R2 hosting runbook, hot-swap contract (EG-2/EG-3 = manifest-only), llama-server build recipe + traps, runtime/unload matrix, quality+perf baselines, PROVISIONAL threshold dials (#1277 — 15s budget expected to change, founder directive), telemetry names, license posture, review lessons. Read before ANY EG-1 work, model update, or engine rebuild.
- [eg1-model-provenance](.claude/knowledge/eg1-model-provenance.md) — how EG-1 was MADE (not how it runs): base model (Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507), training method (QLoRA merged into base, NOT a full fine-tune, NOT a separate runtime adapter), training config + data recipe, English-only training vs multilingual base, the one-time bias gate, and the EG-2 retrain recipe. Read for any "is it a full fine-tune or a LoRA," base-model, training-data, or per-language-support question (#1265).
- [model-delivery](.claude/knowledge/model-delivery.md) — ANY model download/delivery work (Parakeet/WhisperKit/EG-1 downloads, the models.enviouslabs.co mirror, cache validation/repair, download telemetry, first-run model UX, epic #1348 phases). Pointer to the LIVE Model Delivery Contract (`docs/model-delivery/model-delivery-contract.md`) + the ten invariants + scope guardrails + salvage map.
- [eg1-cdn-sharding](.claude/knowledge/eg1-cdn-sharding.md) — frozen pre-implementation research and rationale for #1417. Sharding SHIPPED as eight `componentSet` GGUF shards (2026-07-08); use [model-delivery](.claude/knowledge/model-delivery.md) and [eg1-operations](.claude/knowledge/eg1-operations.md) for current delivery, artifact, and authoring state. Read this file only for the llama.cpp / manifest / R2 rationale and historical migration decisions.

# Gotchas & Non-Obvious Patterns
## FACT: pathway-loaded-gotchas
| Scope | File |
|---|---|
| Swift compiler patterns (FluidAudio qualification, `@preconcurrency`, CFString literals, `nonisolated(unsafe)`) | `.claude/rules/swift-patterns.md` |
| Audio / streaming / AVAudioEngine / VAD / overlay | [`gotchas-audio.md`](gotchas-audio.md) |
| Release / signing / notarization / CI / Sparkle | [`gotchas-release.md`](gotchas-release.md) |
| LLM polish contract invariants | [`llm-contract.md`](llm-contract.md) |
| WhisperKit-specific traps | [`whisperkit-research.md`](whisperkit-research.md) § Non-Obvious Traps |
| Incident response runbooks | [`incident-response.md`](incident-response.md) |
## RULE: carbon-hotkey-after-nsapp-run
Rule: Register Carbon `RegisterEventHotKey` only from `applicationDidFinishLaunching` or later, never before.
Why: Earlier registration returns `noErr` (success) but events are silently never delivered.
Ref: `AppLifecycleCoordinator.runDidFinishLaunching()` → `DictationRuntime.startHotkeyServiceIfEnabled()` → `HotkeyController.startIfEnabled()`.
## RULE: cgevent-needs-axtrust
Rule: CGEvent-based paste needs Accessibility trust to deliver. The shipped path (`PasteService.dispatchCmdV()`) posts Cmd+V via a `.combinedSessionState` source through `.cgAnnotatedSessionEventTap`; `pasteToActiveApp()` reads `AXIsProcessTrusted()` for telemetry, NOT as a per-call gate — the app-level mandatory-Accessibility onboarding (#735/#736) is what guarantees delivery.
Why: On macOS 14+, posted events silently never deliver without Accessibility, regardless of tap level.
Ref: `PasteService.pasteToActiveApp()` / `PasteService.dispatchCmdV()`.
## RULE: modifier-only-needs-accessibility
Rule: Any modifier-only hotkey default MUST be paired with Accessibility-mandatory onboarding (#735); chord defaults via Carbon do not need this.
Why: Global flags-changed monitors silently fail outside the app without Accessibility; Carbon chord paths do not.
Ref: PR #736.
## RULE: permission-status-prompt-only
Rule: Treat `permission.status=granted` for Accessibility with care; `dictation.completed` is the cleanest proxy for "ended up granted".
Why: TWO emit paths now — `requestAccessibilityAccess()` (`PermissionsService.swift`) on explicit prompt, AND `refreshAccessibilityStatus()` (`PermissionsService.swift`, granted in `refreshAccessibilityStatus()`) with `context:"changed"` when the background poll detects a System-Settings grant. So `permission.status=granted` is no longer purely explicit-prompt-only, and Settings grants are no longer invisible.
Ref: 2026-05-15 #733 funnel analysis.
## RULE: activate-ignoring-other-apps-dead
Rule: Use `PasteService.forceActivateApp(pid:)` (sets `"AXFrontmost"` as CFString via AX API) for Tier 2 paste-back activation; do not use `.activateIgnoringOtherApps`.
Why: macOS 14+ ignores the `activateIgnoringOtherApps` flag for LSUIElement apps, breaking paste-back entirely.
Ref: `PasteService.forceActivateApp(pid:)`.
## RULE: audio-constants-from-architecture
Rule: Use `architecture.md` Audio Constants for the canonical capture format, tap sizing, VAD chunking, and pre-roll policy; call `SilenceDetector.reset()` (resets stream state to `.initial()`) before every new session.
Why: Stale VAD state corrupts the next recording.
Ref: [architecture.md](architecture.md) Audio Constants (canonical values); runtime VAD thresholds + filtering + state machine behavior live in [pipeline-mechanics.md](pipeline-mechanics.md) `vad-config-and-filtering`.
## RULE: keychain-two-backends
Rule: Release builds use Apple Keychain (`com.enviouswispr.app.api-keys` service); DEBUG/dev/unrecognized bundles use `~/.enviouswispr-keys/` (0700 dir, 0600 files); never UserDefaults, never log keys, never delete files in `~/.enviouswispr-keys/` (rotate by overwriting).
Why: Wrong storage leaks keys; deletion breaks app and MCP connectors that share the directory.
Ref: `KeychainManager`.
## RULE: ollama-silent-failure
Rule: Before debugging polish code, check Ollama binary existence, then reachability at `http://localhost:11434`, then model availability.
Why: When `ollama serve` is down, polish silently fails under the strict readiness timeout.
Ref: `OllamaSetupService`.
## RULE: tccutil-bundle-id-scope
Rule: Always scope `tccutil reset` to a bundle ID — `com.enviouswispr.app` for prod, `com.enviouswispr.app.dev` for dev; never blanket-reset Accessibility.
Why: Bare `tccutil reset Accessibility` wipes permissions for ALL apps system-wide, breaking window managers, automation tools, screen readers.
How to apply: If TCC stops persisting, verify cert (`security find-identity -v -p codesigning | grep "EnviousWispr Dev"`) and bundle ID (`plutil -extract CFBundleIdentifier raw "build/EnviousWispr Local.app/Contents/Info.plist"`); on dev+prod conflict, quit both, reset both bundle IDs, reboot, re-grant production first then drag dev `.app` into System Settings.
Ref: [distribution.md](distribution.md) § Dev Certificate.
## RULE: uat-runner-background-only
Rule: Run `python3 Tests/RuntimeUAT/uat_runner.py run` with `run_in_background: true`; retrieve via `TaskOutput`.
Why: Foreground UAT silently fails (listing via `uat_runner.py list` is fine in foreground).
Ref: `Tests/RuntimeUAT/uat_runner.py`.
## RULE: asr-backend-unload-before-swap
Rule: Only one ASR backend is active at a time; always unload the active backend → swap → warm the new backend (`ASRManager.switchBackend(to:)` unloads+swaps, then `EngineCoordinator.startWarm(for:)` / `ensureEngineWarm(reason: .engineSwap)` warms it).
Why: Two active backends corrupt lifecycle ownership.
Ref: `ASRManager.switchBackend(to:)`; `EngineCoordinator.startWarm(for:)`.
## RULE: ui-state-via-named-homes
Rule: Route UI-facing pipeline state via `LiveRecordingState.pipelineState` / `LiveRecordingState.currentTranscript`, post-recording error via `LastRecordingResult.polishError`, and reset via `DictationRuntime.resetActivePipeline()`; never reach into a concrete pipeline's `.state` / `.lastPolishError` / `.reset()`.
Why: Direct pipeline access bypasses WhisperKit when it is the active backend.
Ref: 2026-03-22 audit Q2/Q20.
## RULE: accessibility-monitoring
Rule: `PermissionsService.startMonitoring()` polls Accessibility using `TimingConstants.accessibilityPollIntervalSec` (`Constants.swift`) and calls `refreshAccessibilityStatus()` to catch runtime revocation; `resetAccessibilityWarningDismissal()` re-arms the warning banner. Note: `AppLifecycleCoordinator.runDidBecomeActive()` does NOT refresh on activate — the background poll is the live mechanism.
Why: Users can revoke Accessibility at runtime.
Ref: `PermissionsService.startMonitoring()` (`PermissionsService.swift`).
## RULE: nsscreen-guard-first
Rule: Guard `NSScreen.screens.first` with `guard let` or `??`; never force-index `NSScreen.screens[0]`.
Why: Display sleep/wake, monitor disconnect/reconnect, and window-server transitions return an empty `NSScreen.screens` array — `[0]` crashes with index-out-of-bounds.
Ref: `NSScreen.screens`.
## RULE: no-cp-into-worktree
Rule: Apply deltas to a worktree via `git diff -- <file>` + `git apply`; never `cp` files from the root checkout into a worktree.
Why: Root may contain unstaged changes from prior sessions; copying silently includes them in the worktree branch commit.
Ref: PR #109 CI failure.
## RULE: foundationmodels-build-gate
Rule: The required PR gate `build-check` must depend on a `macos-26` lane (`latest-stable` Xcode) that compile-probes FoundationModels; post-#906 that lane is `build-release` in `pr-check.yml` (standalone probe, `build-check` aggregates both lanes). `main-post-merge.yml` has an SDK-version guard (`macOS SDK 26.0+ required`) + Release build, but NOT a standalone `#if canImport(FoundationModels)` compile probe.
Why: `#if canImport(FoundationModels)` is compile-time — missing SDK silently strips AI code and ships unexplained polish failure; after the #906 split `build-check` is an aggregator, so the probe lives on the release lane, not the gate job itself.
Ref: PR #712 (2026-05-09); #906 (2026-05-29).
## RULE: xcode-test-entrypoint
Rule: Run logic tests through `scripts/xcode-test.sh`, never raw `swift test`. The wrapper drives `xcodebuild test` against the generated Tuist project (`-project EnviousWispr.xcodeproj -scheme EnviousWispr -configuration Debug`, arm64) with a "Test run with N test" executed-count guard; `--release` opts into the full release-config test RUN locally (mirrors `main-post-merge.yml`, Release config, `ENABLE_TESTABILITY=YES`) — useful for reproduction, though the PR `build-check` gate itself now compiles (not runs) both Release test targets on its own (#1539). Still wrap `#if DEBUG`-gated symbols (`TelemetryService.testEventHook`, `CapturedTelemetryEvent`) in `#if DEBUG` in the test file.
Why: Raw `swift test` no longer proves the shipped app/XPC/resource topology; release-lane runs catch `#if DEBUG`-symbol compile errors.
Enforced (nudge, not a block): `.claude/scripts/check-swift-build-gate.sh` (PreToolUse Bash) fires on raw `swift test` or `swift build -c release` and points here; conventions.md RULE: xcode-build-engine-cli owns the enforcement detail.
Ref: #913 PR7; [ci-pipeline.md](ci-pipeline.md). (was: never-raw-swift-test, PR #610.)
## RULE: xcode-test-script-path-must-match-worktree
Rule: Invoke `scripts/xcode-test.sh` using the SAME worktree's own path you're testing (relative `scripts/xcode-test.sh` from inside that worktree, or that worktree's absolute path) — never the root checkout's absolute path from inside a feature worktree.
Why: The script derives `PROJECT_ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")/.." && pwd)"` from its OWN invocation path (`$0`), not `cwd` (`scripts/xcode-test.sh:19`). Invoking root's absolute script path from a worktree silently `cd`s into and tests ROOT's checkout instead — full "TEST SUCCEEDED" output, plausible test count, zero of your new tests actually run, no error of any kind.
How to apply: From a worktree, run `./scripts/xcode-test.sh` (relative) or the worktree's own absolute path; if unsure which ran, `grep -c "<your new test name>" <log>` — a genuine 0 with "TEST SUCCEEDED" means it tested the wrong checkout, not that your test was skipped.
Ref: #1392/#1393 (2026-07-11); distinct from tools-and-apps.md RULE: claude-scripts-absolute-path-from-worktrees (that one covers gitignored `.claude/scripts/` returning a silent no-op; this one covers tracked `scripts/` silently testing the wrong checkout).
## RULE: xcode-rebuild-relaunch
Rule: After any change needing runtime validation, run `/wispr-rebuild-and-relaunch` (→ `scripts/build-dev-app.sh`: quit ALL running dev instances by `EnviousWispr Local.app` executable path → Tuist generate → xcodebuild the `EnviousWispr-Dev` scheme → self-signed → copy to `build/EnviousWispr Local.app` → relaunch → verify by worktree path); `/wispr-run-smoke-test` is compile-only.
Why: Users validate the bundled `build/EnviousWispr Local.app`, not a raw `swift build` binary. Only one dev EW runs at a time (founder 2026-07-01). Dev and prod share the `EnviousWispr` executable name, so cleanup is scoped to `EnviousWispr Local.app` paths — never `pkill -x EnviousWispr`, never a bundle-ID quit (the `.dev` id is shared across worktrees).
Ref: #913 PR4/PR7. (was: stale-binary-rebuild-relaunch, PR #109.)
## RULE: xcode-build-after-commit-for-push-gate
Rule: When a ship-path (`Sources/`/`Tests/`/`Package.swift`/`Project.swift`/`Tuist.swift`) commit must pass the push-freshness gate (`.claude/scripts/check-push-discipline.sh`: newest of `build/EnviousWispr Local.app/Contents/MacOS/EnviousWispr` and `.derivedData/Dev/Build/Products/Dev/EnviousWispr Local.app/Contents/MacOS/EnviousWispr` executable mtime ≥ HEAD commit epoch), run `scripts/build-dev-app.sh` AFTER committing — building first then committing leaves the executable older than the commit and the gate blocks the push.
Why: The gate keys freshness to the Xcode dev-build outputs (PR4), not a SwiftPM `.build` binary. Never `SKIP_PUSH_CHECKS=1` to dodge it (banned guardrail bypass).
How to apply: `git commit --amend` (the standard post-Codex-review fix) bumps the committer epoch past your last build and re-blocks the push. A plain rebuild does NOT fix it when no Swift source changed: the content-aware build system skips the relink so the artifact mtime stays stale, and `touch`ing a source with unchanged content does NOT force a recompile. Force a genuine relink by `rm -rf` the built `.app` (BOTH the DerivedData product `.derivedData/Dev/Build/Products/Dev/EnviousWispr Local.app` AND the `build/` copy), then re-run `scripts/build-dev-app.sh` (object files stay cached → fast relink, not a full recompile). When checking freshness by hand, stat the bundle EXECUTABLE (`Contents/MacOS/EnviousWispr`), not the `.app` dir — the dir mtime stays stale across incremental builds and will fool you.
Ref: 2026-05-30 PR #908; #913 PR4/PR7; 2026-06-07 PR #1016 (amend/relink trap re-confirmed). (was: build-after-commit-for-push-gate; spm-never-rm-build-dir deleted — `.build` is no longer the canonical build dir.)
===== /Users/m4pro_sv/Developer/EnviousLabs/EnviousWispr/.claude/knowledge/conventions.md =====
<!-- tier: 1 -->
# Engineering Conventions
Production-breaking rules live in [gotchas.md](gotchas.md); workflow policy lives in [`.claude/rules/workflow-process.md`](../rules/workflow-process.md).
## RULE: xcode-build-engine-cli
Rule: Build EnviousWispr with Xcode's build engine driven from the CLI via the committed Tuist manifests (`Project.swift` / `Tuist.swift`). Do not commit the generated `.xcodeproj` / `.xcworkspace`, and do not treat raw `swift build` / `swift test` as the build or test path for the app, XPC services, release archive, or repo test gate.
Why: The SwiftPM-only path could not place Core ML / SwiftPM resources where the generated `Bundle.module` accessor resolves them in a signed `.app` (#913). Xcode owns app + 2 XPC + test + resource assembly; Tuist keeps it reproducible and CLI-only with no committed project files. (`swift build` of the package products still compiles as an informal quick sanity check, but it is not the canonical build and never produces the shippable bundle.)
How to apply: Dev bundle → `scripts/build-dev-app.sh` (Tuist generate → xcodebuild `EnviousWispr-Dev`/`Dev` config → `build/EnviousWispr Local.app`); logic tests → `scripts/xcode-test.sh`; release DMG → `scripts/build-release-dmg.sh <version>`. Kill old dev processes by `EnviousWispr Local.app` executable path (ALL dev instances — one dev EW at a time, founder 2026-07-01), never `pkill -x EnviousWispr` (dev and prod share the `EnviousWispr` executable name). Enforced (nudge, not a block): `.claude/scripts/check-swift-build-gate.sh` (PreToolUse Bash) fires on raw `swift test` or `swift build -c release`/`--configuration release` and points at the real script; plain `swift build` (debug, with or without `--target`) stays silent as the allowed sanity check. The real wall is downstream: `check-push-discipline.sh` keys push freshness to the Xcode-engine artifact's mtime, so a raw swift build/test can never silently satisfy Definition of Done even if the nudge is ignored.
Ref: [xcode-build-tooling.md](xcode-build-tooling.md); [gotchas.md](gotchas.md) RULE: xcode-test-entrypoint; 2026-07-11 (this session caught itself using `swift build -c release` as the release gate, no hook existed).
## FACT: commit-style
Conventional commits: `type(scope): message`, squash-merged. Types: `feat` / `fix` / `refactor` / `docs` / `chore` / `test` / `perf`. Scopes: `asr` / `audio` / `ui` / `llm` / `pipeline` / `settings` / `hotkey` / `vad` / `build` / `user` / `release`. Example: `feat(hotkey): chord default for fresh installs`.
## RULE: di-narrow-homes
Rule: Inject the narrow `@Environment` home a view needs (e.g. `LiveRecordingState`); never add a broad cross-domain state object and read it as `<object>.<domain>...`.
Why: That god-object pattern is what made AppState the deletion target of epic #763.
How to apply: Settings changes route through `PipelineSettingsSync.handleSettingChanged()`; AppState is freeze-test blocked from reintroduction; full decision tree in [state-ownership.md](state-ownership.md).
Ref: epic #763.
## RULE: settings-persistence
Rule: Persist non-sensitive settings via `UserDefaults.standard` with `didSet`; persist API keys via `KeychainManager` (Apple Keychain for release `com.enviouswispr.app`, file storage at `~/.enviouswispr-keys/` for DEBUG/dev/unrecognized bundles, failing closed).
Why: Failing closed means UAT and beta builds never touch the production Keychain.
Ref: [gotchas.md](gotchas.md) RULE: keychain-two-backends.
## FACT: view-patterns
- Settings tab body: `SettingsContentView { ... }` (branded ScrollView wrapper in `SettingsComponents.swift`; replaced `Form { }.formStyle(.grouped)`).
- Main window: one unified window — `UnifiedWindowView` (`SettingsView.swift`), a `NavigationStack` + custom `HStack` whose sidebar is a `ScrollView` of button rows (NOT a `NavigationSplitView`/`List` — that was replaced for macOS-26 sizing; the sidebar renders as `AXButton`s, uat-testing.md #1296) driven by the `SettingsSection` enum (History + every settings tab); onboarding is a separate `Window(id: "onboarding")`.
- Async on-appear work: `.task { }`.
## FACT: release-versioning
Semver tags with `v` prefix (`v1.2.3`) trigger release CI; local builds carry `-local` suffix (`0.0.0-local`). Full release flow: [github.md](github.md) RULE: release-workflow and [distribution.md](distribution.md). Changelog: `/wispr-generate-changelog`.
## RULE: brand-visual-system
Rule: Use the brand system at `.claude/skills/brand-guide/SKILL.md` for all visual artifacts (HTML mockups, diagrams, landing pages) — tokens, fonts, gradients.
Why: It is the SoT; ad-hoc choices drift the brand.
How to apply: macOS settings use system-ui fonts plus brand accent purple (`#7c3aed`) instead of the default blue.
Ref: `.claude/skills/brand-guide/SKILL.md`.
## RULE: feature-request-docs
Rule: Track feature specs as GitHub Issues; save plans at `docs/feature-requests/issue-<N>-<YYYY-MM-DD>-<slug>.md` (no-issue plans: `plan-<YYYY-MM-DD>-<slug>.md`); all plans outside the zero-blast-radius exception use `TEMPLATE.md` with §4-9 mandatory regardless of tier.
Why: Bias toward SMALL tier is a documented failure mode; legacy files stay to avoid history churn.
How to apply: Commit scope matches the primary code area (e.g. `feat(hotkey):`). New naming applies from 2026-04-14 forward.
Ref: [workflow-process.md](../rules/workflow-process.md) RULE: ten-step-shape (step 3).
## RULE: logging-convention
Rule: Use `AppLogger.shared` actor (always `await` at call site) via `log(_:level:category:)`; never log secrets.
Why: Single actor avoids duplicate sink setup; secrets in logs leak in CI artifacts.
How to apply: Levels `.verbose` / `.debug` / `.info`; categories `"Pipeline"` / `"LLM"` / `"Audio"` / `"PipelineTiming"`; the ENTIRE log pipeline (OSLog + file sink) is `#if DEBUG`-only — release builds are dead code, no output; in DEBUG, OSLog receives every call and the file sink at `~/Library/Logs/EnviousWispr/app.log` (5×10MB rotation) is active only when debug mode is enabled; `CORRECTION_DEBUG` category traces RAW ASR → WordCorrector → Filler Removal → LLM Polish per utterance.
Ref: PR #85.
## RULE: xpc-error-sanitize
Rule: Never call `safeReply(error as NSError)` in `ASRServiceHandler` (the sole remaining XPC service handler after #1543 collapsed audio capture in-process); always pass error paths through `XPCErrorSanitizer.sanitizeForXPC(error)` with the `// XPC error sanitization boundary.` anchor comment.
Why: Unsanitized NSError userInfo can SIGABRT the helper during XPC encode.
How to apply: `scripts/check-xpc-error-hygiene.sh` enforces this in CI.
Ref: issue #297.
## FACT: runtime-cross-refs
Runtime behavior, types, data flow: [pipeline-mechanics.md](pipeline-mechanics.md) (text processing, streaming, audio capture, paste cascade) and [architecture.md](architecture.md) (state machines, modules, window architecture).
## RULE: no-dotclaude-from-shipped-code
Rule: Never reference `.claude/` from shipped files — `Sources/**`, `Tests/**`, `Package.swift`, `.github/workflows/**` (comments / docs / runtime strings), `website/src/**`, `website/public/**`, `workers/**` (prompts shipped to the cloud); inline the salient sentence, move load-bearing content to `docs/` and link there, or delete the cross-ref if it is just attribution.
Why: Ship-path `.claude/` links break on GitHub, fresh clones, and cloud worker prompts.
How to apply: Allowed exceptions are runtime calls to `~/.claude/bin/get-key`, CI path-allowlist regex patterns, and content explicitly about the `.claude/` folder (e.g. blog posts).
Ref: PR #717 (2026-05-10).
