<!-- tier: 2 -->
# EG-1 Operations

When to load: any EG-1 work — model hosting/updates (EG-2/EG-3), llama-server engine rebuilds, download/lifecycle bugs, polish-latency questions, threshold tuning, license questions. Shipped #1271 Phase 1 (PR #1278, 2026-07-02). Phase 2 (onboarding) still open on #1271.

This file = how EG-1 RUNS and SHIPS. For how EG-1 was MADE (base model, QLoRA-vs-full-FT, training config/data, per-language coverage, EG-2 retrain recipe) see [`eg1-model-provenance.md`](eg1-model-provenance.md).

## FACT: eg1-artifact-identity
As of #1417 (2026-07-08, PR #1425) EG-1 ships as 8 sharded GGUF files (`admission.layout: "componentSet"`), not one monolithic file — see `eg1-cdn-sharding.md` for the sharding rationale/mechanism. The pre-#1417 single-file identity (`eg-1-v1.gguf`, sha256 `3343fc1a...`) is historical; still valid for anyone reading old telemetry/support threads referencing it.
| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Model | EG-1 = Qwen3-4B polish tune v2, Q5_K_M GGUF |
| Revision | `v2-sharded` (delivery manifest `identity.revision`; also `eg1-manifest.json`'s `version`, kept in agreement per the cross-manifest identity test) |
| Files | 8 shards, `eg-1-v1-00001-of-00008.gguf` … `eg-1-v1-00008-of-00008.gguf`, largest 399,884,576 bytes (well under Cloudflare's 512MB cache-eligible ceiling and the 450MB Phase-3 warn line) |
| Total size | 2,889,512,544 bytes (sum of real shard files — slightly larger than the old monolithic 2,889,511,680 due to per-shard GGUF header overhead; shards are NOT byte-range slices) |
| Entrypoint | `eg-1-v1-00001-of-00008.gguf` (`admission.entrypointFile`; llama.cpp auto-discovers the other 7 siblings once pointed at shard 1) |
| Manifest digest | `07550d07e242aa660393f5e62d36106ed7916ffa8852d9fb66f5420854a6b70d` |
| contextTokens | 16384 (server `-c`; polish preflight reads it from the endpoint) |
| promptTemplateID | `eg1-v1` (→ `PromptFamily.egOneFixed`; unknown ID = RED app_update_required) |
| minAppVersion | 2.3.0 |
| URL prefix | https://models.enviouslabs.co/eg1/v2-sharded/ (shard 1: `.../eg1/v2-sharded/eg-1-v1-00001-of-00008.gguf`) |
| Legacy URL | https://models.enviouslabs.co/eg1/eg-1-v1-q5km.gguf (untouched, still serves the old monolithic file for any app version predating #1417) |
| License URL | https://models.enviouslabs.co/eg1/EG-1-MODEL-LICENSE.txt |
| Manifest | `Sources/EnviousWispr/Resources/eg1-delivery-manifest.json` (fetch/verify/admit authority) + `Sources/EnviousWispr/Resources/eg1-manifest.json` (runtime config: contextTokens/promptTemplateID/minAppVersion/licenseURL; `sha256`/`sizeBytes` removed as dead fields per #1417 §3.6, superseded by the delivery manifest's per-shard verification) |
| Local install | `~/Library/Application Support/EnviousWispr/PolishModels/` (8 flat shard files, sibling directory to `llama-server`'s `-m` target) |
| Cloudflare | Cache Rule + Transform Rule scoped to `starts_with(path, "/eg1/v2-sharded/")` on zone `370565c81fa22c65cd2322e9c94991f0` — structurally cannot match the legacy path; existing Parakeet rule (`/parakeet/`) untouched, same ruleset |

## RULE: eg1-hot-swap-contract
Rule: Shipping EG-1 v2 / EG-2 = upload the new artifact set + license to R2; update `eg1-delivery-manifest.json` with the new revision, per-shard file paths + measured `sizeBytes`/`sha256`, `totalBytes`, and `admission.layout`/`entrypointFile` (this manifest owns hashes/sizes/layout); then update `eg1-manifest.json`'s runtime config to match (`version`, `contextTokens`/`promptTemplateID`/`minAppVersion`/`licenseURL`) — `eg1-manifest.json` no longer carries `sha256`/`sizeBytes` (dead fields removed #1417, see FACT: eg1-artifact-identity). Keep `modelName`/`promptTemplateID` unless the PROMPT changed; bump `minAppVersion` if runtime behavior requires it. Zero code changes unless the prompt template changes (then: new `promptTemplateID` + new `PromptFamily` case + builder; unknown template IDs read RED "app_update_required" in old apps by design).
Why: `EGOneRuntime` refuses manifests whose `modelName != "eg-1"` (fixed Core literal `LLMProvider.egOneModelName`); `refreshInstalledState` purges the previous version's artifact family automatically (extension-scoped).
How to apply: R2 upload runbook below; validate the new host object with HEAD (etag+length), a Range request (expect 206), and a full curl+shasum before updating the manifest.
Ref: #1271 plan §hot-swap; purge added Codex r11.

## FACT: kill-switch-stops-mutation-not-format-drift (#1386 PR-1, 2026-07-11)
`modelDelivery.egOne.enabled=false` guarantees exactly ONE thing: no EG-1 model bytes are moved, deleted, or fetched. It does NOT guarantee polish keeps working, and for one cohort it structurally cannot.

A user still on the previously-shipped MONOLITH (`eg-1-v1.gguf`, every release ≤ v2.3.2) holds bytes a sharded build cannot load at all: `EGOneRuntime` boots `manifest.resolvedEntrypointPath` (shard 1), so no directory choice makes a monolith loadable. Turning the switch off therefore leaves that user with raw text (EG-1 is a limb) and their model completely untouched; turning it back on runs the migration and polish returns. Nothing is lost, only deferred.

**Do NOT "fix" this by restoring the monolithic load path.** That means shipping the old manifest AND the old load path alongside the new ones, permanently — dual-format support carried forever for a rollback lever. Rejected on scope (founder 2026-07-11: don't over-engineer). Codex PR-1 review r14 raised it as DIAGNOSIS-ONLY; the resolution is this documented limitation, not code.

Corollary for any FUTURE model-format change (EG-2, a new quantization): the kill switch is a byte-mutation stop, not a version rollback. If a format change ever needs a true rollback, that is a deliberate design (ship both manifests), not something the flag gives you for free.

## FACT: eg1-monolith-retirement-and-owned-home (#1386 PR-1, PR #1500, 2026-07-11)
Shipped delivery shape since PR #1500: EG-1 installs to `~/Library/Application Support/EnviousWispr/Models/eg-1` (owned home; old `PolishModels` is the RETIRED store). At launch `EGOneLegacyUpgradeCoordinator` retires the one shipped monolith (`eg-1-v1.gguf`, 2_889_511_680 bytes, sha256 `3343fc1a...74f6`) by exact size+digest proof: containment gate (resolved-to-resolved, firmlink-safe) → durable `eg1-v1-replacement-owed` marker in `ModelDelivery/` → delete → normal ModelDeliveryController download. Marker clears ONLY on admission or explicit Cancel/Remove (decline counts even kill-switched — contract §5c.10). Post-replacement the RUNTIME decides activation via `activateAfterAutomaticReplacementIfNeeded()` (live `isActiveProvider`; the install-state stream never activates). Telemetry: `eg1.legacy_detected` (+`selected_provider`) / `legacy_retired` / `legacy_retirement_failed(reason: marker_write|delete|unreadable|containment)` / `replacement_completed` / `replacement_declined`; release ship-criterion: every detected distinct_id eventually shows completed/declined/diagnosable-failed. UAT fixture: genuine monolith kept at `~/Developer/EnviousLabs/eg1-uat-fixture/eg-1-v1.gguf` (digest-verified; monolith still hosted at models.enviouslabs.co/eg1/eg-1-v1-q5km.gguf). Contract authority: `docs/model-delivery/model-delivery-contract.md` v1.3 §5c (delete-first ONLY for cryptographically-identified app-owned assets the runtime cannot load — does NOT authorize delete-first for working files, e.g. WhisperKit). Plan+RCA: `docs/feature-requests/issue-1386-2026-07-11-eg1-retire-monolith-rev2.md`, `docs/postmortems/2026-07-11-1386-pr1-defect-cascade.md`.

## FACT: r2-hosting-runbook
Bucket `enviouslabs-models` (DEDICATED — but account-level R2 is shared across projects: [infra/cloudflare.md](file:///Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/infra/cloudflare.md) RULE r2-shared-across-projects still applies). Custom domain `models.enviouslabs.co` (zone `370565c81fa22c65cd2322e9c94991f0`, minTLS 1.2, bound 2026-07-02, ~10 min provisioning; interim `error code: 1014` is normal). Cost ~$0.05/mo; egress free.
Upload = mint-use-revoke bucket-scoped token (permission groups `2efd5506f9c8494dacb1fa10a3e7d5b6` write + `6a018a9f2fc74eb6b293b0c548f38b39` read, resource `com.cloudflare.edge.r2.bucket.${ACCT}_default_enviouslabs-models`); S3 creds: access_key=token_id, secret=sha256(token_value); token needs ~10 s propagation; REVOKE after. NEVER print token values.
HF mirror: OFF by founder decision (competitor-lift fear); do not publish without explicit approval.

## FACT: eg1-shard-authoring-runbook (#1417, 2026-07-08)
Splitting EG-1 into CDN-cache-eligible shards (each safely under Cloudflare's 512MB ceiling, target ≤450MB per the Phase-3 warn line) reuses `llama.cpp`'s native `gguf-split` tool and the shared delivery engine's existing `componentSet` layout (already proven by Parakeet's 23-file case) — no new reassembly code, no changes to `ManifestFetchTask`/`CacheAdmission`. One-time steps when cutting a new sharded EG-1 revision:
1. **Split:** run `gguf-split` against the real (unsplit) model weights, choosing `--split-max-tensors` so every output shard lands comfortably under 450MB (shards are NOT byte-range slices — each is an independently-headered valid GGUF file with its own metadata, so per-shard size varies with tensor boundaries, never assume equal division).
2. **Measure, never assume:** compute each shard's real `sizeBytes`/`sha256` from the actual output files — the manifest's `totalBytes` must equal the measured sum, and every `sizeBytes` must be re-verified ≤450MB after the real split (the pre-split total does not divide evenly by shard count).
3. **Upload:** each shard to R2 (`r2-hosting-runbook` above) under a NEW versioned sub-path (e.g. `/eg1/v2-sharded/`) — never reuse the legacy single-file path, so the new Cloudflare Cache Rule (scoped to the new sub-path only) can never accidentally match the old monolithic file, which is over the cache-eligible size ceiling.
4. **Author the manifest:** `admission.layout: "componentSet"`, `admission.entrypointFile` naming the shard `DeliveryManifest.resolvedEntrypointPath`/`EGOneDeliveryAdapter.installedArtifactURL` should boot from (shard 1, by `llama.cpp` convention — it auto-discovers siblings by filename pattern once pointed at shard 1), one `files[]` entry per shard with real measured hash/size.
5. **Validate before shipping:** every `sizeBytes` ≤450,000,000; `totalBytes` == sum of `files[].sizeBytes`; `entrypointFile` resolves to exactly one `resolvedInstallPath` (enforced by `DeliveryManifest.validateStructure()`, throws otherwise); no duplicate `installPath` values (already enforced); every shard file on disk matches its declared `sha256` before upload.
6. **Cloudflare Cache Rule + Transform Rule:** scope to `starts_with(http.request.uri.path, "/eg1/v2-sharded/")` (or whatever the new sub-path is) — same shape as Parakeet's rule (edge TTL 1y, `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable, no-transform`). This is a production infra mutation, executed with explicit founder go, separate from the code/manifest change.
Existing EG-1 installs on the OLD single-file manifest are swept as stale download sidecars by `EGOneDeliveryAdapter.migrateLegacyStoreArtifacts()`'s version-agnostic cleanup and re-download once under the new manifest — the old file stays usable throughout (deleted only after the new N-shard set fully verifies and promotes, via `CacheAdmission`'s existing promote-time orphan cleanup). Full plan: `docs/feature-requests/issue-1417-2026-07-08-eg1-cdn-sharding.md`.

## FACT: eg1-engine-build
Committed binary `Sources/EnviousWispr/Resources/llama-server` (15 MB static arm64, Metal embedded, system frameworks only). Full pinned recipe + traps in `Sources/EnviousWispr/Resources/llama-server-PROVENANCE.md` (commit `fdb1db87`, ggml-org/llama.cpp). The three traps that WILL recur on rebuild: default build produces minos 26.0 (MUST set `-DCMAKE_OSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=14.0` and verify via `otool -l`); rogue `~/.local/bin/cc` shim breaks cmake detection (use `-DCMAKE_C_COMPILER=/usr/bin/clang`); default links Homebrew OpenSSL (`-DLLAMA_OPENSSL=OFF`). Signing: `build-release-dmg.sh` step [5.5/6] signs the bare Mach-O before the app seal; `build-dev-app.sh` step 7 asserts presence. MIT notice ships via `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` (`scripts/ci/gen-third-party-notices.sh` — `repo:` path scheme, license text vendored at `Resources/llama-server-LICENSE.txt`).

## FACT: eg1-runtime-config
Launch flags (in `EGOneRuntime.startServerIfInstalled`): `-c 16384 -fa on --cache-type-k q8_0 --cache-type-v q8_0`, app-chosen bind-probed port, 127.0.0.1 only, per-launch `--api-key` bearer token. Measured (M4 Pro, 2026-07-02): RSS 4.1 GB (naive 32768/fp16 config was 7.4 GB — the MacBook-friendly footprint is a FLAG choice, not a model choice; matches `EGOneRuntime.swift` + `llama-server-PROVENANCE.md`), cold start ~8-9 s, warm restart ~1 s, warm inference ~0.2 s.

## FACT: eg1-unload-matrix
All exits verified live 2026-07-02 (kill drills + AX switch drill):
| Event | Behavior |
|---|---|
| Provider switch away | server killed, RAM freed (defers while a recording froze `.egOne`; terminal-state retry) |
| App quit | synchronous kill via `OSAllocatedUnfairLock` mirror (`applicationWillTerminate`) |
| App crash orphan | pkill sweep (anchored regex, exact binary path) — in-spawn + actor-gated `reapOrphansIfIdle` on every no-spawn launch path |
| Crash once | restart-once (~1 s warm) |
| Crash twice | stays down; recovery = settings-open probe or provider re-pick or relaunch |
| Critical memory pressure | killed even while starting |
| Dictation while dead | silent raw paste (~0.5 s; `local_polish_not_ready`) — verified live |

## FACT: eg1-quality-perf-baselines
Quality: 92.4% behavior / 0 critical on hard-340 at exact ship config (native; same artifact via Ollama scored 89.7%); 94.7% on full 1,890 (the number quoted in settings UI copy). Judge: sonnet-5 `behavior_judge.py --system new`. **ACCEPTED NUMBER SPLIT (founder decision 2026-07-05, do NOT re-raise):** in-app settings/release copy keeps **94.7%** (original internal full-1,890 run); the public README + r/macapps post use **93.7%** from the later reproducible `scripts/eval/runs/type-b-1199/eg1_native_shipped_1890/scores/summary.json` (`pass_rate_pct`, same `--system new` judge). This ~1pp split is a deliberate accepted state, not an open reconcile item — do not silently change either number (session-log 2026-07-05). Perf (live, M4 Pro): typical dictations 0.3-0.9 s polish, e2e 0.8-1.3 s Parakeet / 1.9-2.2 s WhisperKit; 1,817 chars in 8.0 s (~230 chars/s throughput). Re-benchmark recipe: `scripts/eval/subset_polish_runner.py --endpoint` against a locally-launched server with the SHIPPING flags (never via Ollama — different runtime, different numbers; see [ollama-operations.md](ollama-operations.md)).
**Shipped-config topic_shift regression (2026-07-05, NOT root-caused):** on the Type B 1,890 exam, `topic_shift` (paragraph breaks) scored 97% via the tuning harness but 78% through the shipped engine (−19pp), driving nearly the whole 94.4→93.7 gap (e.g. TS-026/TS-028 emit zero `\n\n` where the tuning outputs broke between topics). Same Q5_K_M model both runs, so the suspect is the SHIPPING RUNTIME config — most likely the q8_0 KV-cache quantization (the 4.1 GB-footprint choice, FACT: eg1-runtime-config) degrading long-range structuring. Clean test = re-run with fp16 KV cache (`--cache-type-k f16 --cache-type-v f16`) and see if topic_shift recovers. Possible customer-facing product issue (worse paragraph breaks than the tuned model can deliver); founder to decide whether to chase. Benchmark methodology owner: [polish-eval.md](polish-eval.md).

## FACT: eg1-list-formatting-trigger
EG-1 builds a real **bullet list** from a lead-in phrase followed by a natural run of items with **no spoken ordinals** ("the team needs to confirm backups, feature flags, and the traffic drain" → lead-in `:` then one `- item` per line). Spoken ordinals ("first do X, second do Y, third Z") stay INLINE as sentences — the model reads them as sequential steps, not a list. Verified: `scripts/eval/corpus/list_format_v1.jsonl` + the shipped `type-b-1199/eg1_native_shipped_1890` run passed 74/100 list cases with real bullet output on exactly the lead-in-plus-run pattern; a founder demo dictation using "first/second/third" produced inline prose twice (2026-07-05). Implication for demos/marketing: to SHOW EG-1 building a list, script a lead-in + comma-run, never ordinals. Paragraph structuring + self-correction + filler removal work regardless of phrasing. A true marker list is an EG-1 / cloud behavior, NOT an Apple Intelligence one (AFM list detection is run-to-run inconsistent, confirmed live).

## RULE: eg1-thresholds-are-provisional
Rule: EVERY EG-1 numeric threshold is a PROVISIONAL dial pending measured calibration (#1277) — treat none of them as design constraints; each is one constant at one site, changeable with data + eval gate. FOUNDER DIRECTIVE (2026-07-02): the 15 s polish budget WILL likely change — it is the top calibration item and must never be written about or planned around as a hard rule.
| Dial | Current | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Polish time budget | 15 s (`LLMPolishStep` maxDuration) | PROVISIONAL — the de-facto length limit (~3,000 chars at measured 230 chars/s), binds before the token cap; founder expects to raise |
| Context preflight | worst-case both sides (~8k chars) | PROVISIONAL — deliberately conservative; llama-server usage fields give true per-script ratios |
| Probe "slow" | 5 s | guess; measure warm-probe distribution |
| Disk preflight | 2.2× (partial bytes count as reclaimable) | guess; fine unless telemetry shows disk failures |
| Crash-retry delay | 750 ms single retry | cannot beat ~9 s cold reload (accepted r17); a second retry ~1.5 s would rescue warm-restart (~1 s) crashes — decide from telemetry |
How to apply: failure direction is always "raw text pasted" (silent), never truncation/error — `finish_reason == length` skips whole. Change a dial → update this table + #1277 in the same session.
Ref: #1277 (measured baselines recorded there).

## FACT: eg1-telemetry
EG-1 downloads ride the shared `model_delivery.*` funnel with `family=eg1` (`TelemetryService.swift`) — the legacy `eg1.download_started/completed/failed` events were RETIRED (#1348 Phase 3, `EGOneTelemetryBridge.swift`). Runtime health still emits `eg1.health_changed(from,to,reason)` via `EGOneTelemetryBridge` (content-free by construction). Polish skips ride the `llm.polish_skipped` PostHog event (Swift `TelemetryService.polishSkipped`) with reasons `local_polish_{not_ready,download_pending,crashed,input_too_long,output_truncated}` + runner `local_polish_timeout`. These are the calibration inputs for #1277.

## FACT: eg1-legal-posture
EG-1 Community Model License v1.0 (`docs/launch-assets/EG-1-MODEL-LICENSE.txt`, hosted next to the artifact): free in-app/personal/research/benchmarking; NO redistribution, NO embedding in competing commercial products, NO training/distilling commercial models from outputs. Base Qwen Apache-2.0 permits this. Motivation: competitor founder publicly fishing for our prompts (Reddit). IP-attorney review flagged non-blocking, still open. Weights+prompt are inherently extractable from a local install — the license is the legal deterrent, training data + iteration engine remain the real moat. llama.cpp attribution is a SEPARATE obligation (MIT notice, see engine section).

## FACT: eg1-download-drain-gate (#1287/#1279, PR #1322, 2026-07-04)
Cancel→Try Again is single-flight through a drain gate in `EGOneModelStore`: cancel/remove stash the live task as `drainingTask` (guarded — nil never clobbers a pending drain), an accepted retry publishes `.downloading(existingFraction)` synchronously at acceptance (instant Cancel; no dead Try Again while draining), awaits the predecessor's `Task.value` before running (task completion IS the drain signal: delegate continuation resumes only on `didCompleteWithError`, then the handle closes), and `runDownload` refuses stale generations before any disk touch. NO timer escape by design (`timeout-numbers-need-distribution-evidence`); a wedged drain presents as a stalled download with live Cancel, diagnosable via the debug drain-wait log lines ("EG-1 retry waiting for cancelled download to drain" / "drain wait finished in ..."). Tests are negative-control validated (protection removed → test fails); lesson: an absence-of-effect test window must outlast the un-gated mutation latency — pre-warm `AppLogger` in the test or first-time logger init masks the violation. Live drill precedent (real host, same-second cancel/retry at 15%/40%, resume + verified install): session-log 2026-07-04.

## FACT: eg1-review-lessons
For the next new-subsystem feature (this cost 17 local review rounds + founder stop-call):
1. Async/long-running machinery (downloads, child processes) gets the FULL situation-grid enumeration at PLAN time (async-edge-case-enumeration rule applied hard, not shallowly) — the dictation path planned this way stayed clean through every round.
2. Budget the plain review loop: 3-4 rounds, then SWITCH MODES to one-pass exhaustive enumeration (matrix / callback-staleness populations). The matrix+enumeration passes here each replaced ~4 discovery rounds.
3. Threshold findings are calibration items, not review arguments — route to measurement immediately (founder directive; see thresholds rule above).
4. The recurring defect family was "async completion acts on a stale generation" — every callback/queued-Task/post-await resume needs a named staleness guard (generation token, state re-check, single-flight flag, actor-idle gate). Enumerate the population once; audits at `docs/audits/2026-07-02-1271-{eg1-lifecycle-matrix,callback-enumeration}.txt`.
Ref: session-log 2026-07-02; #1271.
===== .claude/knowledge/eg1-model-provenance.md =====
<!-- tier: 2 -->
# EG-1 Model Provenance (how the model was MADE)

When to load: any question about how EG-1 was trained — "is it a full fine-tune or a LoRA," base model, training method/config, training data, per-language coverage, "did we build our own model from scratch," or how to build EG-2/EG-3 (retrain recipe). This is the "how it was made" record; [`eg1-operations.md`](eg1-operations.md) is the "how it runs / how it ships" record (hosting, runtime flags, unload matrix, licensing). Do not duplicate — cross-link.

Source of record: GitHub issue **#1265** (closed, founder-approved 2026-07-02) — its "Method of record" + results comments are authoritative; research + citations in `docs/competitive-analysis/2026-07-02-tune-base-model-vs-fluid1.md`. Training data lives (gitignored, Docs/dev-tooling lane) in `scripts/eval/runs/bakeoff-1265/train_sft_v{1,2}.jsonl`. Epic: **#1190**. Wiring into the app: **#1269 / #1271**.

July 15-16 multilingual/list research resume point: `docs/experiments/eg1-multilingual/CLAUDE-CODE-SALVAGE-HANDOFF-2026-07-16.md` and GitHub issue **#1570**. They classify what is proven, reusable, rejected, and missing after the paused 24-hour run. Read them before resuming PR #1562 or the older running tracker #1364.

## FACT: eg1-headline-what-it-is
| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| Full fine-tune or LoRA? | **QLoRA** (LoRA on the 4-bit-quantized base) — NOT a full-parameter retrain. |
| Adapter shipped separately? | **No.** The LoRA adapter is **merged back into the base weights**, then converted and quantized; there is no runtime "base + adapter." Physical packaging + the current shard identity are owned by [`eg1-operations.md`](eg1-operations.md) FACT: eg1-artifact-identity (EG-1 ships as 8 componentSet GGUF shards, #1417 — not one file). |
| Base model | **Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507** (Alibaba, Apache-2.0, the non-thinking variant). |
| Built from scratch? | No. It is a fine-tuned **derivative** of Qwen3-4B — this is why the README says "fine-tuned derivative of Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507 (Apache-2.0)" and the EG-1 license applies to the *fine-tuned weights*, not the base. |
| Plain-English framing for Saurabh | "We took Alibaba's Qwen 4B, taught it our dictation-cleanup behavior with a QLoRA fine-tune, and baked the result into one custom model. It's ours, but it stands on an open base." |

The one-line honest positioning: **"a QLoRA fine-tune of Qwen3-4B, merged and quantized."** Do NOT claim "full fine-tune" (false — it's LoRA) and do NOT claim "trained our own model from scratch" (false — Qwen3-4B is the base).

## FACT: eg1-training-config
Settled by council + Codex (#1265), trained on the RTX 4090:
| Knob | Value |
|---|---|
| Method | QLoRA via **Unsloth** (CUDA-only; does not run on Apple Silicon) |
| LoRA rank / alpha | **r16 / a32** |
| Learning rate | **5e-5** (dropped from the research doc's 2e-4 starting point) |
| Epochs | **2** |
| Loss | **response-only** (loss on the cleaned output, not the transcript/prompt) |
| Target modules | all attention + MLP projections |
| Precision | bf16, adamw_8bit, gradient checkpointing |
| Rig | **AlienSV RTX 4090** (24 GB, WSL2 Ubuntu), always-on via Tailscale. **$0 spend** — owned hardware, no cloud GPU. |
| Division of labor | TRAIN on the PC, EVAL on the Mac (llama.cpp/Ollama + judge harness), ship the merged GGUF back over Tailscale. |

Rig details: `~/.claude/knowledge/infra/aliensv-pc.md`.

## FACT: eg1-build-pipeline
The end-to-end pipeline (mirror of Fluid-1's exact approach — competitor-proven):
```
QLoRA train (4090)  →  merge adapter into base  →  GGUF convert  →  Q5_K_M quantize  →  judge the QUANTIZED artifact
```
- **Judge the quantized artifact, never the fp16 checkpoint.** The shipped quality number must be measured on exactly the GGUF users run. (Q5_K_M was chosen over Q4_K_M: Q4 scored 86.5% vs Q5 88.8% on the hard-340 at bake-off; the feared Qwen-Q4 collapse did not materialize, but Q5's ~2pp edge + fewer criticals won.)
- Shipped quant: **Q5_K_M, ~2.7–2.9 GB total**. Current sharded artifact identity + exact per-shard SHAs: [`eg1-operations.md`](eg1-operations.md) FACT: eg1-artifact-identity.

## FACT: eg1-training-data
Two data generations; **v2 is what shipped** (`train_sft_v2.jsonl`, **5,656 pairs**). Format is flat `{id, source, input(raw) → output(cleaned)}` JSONL.
- **v1 (3,036 pairs):** SFT built from 1,549 Type-B-approved rows after excluding the hard-340 IDs/texts, plus founder dictations. The exclusion protected the hard-340 exact cases, not the full 1,890 working set. The 900-case overflow set stayed exact-text sealed but was derived from the same provenance families.
- **v2 = v1 + 1,924 teacher-distilled real dictations** (teacher = gemini-2.5-flash + the v6 cloud prompt; hallucination-filtered, noop-capped) **+ 696 targeted convention drills.**
- **Leakage correction (2026-07-15):** shipping v2 has exact ID and normalized-input overlap with **1,549/1,890** full Type B rows; conservative normalization finds 1,551. Provenance-family components expose 1,866/1,890 approved rows and 899/900 overflow rows. The old zero-overlap statement conflated the protected hard-340/exact-overflow checks with the broader working set. The old 1,890 and overflow scores are historical development/robustness evidence, not held-out quality estimates. Owner receipt: `docs/experiments/eg1-multilingual/OVERNIGHT-LOG-2026-07-15.md` TYPE-B-001.
- **Prompt = training contract:** the model was trained AND ships with ONE fixed short system prompt + `<TRANSCRIPT>…</TRANSCRIPT>` wrapper (`scripts/eval/prompts/eg1-polish-prompt-v1.txt`, pinned by a golden-string test in `EGOnePromptBuilder`). Training prompt MUST equal inference prompt — prompt-shape drift cost ±18pp on the untuned base. This is why EG-1 gets its own `.egOneFixed` prompt family and ignores per-mode prompt rules (behavior is in the weights).

## FACT: eg1-quality-and-bias-gate
Judge: sonnet-5 `behavior_judge.py --system new`. (Numbers here are the #1265 acceptance run; note the separate marketing-vs-settings discrepancy tracked in `eg1-operations.md` FACT: eg1-quality-perf-baselines — 94.4/94.7 internal vs 93.7 public, same model, different run.)
- **Hard-340 subset:** 94.7%, **0 critical fails** (beat the gpt-4o + v6 cloud champion same-batch: 93.5%, 2 crit).
- **Full 1,890:** 94.4% (traps 97.3%, false-positive 2.4%).
- **Historical one-time bias gate:** exact-text-sealed 900-overflow scored 88.0% vs untuned-base anchor 61.2%; Type-C mixed slice 94.5%; 100 train-excluded real dictations ~95%. This does not prove held-out generalization because the 2026-07-15 provenance audit found 899/900 overflow rows in training-exposed Type B families.

## FACT: eg1-language-coverage-provenance
- **Trained overwhelmingly on English.** The v2 corpus is English-oriented `raw → cleaned` dictation data, but it is not literally ASCII-only. The historical quality numbers above are English.
- **Base is multilingual** (Qwen3-4B handles ~100+ languages) and the fixed prompt says "keep the same language … never translate," so EG-1 will *accept* and attempt to polish non-English — but that rides on base-model ability, un-benchmarked per-language. Lower-resourced languages will be rougher.
- **Multilingual training pairs are an explicit v3 lever** (epic #1190 follow-ups), not shipped in EG-1 v1/v2.
- Do not conflate with transcription: the ASR engines (Parakeet = English + 24 European; WhisperKit = 99) are a separate stage feeding EG-1 (`architecture.md`).

## RULE: eg1-retrain-recipe-for-eg2
Rule: To build EG-2 (or EG-1 v3), reuse this exact pipeline — QLoRA (Unsloth, 4090) → merge → GGUF → quantize → judge the quantized artifact against the SEALED holdout — and keep the training prompt byte-identical to the shipped prompt. A PROMPT change means a new `promptTemplateID` + new `PromptFamily` case (see `eg1-operations.md` RULE: eg1-hot-swap-contract); a weights-only update keeps the template id.
Why: prompt-shape drift alone swings quality ±18pp; judging fp16 instead of the quantized GGUF ships a number users never see.
How to apply: expand data on the v2 recipe (synthetic + teacher-distilled real dictations + targeted drills), preserve zero-overlap with the eval, re-run the one-time bias gate on any new data generation, and route any prompt-family change through a code-lane PR. Distribution (R2 upload, manifest bump) is `eg1-operations.md`.
Ref: #1265 method-of-record; #1190 epic.

## FACT: eg1-provenance-sources
| Want | Where |
|---|---|
| Method of record (final config, data recipe, results) | GitHub issue **#1265** comments (closed) |
| Base-model choice + QLoRA rationale + Fluid-1 teardown | `docs/competitive-analysis/2026-07-02-tune-base-model-vs-fluid1.md` |
| Bake-off scores (2 models × 3 prompts, quant rungs) | `scripts/eval/runs/bakeoff-1265/BASELINE_TABLE.md` |
| Training data (gitignored) | `scripts/eval/runs/bakeoff-1265/train_sft_v{1,2}.jsonl` |
| Shipped prompt (golden) | `scripts/eval/prompts/eg1-polish-prompt-v1.txt` → `EGOnePromptBuilder.systemPrompt` |
| Runtime / hosting / license / perf | [`eg1-operations.md`](eg1-operations.md) |
| The earlier, DEAD Apple-adapter LoRA PoC (different model) | [`afm-adapter-poc.md`](afm-adapter-poc.md) — do NOT confuse with EG-1 |

# EnviousWispr Personas

**when:** answering the User Rubric in a feature plan; invoking council on a human-centric feature (polish, UX, formatting, onboarding, error messaging); writing user-facing copy.

## RULE: named-persona-rubric
Rule: Resolve user-facing plans, persona council, and user-facing copy against named personas; do not answer as "a user."
Why: Generic users yield generic features; named cards force concrete trade-off framing.
How to apply: Cite personas by name in `docs/feature-requests/TEMPLATE.md`'s `Preface — User Rubric`; for council, one call per persona using the prompt template in [`council-operations.md`](council-operations.md). Disagreement between persona responses IS the design tension. For copy, write with one or two personas in mind per the EnviousMarketing content-type method (`~/Developer/EnviousLabs/EnviousMarketing/content/content-types.md`).

## FACT: persona-scope
Seven archetypes across six segments: five Ideal Buyer Persona segments in the EnviousWispr pack (`~/Developer/EnviousLabs/EnviousMarketing/packs/enviouswispr/brand-guide.md`) plus a sixth twin-persona slot added 2026-04-17 for caregivers and seniors. Archetypes, not real individuals — do not fabricate interview quotes.

## FACT: persona-cards
| Persona | Segment / constraint | Main surfaces | Failure mode | Demands |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Priya Ramachandran** (Developer) | Staff SWE, 200-person Series B fintech; leads team of 3. | Slack 40%, GitHub PR comments 30%, Notion/RFC 20%. | Apple Dictation drops technical terms; cloud tools leak proprietary code; subscription friction at 50 uses/day. | Sub-second, technical-vocab accurate, private-by-default, no subscription. |
| **Marcus Weber** (Writer / Journalist) | Freelance, 20yr print, 8K-sub Substack; early carpal tunnel. | Scrivener/Notes 60%, Gmail 20%, Google Docs 20%. | Over-polish destroys voice; "corrected" contractions/formalized prose unusable; lost line breaks on pauses. | Minimal edits, preserve voice and rhythm; stenographer, not editor. |
| **Diana Foster** (PM / Knowledge Worker) | Senior PM, healthtech startup; doesn't code; 30+ Slack/day. | Slack 50%, Notion specs 25%, Gmail 15%. | App-switch tools break flow; weird auto-punctuation. | Zero-switch (hold hotkey → speak → paste lands in focused app), clean output, respects list structure. |
| **Dr. Elena Vasquez** (Privacy-first Professional) | Clinical psychology researcher; HIPAA-adjacent. | Word 50% (notes/manuscripts), Mail 25%, Scrivener 25%. | Server upload, account requirement, recurring SaaS (institution won't approve), discoverable patient record on vendor infra. | 100% on-device verifiable, no account, one-time/free, IT-defensible. |
| **Aaron Wu** (RSI / Accessibility) | Senior FE engineer, 50-person startup; carpal tunnel, 3mo physio. | Slack 35%, GitHub PR review 30%, Linear 20%, code comments 15%. | Accuracy drops on technical terms; two-hand shortcuts; modal interruptions. | One-hand or foot-pedal trigger; technical-vocab accurate; no modals; graceful on pauses. |
| **Meera Patel** (Time-Pressed Parent) | Senior ProdOps, mid-size SaaS; two kids (3 and 7). | iMessage to partner 50% (one-handed), Notes 20%, Gmail 15%, Calendar 15%. | Inferred emoji misfires ("got fired today" → 🔥); over-polish formalizes kid-speak; sub-toggles she has no time for. | Simple, predictable, no proofreading budget, works instantly with zero setup. |
| **Frank Chen** (Senior) | Retired math teacher, 72; texts grandkids daily; arthritis. | iMessage family 60%, Mail 25%, Notes 10%, Photos captions 5%. | Tech complexity; account creation; UI changes between releases; cost (fixed income). | Free/one-time, no account, no choices; iOS-dictation-like but more accurate; ≤3 settings. |

## FACT: emoji-control-current
Current #341 is a binary toggle. When enabled, it converts only explicit "<phrase> emoji|emoticon" triggers. Inferred emoji is out of scope — which is why on-by-default is safe (no sentiment-driven surprise emoji; an uncustomized user sees emoji only when they explicitly say "X emoji"). Earlier persona ladder (Off / Controlled / Inferred) was superseded by the 2026-05-16 binary toggle. For the shipped default value, see [`settings-defaults.md`](settings-defaults.md) `## FACT: canonical-defaults-table` (single source of truth; `SettingsDefaultValues.emojiFormatterEnabled`).
Ref: #341 binary toggle; #923 default ratification (2026-05-30). The earlier `issue-341-2026-05-16-emoji-formatter.md` predates #923 and reads "Off (default)" — use `settings-defaults.md` for the current default, never that plan.

# Distribution — Bundling, Signing, Release

## FACT: distribution-scope
Source-to-distribution reference for dev and release bundles. Production is a non-sandboxed, Developer-ID-signed, notarized direct download, not a Mac App Store build. For MAS work, first read [mac-app-store-feasibility.md](mac-app-store-feasibility.md).

## FACT: build-tiers
| Tier | Command/source | Version and IDs | Signing | Output |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Dev | `/wispr-rebuild-and-relaunch` or `./scripts/build-dev-app.sh` using Tuist/Xcode Dev. | `-local`/`-dev`; app `com.enviouswispr.app.dev`; XPC `com.enviouswispr.audioservice.dev` and `com.enviouswispr.asrservice.dev`. | Stable self-signed `EnviousWispr Dev`. | `.derivedData/Dev/Build/Products/Dev/EnviousWispr Local.app` and `build/EnviousWispr Local.app`. |
| Release | `git tag v*` CI or `./scripts/build-release-dmg.sh <version>`. | Clean semver; app `com.enviouswispr.app`; XPC `com.enviouswispr.audioservice` and `com.enviouswispr.asrservice`. | Developer ID, embedded profile, inside-out signing, notarization, stapling. | Xcode archive, signed app, dSYMs, DMG. |

`build-release-dmg.sh` replaced the SwiftPM `build-dmg.sh` in #913 PR6 and requires a version. Runtime tier detection uses `CFBundleShortVersionString`. Dev and release intentionally share `~/Library/Application Support/EnviousWispr/transcripts/` because `appSupportDir = "EnviousWispr"`; founder-confirmed 2026-05-04, #604.

## FACT: release-signing-identity
- Active since 2026-04-29: `Developer ID Application: ENVIOUS LABS LLC (9UT54V24XG)`, G2 Sub-CA, valid through 2031-04-30.
- Team ID: `9UT54V24XG`; Team Name: `ENVIOUS LABS LLC`. Migration case 102867726755 completed 2026-04-28.
- CI forms `CODESIGN_IDENTITY = "Developer ID Application: ${APPLE_TEAM_NAME} (${APPLE_TEAM_ID})"` from `APPLE_TEAM_NAME`, `APPLE_TEAM_ID`, `DEVELOPER_ID_CERT_BASE64`, and `DEVELOPER_ID_CERT_PASSWORD`.
- Legacy, never revoke: `Developer ID Application: Saurabh Vaish (9UT54V24XG)`. It signed releases before 2026-04-29. The working-keychain copy was removed 2026-05-31 after #925; the authoritative backup remains `~/Developer/EnviousLabs/EnviousLabsLLC/vault/apple-developer/developer-id.p12`. Revoke is forbidden; working-keychain deletion is not.
- Lifecycle source: `~/Developer/EnviousLabs/EnviousLabsLLC/vault/apple-developer/README.md`.

## RULE: use-dev-bundle-script
Rule: Build dev bundles only with `./scripts/build-dev-app.sh`.
Why: It owns Tuist generation, the `EnviousWispr-Dev` arm64 build, XPC embedding, stable signing, strict verification, copy, xattr cleanup, launch, bundle-ID checks, blank `SUFeedURL`, and executable checks.
How to apply: Output is `build/EnviousWispr Local.app`; Dev uses `EnviousWispr-Dev.entitlements` without team-prefixed keychain entitlements or a provisioning profile. Ref: #913 PR4.

## FACT: dev-bundle-invariants
- App: `com.enviouswispr.app.dev`; cert: `EnviousWispr Dev`; `SUFeedURL` blank.
- XPC: `com.enviouswispr.audioservice.dev`, `com.enviouswispr.asrservice.dev`.
- Transcripts: `~/Library/Application Support/EnviousWispr/transcripts/`.
- PkgInfo: encode `?` as `\x3f`; bare `?` is a zsh glob.

Active entitlement keys:
- Main app, Dev and Release: `com.apple.security.device.audio-input`, `com.apple.security.personal-information.addressbook`, `com.apple.security.automation.apple-events`.
- Release main app only: `com.apple.application-identifier`, `com.apple.developer.team-identifier`, `keychain-access-groups`.
- Audio XPC: `com.apple.security.device.audio-input`.
- ASR XPC: none currently. `com.apple.developer.kernel.increased-memory-limit` is only a future comment, not active.

## RULE: stable-dev-certificate
Rule: Use a stable self-signed "EnviousWispr Dev" certificate for dev builds so TCC grants persist across rebuilds.
Why: macOS anchors TCC to (bundle ID + signing identity); rotating signing identity loses Accessibility/Microphone grants on every rebuild.
How to apply: First-time setup creates a 10-year self-signed cert and grants Accessibility once in System Settings; subsequent rebuilds preserve grants.
```bash
TMPDIR=$(mktemp -d)
openssl req -x509 -nodes -newkey rsa:2048 -days 3650 \
  -keyout "$TMPDIR/dev.key" -out "$TMPDIR/dev.crt" \
  -subj "/CN=EnviousWispr Dev/O=EnviousWispr Development"
openssl pkcs12 -export -out "$TMPDIR/dev.p12" \
  -inkey "$TMPDIR/dev.key" -in "$TMPDIR/dev.crt" -passout pass:""
security import "$TMPDIR/dev.p12" -k ~/Library/Keychains/login.keychain-db \
  -T /usr/bin/codesign -f pkcs12 -P ""
sudo security add-trusted-cert -d -r trustRoot \
  -k ~/Library/Keychains/login.keychain-db "$TMPDIR/dev.crt"
rm -rf "$TMPDIR"
```
Delete: `security delete-certificate -c "EnviousWispr Dev" ~/Library/Keychains/login.keychain-db`.

## FACT: self-hosted-release-runner-decommissioned (2026-07-02, supersedes offline-emergency-only)
The `enviouswispr-release` M4 Pro runner is documented as fully decommissioned: runner 22 unregistered and `~/actions-runner/` deleted after hosted releases v2.2.0 and v2.2.1. No workflow has targeted `[self-hosted, enviouswispr-release]` since #1113; current release macOS jobs use `macos-26`. Recovery recipe: `docs/self-hosted-runner.md`. Ref: #1087. Registration/deletion state requires live GitHub and machine verification.

## FACT: first-hosted-release-appcast-purge-race (v2.2.0, 2026-06-24, #1217)
Historical: v2.2.0 exposed a Pages-deploy/edge-purge race that left `https://enviouswispr.com/appcast.xml` stale for about 12 minutes. Current `publish-appcast` waits for origin, purges zone `b4416a0f0ebd699e96969a331c7ad7ff`, then verifies the canonical URL. If that limb fails, follow [`infra/cloudflare.md`](file:///Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/infra/cloudflare.md) and recheck the non-cache-busted URL.

## FACT: release-pipeline-jobs
A tag or `mode=full` runs the original six release stages plus the newer Homebrew limb:

| # | Job | Role | Recovery |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `preflight` | Validates tag, inputs, secrets, and source run. | Included in all modes. |
| 2 | `build-release-artifacts` | SDK probe, Tuist/Xcode archive, sign, profile, DMG, notarize, staple, Sparkle-sign, dSYMs. | `full`; `build-only` validates without publishing. |
| 3 | `publish-github-release` | Idempotent release create/upload. | `release-only` + `source_run_id`. |
| 4 | `publish-appcast` | Push or PR fallback, Pages wait, edge purge, canonical-feed verification. Non-blocking limb. | `appcast-only` + `source_run_id`. |
| 5 | `upload-sentry-dsyms` | Upload symbols. Non-blocking limb. | `sentry-only` + `source_run_id`. |
| 6 | `release-summary` | Always reports heart and limb results. | Included automatically. |
| 7 | `bump-homebrew-cask` | Dispatches tap `bump-cask.yml` with the published version. Non-blocking limb. | Manual tap dispatch fallback. |

Modes: `full`, `release-only`, `appcast-only`, `sentry-only`, `build-only`. Cross-run recovery uses explicit `source_run_id`. Hosted migration refs: #1113, #1117, PR #1133. Appcast refs: #1019. Homebrew automation: #1368.

## RULE: ci-only-pre-tag-validation
Rule: Tag only after the release PR’s `build-check` and main’s `main-post-merge-build` pass.
How to apply: Follow `/wispr-release-checklist`. Governance and release flow are owned by [`github.md`](github.md).

## RULE: ci-governance-table
Pointer only: action SHA pinning, immutable releases, required checks, strict-mode setting, and branch protection are owned by [`github.md`](github.md). Relevant history: PR #712.

- `pr-check.yml`: `build-debug` and `build-release` use `macos-26`; the `build-check` aggregator uses `ubuntu-latest`.

## FACT: observability-release-relevant
- PostHog: `POSTHOG_API_KEY` is stamped as `PostHogAPIKey`; dev fallback is `~/.enviouswispr-keys/posthog-api-key`.
- Sentry: `SENTRY_DSN` is stamped into app/XPC plists; dev fallback is `~/.enviouswispr-keys/sentry-dsn`.

- The archive copies all dSYMs; the release gate requires the three first-party dSYMs. The v2.1.4 artifact contained eight total, including five Sparkle dSYMs. Ref: #1088 and #913 PR6.

## FACT: sparkle-autoupdate
Version ownership: [`tech-stack.md`](tech-stack.md). Release behavior:
- `@preconcurrency import Sparkle`; `SPUStandardUpdaterController(startingUpdater: true)`.
- “Check for Updates...” calls `updaterController.checkForUpdates(_:)`.
- EdDSA public key: Info.plist `SUPublicEDKey`.
- Feed: Info.plist `SUFeedURL = https://enviouswispr.com/appcast.xml`.
- `sign_update` is found under `build/DerivedData/.../SourcePackages/artifacts/sparkle/Sparkle/bin/sign_update`.
- Controller ownership moved in PR-B.1 of #763.

## FACT: update-adoption-posture (decided 2026-06-17)
Info.plist policy: `SUEnableAutomaticChecks=true`, `SUScheduledCheckInterval=3600`, `SUAutomaticallyUpdate=false`. The app checks and prompts but never silently installs. Refs: #639, #958, #1019.

The 1.x cohort remains accepted-unreachable: Sparkle plumbing is compatible, but those builds lack the 2.x proactive checks/banner and no contact channel exists. Do not re-file this as a plumbing bug.

Possible future lever: `SUAutomaticallyUpdate=true` for ordinary patches, with `minimumAutoupdateVersion` for attended high-risk upgrades. Undecided; ref #953 and the 2026-06-16 scorecard/dossier.

## FACT: dmg-build
`./scripts/build-release-dmg.sh <version>` performs Tuist generation, unsigned Xcode archive, plist stamping, profile embedding, GPL/notices/source bundling INTO the app (step `[4b/9]`, before signing seals it), inside-out signing, verification, dSYM collection, and branded DMG creation. CI notarizes and staples afterward.

DMG install window (#1486): a committed `assets/installer/EnviousWispr-DMG-Background@2x.png` (1200×800, re-tagged 144 dpi on a build copy so 1200 px → 600 pt) is set via `create-dmg --background`; the real app icon and Applications alias land in two artwork "landing boxes" at point coords held as named constants near the top of the script (`DMG_APP_X/Y=131/258`, `DMG_APPLICATIONS_X/Y=469/258`, window 600×400, icon 100). `.app` extension hidden. Coords were pinned by real mounted-DMG UAT — the artwork is authoritative, do not recompute from box math.

`PostHogAPIKey` is xcconfig-stamped. `SentryDSN` and `SUFeedURL` are post-archive `plutil` stamps because xcconfig treats `//` as a comment; #1113 fixed the hosted-runner Sentry slash bug. The script hard-checks signatures, team, hardened runtime, timestamps, profile authorization, DMG contents, and the three first-party dSYMs. Ref: #913 PR6.

## RULE: embed-provisioning-profile
Rule: Copy `signing/EnviousWispr_DeveloperID.provisionprofile` to `Contents/embedded.provisionprofile` after xattr cleanup and before main-app signing.
Why: `keychain-access-groups` is restricted. Without an authorizing Developer ID profile, macOS 26 rejects launch with taskgated “no eligible provisioning profiles found”, amfid `-413`, and runningboardd `error=163`.
How to apply:
- Bundle: `com.enviouswispr.app`; Team ID: `9UT54V24XG`.
- Release entitlement keys: `com.apple.application-identifier`, `com.apple.developer.team-identifier`, `keychain-access-groups`.
- Active profile: “EnviousWispr Developer ID”, id `4Y275QFC59`, type MAC_APP_DIRECT, expires 2044-05-25.
- Profile regeneration and Apple ASC keys are owned by [`~/.claude/knowledge/secrets-management.md`](file:///Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/secrets-management.md), using `apple-api-admin-key-id`, `apple-api-admin-p8`, and `apple-api-issuer-id`.
Ref: #913.

## RULE: sign-inside-out
Rule: Sign each Mach-O before its container in this exact order:
1. Sparkle nested app: Updater.app.
2. Sparkle nested XPCs: Downloader.xpc, Installer.xpc.
3. Sparkle bare Mach-O: Autoupdate.
4. Sparkle.framework/Versions/B.
5. App XPC services: audioservice, asrservice.
6. EG-1 bare Mach-O: `Contents/Resources/llama-server`.
7. Embed the profile, then sign the main app.

Use `--options runtime` and secure timestamps. `build-release-dmg.sh` owns execution. Missing Autoupdate caused v1.5.2 and v1.5.3 failures; EG-1 is #1271. Trap ownership: [`gotchas-release.md`](gotchas-release.md).

## RULE: never-deep-sign
Rule: Never sign with `--deep`; it can re-sign and invalidate embedded code. `--deep` is verification-only.

## FACT: verify-and-notarize
```bash
codesign --verify --strict --verbose=2 EnviousWispr.app
spctl --assess --type exec --verbose EnviousWispr.app

xcrun notarytool submit EnviousWispr.dmg \
  --key "$API_KEY_PATH" --key-id "$API_KEY_ID" --issuer "$API_ISSUER_ID" --wait
xcrun stapler staple EnviousWispr.dmg
```
Use `notarytool submit --wait --timeout 18000`; never hand-roll polling. See [`gotchas-release.md`](gotchas-release.md) `notarytool-wait`.

## FACT: release-build-config
The release artifact is an arm64 `xcodebuild archive` in `Release`, driven by `build-release-dmg.sh`; FluidAudio uses Float16. `swift build -c release` is only a quick compile check, not a distributable build. Prefer a local release-DMG proof or `scripts/xcode-test.sh --release` before tagging. Release uses `-O`/WMO; Debug uses `-Onone`.

## FACT: ci-secrets
Current workflow names:
- Signing: `DEVELOPER_ID_CERT_BASE64`, `DEVELOPER_ID_CERT_PASSWORD`, `APPLE_TEAM_ID`, `APPLE_TEAM_NAME`.
- Notarization: `APPLE_API_KEY_BASE64`, `APPLE_API_KEY_ID`, `APPLE_API_ISSUER_ID`.
- Sparkle: `SPARKLE_PRIVATE_KEY`. `SPARKLE_EDDSA_PUBLIC_KEY` is retained as a named legacy/config item; the current public key is committed under `SUPublicEDKey`.
- App metadata/telemetry: `POSTHOG_API_KEY`, `SENTRY_DSN`.
- Sentry upload: `SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN`, `SENTRY_ORG`, `SENTRY_PROJECT`.
- GitHub App: `APP_ID`, `APP_PRIVATE_KEY`.
- Appcast/cache: `CLOUDFLARE_PURGE_TOKEN`.
- Release alert: `DISCORD_RELEASE_WEBHOOK`.
- Automatic Actions token: `GITHUB_TOKEN`.
- Retained but unused legacy altool names: `APPLE_ID`, `APPLE_ID_PASSWORD`.

Do not print secret values. Apple key ownership and retrieval live in global `secrets-management.md`.

## RULE: appcast-push-token
Rule: Appcast and tap dispatch use the `enviouswispr-release-bot` GitHub App, ID 3176135, through `actions/create-github-app-token` with `APP_ID` and `APP_PRIVATE_KEY`.
Why: Appcast checkout uses `persist-credentials: false`; direct push can bypass `main-protection`, with PR fallback. The tap token is scoped to `saurabhav88/homebrew-tap` with `actions:write`.
Ref: `release.yml`.

## FACT: github
Repo: `saurabhav88/EnviousWispr`, public. Use the PATH `gh`, not stale `~/bin/gh`. Git, PR, branch-protection, CI-governance, and issue-command ownership: [`github.md`](github.md) and [`github-light.md`](github-light.md).

## FACT: license-gplv3
EnviousWispr is GPLv3, an OSI-approved copyleft license, since 2026-06-22. Relicensing shipped in PRs #1184 and #1185.

GPLv3 §6 applies to the DMG. Since #1486 the license payload rides INSIDE the app at `Contents/Resources/Licenses/` (`GPL-3.0.txt`, `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt`, commit-pinned `SOURCE.txt`), sealed by the main-app signature and re-asserted post-mount; the DMG install window shows ONLY the app + Applications alias (no loose files). Codex-grounded legal basis: license text/notices travel with the binary either way (bundle satisfies MIT/BSD/Apache and GPLv3 §4); GPLv3 §6(d) source *directions* may live on the download page. Earlier (#1186 / PR #1188) the three files were loose in the DMG root. `gen-third-party-notices.sh --check` still gates freshness; the notices now also carry the Apache-2.0 swift-transformers attribution from Argmax's `NOTICES` (was omitted pre-#1486). Follow-up #1487: in-app "Open Source Licenses" screen + download-page source link, after which `SOURCE.txt` can be dropped from the bundle. “Open source” is correct. The EnviousWispr name and logo remain Envious Labs LLC trademarks.

## FACT: homebrew-tap
Tap: `saurabhav88/homebrew-tap`. Install:
`brew install --cask saurabhav88/tap/enviouswispr`

Tap contract:
- `Casks/enviouswispr.rb` pins release `version` and `sha256`, uses the versioned `EnviousWispr-<version>.dmg`, `verified:`, `auto_updates true`, Sonoma minimum, arm64, and `zap trash`.
- `bump-cask.yml` accepts optional `version`; with it, validates anchored `X.Y.Z` and avoids cache-stale `releases/latest`. Without it, manual and scheduled runs resolve latest.
- It reconciles both version and SHA, commits only on change, self-heals a bad SHA, and otherwise no-ops.
- `release.yml` dispatches it after publication with an App token. Failure is non-blocking, files a bug, appears in release summary/Discord, and falls back to:
  `gh workflow run bump-cask.yml -R saurabhav88/homebrew-tap -f version=<X.Y.Z>`
- Weekly backstop: Mondays 09:17 UTC.
- No fine-grained PAT. Ref: #1368.
- Earlier manual-only workflow was completed 2026-06-23; automated trigger shipped 2026-07-06.
- Discoverability shipped in PR #1189: README Quick Start, homepage hero, and bottom CTA.

Tap-internal details and cron require verification against the tap checkout when network access is available.

# Model Delivery (epic #1348)

**when:** ANY work touching how model bytes reach a user's machine — Parakeet/WhisperKit/EG-1 downloads, the models.enviouslabs.co mirror, download telemetry, cache validation/repair, first-run model UX, or planning any #1348 phase.
**why:** the #1339 launch-night incident proved the default model path depended on a borrowed downloader (FluidAudio's → Hugging Face) with no failover/verification/cancel; the founder-ratified pivot is one owned, verified cache-admission layer.
**Authority:** `docs/model-delivery/model-delivery-contract.md` (LIVE contract, currently v1.2). This knowledge file is the stable pointer + invariants summary; the contract wins where they diverge. Phases 1-3 shipped; Phase 4 WhisperKit convergence remains parked. Durable final artifacts are posted to epic #1348 as comments (one per artifact). At epic completion, promote the final contract INTO this file.

## RULE: model-delivery-contract-is-the-authority
Rule: Before designing, reviewing, or amending ANYTHING in the delivery layer, read `docs/model-delivery/model-delivery-contract.md`; a plan that needs to deviate amends the contract FIRST, with rationale, and posts the amendment to epic #1348.
Why: architecture negotiated through PR comments is the failure mode that cost 17 review rounds on EG-1 (#1271) and 7 on the parked PR-2.
Ref: epic #1348; founder directive 2026-07-06 ("document all of this... in-depth plan phase, large/refactor tier").

## FACT: the-ten-invariants (contract §1, stable)
1. Verified or absent — no unverified bytes in a final cache path; temp download → SHA-256 BEFORE admission → atomic move.
2. One writer — single-flight per ModelIdentity; readers only after admission.
3. Atomic admission — old valid state or new valid state, never a mix.
4. Manifest-pinned — versioned manifest (files+sizes+SHA-256+source templates); NO runtime listing calls (#1339 failure mode #1).
5. Cooperative cancellation — typed result, never process kill; no partial in final cache; temp resumable or discardable.
6. Resumable where possible — URLSession resume data + Range (mirror serves full Range semantics).
7. Source-aware — our copy (models.enviouslabs.co) first, upstream backup second, per-file failover, order overridable.
8. Telemetry-complete — every attempt speaks the shared 14 emitted failure classes plus the reserved `constrained_network_deferred` class (contract §7); Ollama included at lifecycle granularity.
9. Runtime-agnostic — the layer ENDS at a verified cache location; never loads/compiles models.
10. Deletes old supervision code — net launch-path complexity DOWN per migration PR (deletion ledger is a merge criterion).

Plus (v1.1): manifest trust root — source selection is mutable; expected identity/hash/size is immutable except via trusted app update (bundled pin is the authority; a drifted mirror can never achieve admission). Disk lifecycle — preflight before fetch, stale-version eviction, disk-full is a first-class recoverable failure; constrained/metered-network policy is a deliberate decision, not a default-by-omission.

## FACT: scope-guardrails (founder + CTO agreed, binding)
Unify ONLY where WE move bytes: Parakeet, WhisperKit, EG-1. Ollama = shared telemetry only (its daemon owns bytes; `OllamaSetupService` commands it). Sparkle = fully out of scope. No god-layer: one fetcher + thin per-backend adapters. The shared `ModelDeliveryController` + `ManifestFetchTask` + `CacheAdmission` engine (`Sources/EnviousWisprModelDelivery/`) generalized the now-retired `EGOneModelStore`'s mechanisms (resume identity, generation tokens, cancel drain-gate, SHA-before-install, disk preflight, stale purge) from one file to manifest sets.

## FACT: phase-map
1. **Phase 1 SHIPPED:** invariants + D1-D7 documentation (plan `docs/feature-requests/issue-1348-2026-07-06-modeldelivery-phase1-invariants.md`, Gate-2 approved 2026-07-06 after council + 4 grounded-review rounds): inventory grid / identity+manifest schema / failure taxonomy+metric glossary / concurrency+cancel contract / local override flags+rollback / UX state semantics / drill matrix with 3 oracles per drill; documentation only, zero code.
2. **Phase 2 SHIPPED:** Parakeet moved onto the owned fetcher.
3. **Phase 3 SHIPPED:** EG-1 converged onto the shared engine (`EGOneDeliveryAdapter`); `eg1.download_*` telemetry retired in favor of `model_delivery.*`.
4. **Phase 4 PARKED:** WhisperKit still moves bytes through `WhisperKit.download` (`WhisperKitSetupService.swift`); the loader already uses `download:false` + supplied `modelFolder` (`WhisperKitBackend.swift`), so only the byte-moving replacement remains. Plan `docs/feature-requests/issue-1386-2026-07-07-modeldelivery-phase4-whisperkit.md`. Ollama telemetry convergence is part of this remaining scope.

## FACT: salvage-map
Historical #1339 salvage has landed in current owners: bundled pinned manifests + component validate-and-repair in `Sources/EnviousWisprModelDelivery/` (`DeliveryManifest.swift`, per-file fetch loop in `ManifestFetchTask.swift`), the live `enviouswispr-parakeet-mirror` Worker on models.enviouslabs.co, and its deploy/drill gate at `workers/parakeet-mirror/scripts/verify-deploy.py`. The former `feat/1339-reliable-model-source` branch no longer exists (local + remote) — use the #1339 checkpoint comment only for history.

## FACT: global-edge-caching (#1405, Phase 1 shipped 2026-07-07, Phase 2 shipped 2026-07-08)
Far-from-origin first-run downloads stalled on single-region R2 with no edge caching (distance, not volume). **Phase 1 SHIPPED** (no app update — reaches installed users): the worker 302-redirects known downloads to the object's native cache-enabled R2 `/parakeet/…` path; caching is scoped to `/parakeet/` only (`/eg1/` and worker paths excluded). Canonical config + verification: `~/.claude/knowledge/infra/cloudflare.md` FACT: model-delivery-edge-caching and `workers/parakeet-mirror/`. **Phase 2 SHIPPED** (PR #1410, `8316bff`, closes #1371): bounded same-source retry + restored the load watchdog (`LoadProgressWatcher`) to LOAD-only supervision (parked during `downloadOwnedPhases`); the fetcher's own 60s idle timeout is the sole download-stall authority — net deletion, no new timeout number. Canonical retry/backoff/timeout/stall behavior: [download-resilience-standards.md](download-resilience-standards.md).

## FACT: install-phase-false-wedge-is-NOT-a-download-problem (#1388, fixed 2026-07-09)
Do not re-diagnose "Setup is taking longer than expected... check your VPN" (or `wedge_detected` with `observed_phase="Installing model..."`) as a download problem. Production forensics (14d): **117 of 126 wedge fires happened during the CoreML INSTALL (compile) phase**, which runs with networking disabled — every one a false positive. Root cause: the guard's post-signal silence gate ("gate B", `silence > max(floor, 5x maxGap)`) assumes homogeneous work units, but the compile emits ~52 ticks ~1.5s apart (small models) then goes silent for one ~445MB encoder taking ~20x longer — the ratio self-calibrates on the wrong distribution and the 15s floor becomes the sole trigger. Worse, the fire's teardown never resumed the pending load continuation (`ASRManagerProxy`'s invalidation handler didn't touch it), so **119/126 fires reached no terminal outcome** — the launch warm-up silently hung, the guard slot leaked, and users got warm only via the separate onboarding warm-up. #1388 fixed the completion contract (resume-once, typed causes: death → `serviceUnreachable` and the adapter's one-shot retry; cancel → `ASRLoadCancelledError`, non-transport so the retry can't resurrect it), REMOVED gate B from the model-load guard (gate A — the 120s no-signal-at-all deadline — survives, and the Phase-2 download parking (`downloadOwnedPhases`/`wasInDownloadPhase` + boundary reset) is RETAINED because it protects gate A too, not just gate B — without it download ticks satisfy the pre-first-signal deadline and a service that hangs before its first LOAD signal is undetectable (Codex r2 P1); only `transferSilenceFloorSeconds` was deleted as genuinely gate-B-only), added a user Cancel during install (`EngineWarmupOutcome.cancelled`, never a failure), corrected the copy, and added un-truncated install telemetry (`install_duration_ms`/`install_silence_max_ms` on `coldstart.warmup_completed`). Success-duration data justified removal over re-tuning: onboarding warm-up p99.9 = 2080s, so any non-false-firing deadline would exceed ~35 minutes — a give-up policy, not a detector. Plan: `docs/feature-requests/issue-1388-2026-07-09-install-phase-false-wedge.md` (5 grounded-review rounds). Fault drill: `A11_asr_kill_mid_model_load`.

**Still open (#1405): Phase 3** = object-size guardrail (>512MB fail / >450MB warn) + cache-HIT/per-country monitoring in `verify-deploy.py` + DE canary — not started, low priority (safety-net/monitoring, not user-facing). Plan: `docs/feature-requests/issue-1405-2026-07-07-model-delivery-global-cdn.md` (Phase 1/2); Phase 2 root-cause pivot plan: `docs/feature-requests/issue-1405-2026-07-08-downloader-owns-stall.md`. Industry-standard retry/backoff/timeout/stall/resume defaults (cited) + how EW maps to them: [download-resilience-standards.md](download-resilience-standards.md) (read before designing ANY retry/timeout value — copy its §1 consensus numbers, do not invent).

**EG-1 CDN sharding — delivery SHIPPED #1417 (2026-07-08), edge-cache rule still pending:** the monolithic ~2.9GB EG-1 was ~5.6x over the 512MB free-tier cache ceiling. #1417 split it into eight independently-verified GGUF shards (each ≤450MB) using the shared engine's `componentSet` admission (`Sources/EnviousWispr/Resources/eg1-delivery-manifest.json`, baseURL `/eg1/`); shard 1 is the runtime entrypoint and llama.cpp auto-discovers the siblings. EG-1 already inherits Phase 2 retry/resume via `ManifestFetchTask` (self-heals like Parakeet), and each shard is now SIZE-eligible for free-tier caching — but the Cloudflare Cache Rule covering the shard path is a separate, founder-gated infra mutation NOT yet deployed (cloudflare.md still scopes caching to `/parakeet/`; `/eg1/` excluded), so EG-1 gets no edge-cache HIT benefit yet. Still single-source (no mirror/backup), unlike Parakeet's mirror+HF failover. Canonical artifact/hosting/authoring: [eg1-operations.md](eg1-operations.md) FACT: eg1-artifact-identity + FACT: eg1-shard-authoring-runbook. Design rationale/history: [eg1-cdn-sharding.md](eg1-cdn-sharding.md). Briefing: `docs/model-delivery/2026-07-08-cdn-caching-briefing.md`.
