   230	def mechanical_gate(report: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
   231	    baseline = report["models"]["baseline"]
   232	    candidate = report["models"]["candidate"]
   233	    comparison = next(
   234	        item
   235	        for item in report["paired_comparisons"]
   236	        if item["left_model"] == "baseline" and item["right_model"] == "candidate"
   237	    )
   238	    positive = comparison["positive_strict"]
   239	    net_gain = positive["right_only"] - positive["left_only"]
   240	    baseline_by_id = {row["id"]: row for row in baseline["case_results"]}
   241	    candidate_by_id = {row["id"]: row for row in candidate["case_results"]}
   242	    restraint_ids = [
   243	        case_id
   244	        for case_id, row in baseline_by_id.items()
   245	        if row["role"] == "prose_restraint"
   246	    ]
   247	    candidate_only_false_lists = [
   248	        case_id
   249	        for case_id in restraint_ids
   250	        if candidate_by_id[case_id]["false_list"] and not baseline_by_id[case_id]["false_list"]
   251	    ]
   252	    loss_checks: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {}
   253	    for role in ("positive_list", "prose_restraint"):
   254	        for key in ("items_preserved", "scope_preserved"):
   255	            name = f"{role}.{key.replace('_preserved', '_loss')}"
   256	            baseline_loss = role_losses(baseline, role, key)
   257	            candidate_loss = role_losses(candidate, role, key)
   258	            loss_checks[name] = {
   259	                "baseline": baseline_loss,
   260	                "candidate": candidate_loss,
   261	                "pass": candidate_loss <= baseline_loss,
   262	            }
   263	
   264	    conditions = {
   265	        "both_arms_inference_healthy": {
   266	            "pass": baseline["inference_failure_count"] == 0
   267	            and candidate["inference_failure_count"] == 0,
   268	        },
   269	        "positive_net_gain_at_least_8": {"value": net_gain, "pass": net_gain >= 8},
   270	        "positive_mcnemar_p_below_0_05": {
   271	            "value": positive["exact_mcnemar_p_two_sided"],
   272	            "pass": positive["exact_mcnemar_p_two_sided"] < 0.05,
   273	        },
   274	        "zero_candidate_only_restraint_false_lists": {
   275	            "ids": candidate_only_false_lists,
   276	            "pass": not candidate_only_false_lists,
   277	        },
   278	        "candidate_total_false_lists_not_above_baseline": {
   279	            "baseline": baseline["restraint"]["false_list"]["successes"],
   280	            "candidate": candidate["restraint"]["false_list"]["successes"],
   281	            "pass": candidate["restraint"]["false_list"]["successes"]
   282	            <= baseline["restraint"]["false_list"]["successes"],
   283	        },
   284	        "item_and_scope_loss_not_increased_per_lane": {
   285	            "details": loss_checks,
   286	            "pass": all(item["pass"] for item in loss_checks.values()),
   287	        },
   288	    }
   289	    return {
   290	        "mechanical_conditions": conditions,
   291	        "mechanical_pass": all(item["pass"] for item in conditions.values()),
   292	        "semantic_condition": "pending_arm_blind_review",
   293	        "candidate_advances": False,
   294	        "candidate_advances_reason": "semantic review is required after mechanical scoring",
   295	    }
   296	
   297	
   298	def main() -> int:
   299	    args = parse_args()
   300	    if args.out.exists() or args.out.is_symlink():
   301	        raise SystemExit("--out already exists; refusing to overwrite A/B scoring evidence")
   302	    if not args.out.parent.is_dir():
   303	        raise SystemExit("--out parent directory must already exist")
   304	
   305	    ab, baseline_path, candidate_path, render_path, ab_sha = load_bound_inputs(
   306	        args.positive_corpus,
   307	        args.restraint_corpus,
   308	        args.ab_bundle,
   309	        args.expected_ab_receipt_sha256,
   310	    )
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Deterministically score audited two-item list development cases.
     3	
     4	This scorer is intentionally narrow. It checks only facts that the corpus can
     5	prove mechanically: exactly two bullet lines, no extra prose beyond an optional
     6	header, all audited required phrases, and no audited forbidden phrases. Human or
     7	model-assisted semantic review remains a separate gate.
     8	"""
     9	
    10	from __future__ import annotations
    11	
    12	import argparse
    13	import json
    14	import math
    15	import re
    16	import unicodedata
    17	from collections import Counter, defaultdict
    18	from pathlib import Path
    19	from typing import Any
    20	
    21	
    22	BULLET_RE = re.compile(r"^\s*[-*•]\s+\S")
    23	
    24	
    25	def parse_args() -> argparse.Namespace:
    26	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    27	    parser.add_argument("--corpus", nargs="+", required=True)
    28	    parser.add_argument("--candidates", nargs="+", required=True)
    29	    parser.add_argument("--out", required=True)
    30	    parser.add_argument("--model-id", help="Force multiple candidate shards into one model group")
    31	    return parser.parse_args()

    33	
    34	def read_jsonl(path: Path) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    35	    return [json.loads(line) for line in path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines() if line]
    36	
    37	
    38	def normalized(text: str) -> str:
    39	    value = unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", text).casefold()
    40	    value = value.replace("’", "'").replace("–", "-").replace("—", "-")
    41	    value = re.sub(r"[-_/]", " ", value)
    42	    value = re.sub(r"[^\w$%.']+", " ", value)
    43	    return " ".join(value.split())
    44	
    45	
    46	def contains_phrase(text: str, phrase: str) -> bool:
    47	    haystack = f" {normalized(text)} "
    48	    needle = normalized(phrase)
    49	    return bool(needle) and f" {needle} " in haystack
    50	
    51	
    52	def wilson(successes: int, total: int) -> tuple[float, float]:
    53	    if total == 0:
    54	        return 0.0, 0.0
    55	    z = 1.959963984540054
    56	    p = successes / total
    57	    denominator = 1 + z * z / total
    58	    center = (p + z * z / (2 * total)) / denominator
    59	    margin = z * math.sqrt((p * (1 - p) + z * z / (4 * total)) / total) / denominator
    60	    return center - margin, center + margin
    61	
    62	
    63	def structure_ok(output: str) -> tuple[bool, int, list[str]]:
    64	    lines = [line.strip() for line in output.splitlines() if line.strip()]
    65	    bullets = [line for line in lines if BULLET_RE.match(line)]
    66	    other = [line for line in lines if not BULLET_RE.match(line)]
    67	    bare_list = len(lines) == 2 and len(bullets) == 2
    68	    headed_list = (
    69	        len(lines) == 3
    70	        and not BULLET_RE.match(lines[0])
    71	        and (lines[0].endswith(":") or lines[0].endswith("—"))
    72	        and all(BULLET_RE.match(line) for line in lines[1:])
    73	    )
    74	    return bare_list or headed_list, len(bullets), other
    75	
    76	
    77	def main() -> None:
    78	    args = parse_args()
    79	    cases: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {}
    80	    for raw_path in args.corpus:
    81	        for case in read_jsonl(Path(raw_path)):
    82	            if case["id"] in cases:
    83	                raise SystemExit(f"Duplicate corpus id: {case['id']}")
    84	            cases[case["id"]] = case
    85	
    86	    grouped_rows: dict[str, list[dict[str, Any]]] = defaultdict(list)
    87	    grouped_paths: dict[str, list[str]] = defaultdict(list)
    88	    for raw_path in args.candidates:
    89	        path = Path(raw_path)
    90	        rows = read_jsonl(path)
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Mechanically score positive-list activation and prose-restraint outputs."""
     3	
     4	from __future__ import annotations
     5	
     6	import argparse
     7	import json
     8	import math
     9	import re
    10	from collections import defaultdict
    11	from pathlib import Path
    12	from typing import Any
    13	
    14	
    15	LIST_LINE_RE = re.compile(r"^\s*(?:[-*•]|\d+[.)])\s+\S")
    16	
    17	
    18	def parse_args() -> argparse.Namespace:
    19	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    20	    parser.add_argument("--positive-corpus", type=Path, required=True)
    21	    parser.add_argument("--trap-corpus", type=Path, required=True)
    22	    parser.add_argument("--positive-candidates", type=Path, nargs="+", required=True)
    23	    parser.add_argument("--trap-candidates", type=Path, nargs="+", required=True)
    24	    parser.add_argument("--out", type=Path, required=True)
    25	    return parser.parse_args()
    26	
    27	
    28	def rows(path: Path) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    29	    return [json.loads(line) for line in path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines() if line]
    30	
    31	
    32	def output_text(row: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
    33	    return str(row.get("output", row.get("candidate", ""))).strip()
    34	
    35	
    36	def list_lines(text: str) -> int:
    37	    return sum(bool(LIST_LINE_RE.match(line)) for line in text.splitlines())
    38	
    39	
    40	def wilson(successes: int, total: int) -> list[float]:
    41	    if total == 0:
    42	        return [0.0, 0.0]
    43	    z = 1.959963984540054
    44	    p = successes / total
    45	    denominator = 1 + z * z / total
    46	    center = (p + z * z / (2 * total)) / denominator
    47	    margin = z * math.sqrt((p * (1 - p) + z * z / (4 * total)) / total) / denominator
    48	    return [center - margin, center + margin]
    49	
    50	
    51	def group(paths: list[Path]) -> dict[str, dict[str, dict[str, Any]]]:
    52	    grouped: dict[str, dict[str, dict[str, Any]]] = defaultdict(dict)
    53	    for path in paths:
    54	        part = rows(path)
    55	        if not part:
    56	            raise ValueError(f"empty candidate file: {path}")
    57	        model_id = str(part[0].get("model_id", path.stem))
    58	        for row in part:
    59	            row_id = str(row["id"])
    60	            if row_id in grouped[model_id]:
    61	                raise ValueError(f"duplicate candidate id {row_id!r} for {model_id}")
    62	            grouped[model_id][row_id] = row
    63	    return grouped
    64	
    65	
    66	def main() -> None:
    67	    args = parse_args()
    68	    positive = {str(row["id"]): row for row in rows(args.positive_corpus)}
    69	    traps = {str(row["id"]): row for row in rows(args.trap_corpus)}
    70	    positive_groups = group(args.positive_candidates)
    71	    trap_groups = group(args.trap_candidates)
    72	    if set(positive_groups) != set(trap_groups):
    73	        raise ValueError("positive and trap candidate model sets differ")
    74	
    75	    report: dict[str, Any] = {
    76	        "positive_corpus": str(args.positive_corpus),
    77	        "trap_corpus": str(args.trap_corpus),
    78	        "models": {},
    79	    }
    80	    for model_id in sorted(positive_groups):
    81	        positive_rows = positive_groups[model_id]
    82	        trap_rows = trap_groups[model_id]
    83	        if set(positive_rows) != set(positive):
    84	            raise ValueError(f"{model_id}: positive IDs do not match corpus")
    85	        if set(trap_rows) != set(traps):
    86	            raise ValueError(f"{model_id}: trap IDs do not match corpus")
    87	
    88	        positive_details = []
    89	        for row_id, case in positive.items():
    90	            count = list_lines(output_text(positive_rows[row_id]))
    91	            intended = int(case["item_count"])
    92	            positive_details.append(
    93	                {
    94	                    "id": row_id,
    95	                    "list_lines": count,
    96	                    "intended_items": intended,
    97	                    "activated": count >= 2,
    98	                    "intended_count": count == intended,
    99	                }
   100	            )
   101	        trap_details = []
   102	        for row_id in traps:
   103	            count = list_lines(output_text(trap_rows[row_id]))
   104	            trap_details.append({"id": row_id, "list_lines": count, "false_list": count >= 2})
   105	
   106	        activated = sum(item["activated"] for item in positive_details)
   107	        intended_count = sum(item["intended_count"] for item in positive_details)
   108	        false_lists = sum(item["false_list"] for item in trap_details)
   109	        report["models"][model_id] = {
   110	            "positive_total": len(positive_details),
   111	            "activated": activated,
   112	            "activated_wilson_95": wilson(activated, len(positive_details)),
   113	            "intended_count": intended_count,
   114	            "intended_count_wilson_95": wilson(intended_count, len(positive_details)),
   115	            "trap_total": len(trap_details),
   116	            "false_lists": false_lists,
   117	            "restraint": len(trap_details) - false_lists,
   118	            "restraint_wilson_95": wilson(len(trap_details) - false_lists, len(trap_details)),
   119	            "positive_mismatches": [item for item in positive_details if not item["intended_count"]],
   120	            "false_list_cases": [item for item in trap_details if item["false_list"]],
   121	        }
   122	
   123	    args.out.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   124	    args.out.write_text(json.dumps(report, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
   125	    print(

   130	def model_family(config: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
   131	    model_type = str(config.get("model_type", "")).casefold().replace("_", "")
   132	    architectures = " ".join(str(item) for item in config.get("architectures", []))
   133	    if "qwen35" in model_type or "qwen3_5" in architectures.casefold():
   134	        return QWEN35_FAMILY
   135	    if "gemma" in model_type or "gemma" in architectures.casefold():
   136	        return "gemma"
   137	    return "other"

   140	def resolve_training_mode(family: str, requested: str) -> str:
   141	    resolved = BF16_LORA_MODE if family == QWEN35_FAMILY else QLORA_MODE
   142	    if requested != "auto":
   143	        resolved = requested
   144	    if family == QWEN35_FAMILY and resolved == QLORA_MODE:
   145	        raise ValueError(
   146	            "Refusing Qwen3.5 QLoRA: Unsloth recommends BF16 LoRA because its "
   147	            "4-bit quantization differences are higher than normal"
   148	        )
   149	    if family != QWEN35_FAMILY and resolved == BF16_LORA_MODE:
   150	        raise ValueError("BF16 LoRA is currently implemented only for Qwen3.5")
   151	    return resolved

   785	def scan(*, contract: dict[str, Any], benchmark_rows: Sequence[dict[str, Any]], sources: dict[tuple[str, str], Path], backend_name: str, embedding_backend: EmbeddingBackend, source_bytes: dict[tuple[str, str], bytes] | None = None) -> tuple[list[dict[str, Any]], str]:
   786	    candidate_inputs, candidate_outputs = text_sides(benchmark_rows, "benchmark")
   787	    candidate_texts = candidate_inputs + candidate_outputs
   788	    if not candidate_texts:
   789	        raise ValueError("benchmark has no scannable text")
   790	    results = []
   791	    synthetic_thresholds = contract["synthetic_test_only_thresholds"]
   792	    for (role, name), path in sorted(sources.items()):
   793	        value = source_bytes[(role, name)] if source_bytes is not None else path.read_bytes()
   794	        rows = read_records_bytes(value, f"{role}:{name}")
   795	        texts = extract_texts(rows)
   796	        source = {"role": role, "name": name, "sha256": sha256_bytes(value), "record_count": len(rows), "authenticated_raw_text_count": len(texts)}
   797	        methods: dict[str, Any] = {}
   798	        if role in REGISTRY_ROLES:
   799	            if texts:
   800	                raise ValueError(f"{role}:{name} registry unexpectedly contains raw text")
   801	            registry_values = {str(value) for row in rows for value in row.values() if isinstance(value, str)}
   802	            if role == "blocked_text_hash_registry":
   803	                candidate_values = {
   804	                    sha256_bytes(v2.normalize_text(value).encode("utf-8"))
   805	                    for value in candidate_texts
   806	                }
   807	            else:
   808	                candidate_values = {
   809	                    str(row["semantic_family_id"])
   810	                    for row in benchmark_rows
   811	                    if isinstance(row.get("semantic_family_id"), str)
   812	                }
   813	            matches = candidate_values & registry_values
   814	            methods["exact_normalized"] = {"status": "passed" if not matches else "failed", "matches": len(matches)}
   815	            for method in METHODS[1:]:
   816	                methods[method] = {"status": "not_applicable_no_raw_text", "authenticated_raw_text_count": 0}
   817	        elif role in RAW_ROLES:
   818	            if not texts:
   819	                raise ValueError(f"{role}:{name} has no authenticated raw text")
   820	            normalized_candidates = [v2.normalize_text(value) for value in candidate_texts]
   821	            normalized_sources = [v2.normalize_text(value) for value in texts]
   822	            exact = set(normalized_candidates) & set(normalized_sources)
   823	            exact_violations = sum(left == right for left in normalized_candidates for right in normalized_sources)
   824	            thresholds = synthetic_thresholds if backend_name == "synthetic_test_only" else {method: None for method in METHODS[1:]}
   825	            source_inputs, source_outputs = text_sides(rows, role)
   826	            embedding_axes = {
   827	                "input_input": cosine_stats(candidate_inputs, source_inputs, embedding_backend, thresholds["embedding_cosine"]),
   828	                "output_output": cosine_stats(candidate_outputs, source_outputs, embedding_backend, thresholds["embedding_cosine"]),
   829	                "input_output": cosine_stats(candidate_inputs, source_outputs, embedding_backend, thresholds["embedding_cosine"]),
   830	                "output_input": cosine_stats(candidate_outputs, source_inputs, embedding_backend, thresholds["embedding_cosine"]),
   831	            }
   832	            embedding_axis_counts = {
   833	                "input_input": len(candidate_inputs) * len(source_inputs),
   834	                "output_output": len(candidate_outputs) * len(source_outputs),
   835	                "input_output": len(candidate_inputs) * len(source_outputs),
   836	                "output_input": len(candidate_outputs) * len(source_inputs),
   837	            }
   838	            scores = {
   839	                "token_ngram_jaccard": pairwise_jaccard_stats(candidate_texts, texts, width=contract["token_ngram_width"], characters=False, threshold=thresholds["token_ngram_jaccard"]),
   840	                "character_ngram_jaccard": pairwise_jaccard_stats(candidate_texts, texts, width=contract["character_ngram_width"], characters=True, threshold=thresholds["character_ngram_jaccard"]),
   841	                "embedding_cosine": (max(value[0] for value in embedding_axes.values()), None if backend_name == "production" else sum(value[1] or 0 for value in embedding_axes.values())),
   842	            }
   843	            comparisons = len(candidate_texts) * len(texts)
   844	            methods["exact_normalized"] = {"status": "passed" if not exact else "failed", "matches": len(exact), "comparison_count": comparisons, "violation_count": exact_violations}
   845	            for method, (score, violation_count) in scores.items():
   846	                if backend_name == "production":
   847	                    status, threshold = "calibration_required", None
   848	                else:
   849	                    threshold = synthetic_thresholds[method]
   850	                    status = threshold_status(score, threshold)
   851	                methods[method] = {"status": status, "maximum_score": round(score, 8), "threshold": threshold, "comparison_count": comparisons, "violation_count": violation_count}
   852	                if method == "embedding_cosine":
   853	                    methods[method]["comparison_count"] = len(candidate_inputs) * len(source_inputs) + len(candidate_outputs) * len(source_outputs) + len(candidate_inputs) * len(source_outputs) + len(candidate_outputs) * len(source_inputs)
   854	                    methods[method]["axes"] = {axis: {"maximum_score": round(values[0], 8), "violation_count": values[1], "comparison_count": embedding_axis_counts[axis]} for axis, values in embedding_axes.items()}
   855	        else:
   856	            raise ValueError(f"unsupported leakage role: {role}")
   857	        results.append({"source": source, "methods": methods})
   858	    all_exact = all(row["methods"]["exact_normalized"]["status"] == "passed" for row in results)
   859	    if backend_name == "production":
   860	        overall = "calibration_required_noncertifying" if all_exact else "failed"
   861	    else:
   862	        statuses = [method["status"] for row in results for method in row["methods"].values()]
   863	        overall = "synthetic_not_quality_evidence" if all(value in {"passed", "not_applicable_no_raw_text"} for value in statuses) else "failed"
   864	    return results, overall

   867	def build_expected_receipt(*, backend_name: str, status: str, benchmark_bytes: bytes, benchmark_rows: Sequence[dict[str, Any]], contract_bytes: bytes, inventory_bytes: bytes, blocked_receipt_bytes: bytes, controls: dict[str, str], expected_head: str, tree: dict[str, Any] | None, runtime_versions: dict[str, str], results: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> dict[str, Any]:
   868	    return {
   869	        "schema_version": RECEIPT_SCHEMA_VERSION,
   870	        "status": status,
   871	        "backend": backend_name,
   872	        "benchmark_sha256": sha256_bytes(benchmark_bytes),
   873	        "benchmark_content_sha256": v2.benchmark_content_sha256(benchmark_rows),
   874	        "contract_sha256": sha256_bytes(contract_bytes),
   875	        "source_inventory_sha256": sha256_bytes(inventory_bytes),
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Score model-blind English list development cases deterministically.
     3	
     4	This scorer checks only audited spans and visible list structure. It does not
     5	provide semantic, native-speaker, frozen-benchmark, or release-quality proof.
     6	"""
     7	
     8	from __future__ import annotations
     9	
    10	import argparse
    11	import hashlib
    12	import json
   115	        "rate": successes / total if total else None,
   116	        "wilson_95": wilson(successes, total),
   117	    }
   118	
   119	
   120	def load_corpus(path: Path, role: str) -> dict[str, dict[str, Any]]:
   121	    cases: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {}
   122	    for line_number, case in enumerate(read_jsonl(path), 1):
   123	        case_id = case.get("id")
   124	        if not isinstance(case_id, str) or not case_id:
   125	            raise ValueError(f"{path}:{line_number}: missing id")
   126	        if case_id in cases:
   127	            raise ValueError(f"{path}:{line_number}: duplicate corpus id {case_id!r}")
   128	        if (
   129	            case.get("split") != "dev"
   130	            or case.get("gold_status") != "candidate_unreviewed"
   131	            or case.get("native_reviewed") is not False
   132	            or case.get("training_eligible") is not False
   133	        ):
   134	            raise ValueError(f"{case_id}: corpus is not development/unreviewed/non-training data")
   135	        if case.get("benchmark_role") != role:
   136	            raise ValueError(f"{case_id}: expected benchmark_role {role!r}")
   137	        for field in ("domain", "case_type", "length_bucket"):
   138	            if not isinstance(case.get(field), str) or not case[field]:
   139	                raise ValueError(f"{case_id}: invalid {field}")
   140	        if not isinstance(case.get("item_count"), int) or case["item_count"] < 1:
   141	            raise ValueError(f"{case_id}: invalid item_count")
   142	        if not isinstance(case.get("compound_required"), bool):
   143	            raise ValueError(f"{case_id}: invalid compound_required")
   144	        for field in ("items", "compound_items", "scope_anchors", "forbidden"):
   145	            if not isinstance(case.get(field), list) or not all(
   146	                isinstance(value, str) and value.strip() for value in case[field]
   147	            ):
   148	                raise ValueError(f"{case_id}: invalid {field}")
   149	        if len(case["items"]) != case["item_count"] or not case["scope_anchors"]:
   150	            raise ValueError(f"{case_id}: audited item/scope coverage is invalid")
   151	        normalized_items = {normalized(value) for value in case["items"]}
   152	        normalized_compounds = {normalized(value) for value in case["compound_items"]}
   153	        if (
   154	            len(normalized_items) != case["item_count"]
   155	            or not normalized_compounds.issubset(normalized_items)
   156	            or case["compound_required"] != bool(case["compound_items"])
   157	        ):
   158	            raise ValueError(f"{case_id}: audited compound/item contract is invalid")
   159	        expected_format = case.get("expected_formatting")
   160	        allowed = {"bullets", "numbered"} if role == "positive_list" else {"prose"}
   161	        if expected_format not in allowed:
   162	            raise ValueError(f"{case_id}: invalid expected_formatting {expected_format!r}")
   163	        cases[case_id] = case
   164	    return cases
   165	
   166	
   167	def candidate_text(row: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
   168	    value = row.get("output", row.get("candidate"))
   169	    return value.strip() if isinstance(value, str) else ""

   178	
   179	- The visible 5a/D2 shipped wins were: one way to open a mic, capture running in one process, recording states reduced from 14 to 5, immediate pill when warm, caching only for a true cold model load, and "No audio captured" only when the microphone actually delivers no audio. [Task 1]
   180	- The real Bluetooth proof that survived this window was strong enough to move on: an AirPods hands-free dictation captured about 8.6 seconds, polished correctly, and completed in under a second; the remaining live problem was double-tap sometimes resolving as triple-tap cancel. [Task 1]
   181	- The 5b contract that kept reappearing was subtraction-first: after a take comes back with no sound, retire that source so the next press opens fresh connection; keep the warm-mic feature, do not add quiet-room detectors, and fence the throw-away action to the right take with existing `currentCaptureSessionID` / capture-session-counter behavior and `zeroSignalDeviceEligible`. [Task 2]
   182	- The TIK redesign direction on July 15 was to shrink prompt state and push more decision-making into deterministic Python per issue. The concrete must-fix list included missing CLI dispatch, a timestamp shortcut that could suppress a different bad event, closure-cycle evidence reuse, nondeterministic fingerprint formatting, Step H not doing per-event inspection yet, v4/v5 rollback not being automatically safe, and a deployment-bootstrap gap. [Task 3]
   183	- The same TIK lane later exposed the safe live-update pattern for the Routine prompt/config: inspect the working `/v1/code/triggers/...` path, compare live content against `workers/sentry-triage/routine-prompt-tik-sentry.md` with `git show` / `wc -c`, and use the app/UI save flow rather than hand-crafting an update API call. [Task 3]
   184	- The durable EG-1 baseline was: treat `93.7%` as an English-heavy harness score, note that list creation on true list cases was `73%`, run the exact Mac shipping path, and classify outputs as safe-and-improved, safe-but-not-improved, or damaging before retraining. PostHog country was only a proxy because `language`, `asr_language`, and `transcription_language` fields were blank in the visible production dataset. [Task 4]
   185	- The multilingual evaluation ladder that held up here was: build list cue-family discovery sets, start with Russian/Dutch sentinel gaps plus a German release-grade benchmark, freeze the release split before prompt/data/model experiments, and prefer one shared offline multilingual model if possible. The visible fallback path was shared-base plus language adapters, with Qwen3.5-4B looking strongest, Gemma E4B likely too large for the ~2.9 GB target, and Ministral 3B / Phi-4 Mini kept as compact controls. [Task 4]
   186	- Later July 15 EG-1 work added two high-leverage proof rules: non-semantic corpus-hash drift can come from bundle-path renames even when IDs/inputs/golds/scores are identical, and a bad emergency scrub that collapses unrelated `main` history should trigger a clean replay of only the overnight commits onto current `origin/main`, with the private bundle kept ignored and verified absent from every rebuilt commit. [Task 4]
   187	- The July 16 founder readout turned the overnight lane into a trust/accounting problem, not just a modeling problem: the visible artifact set was huge (`70 commits`, `441 files`, `86,003` added lines, `336` receipts/docs), but no release winner or statistically valid five-language ranking existed yet. The honest summary was that time went into invalidating bad benchmarks, proving tests trustworthy, rejecting prompt-only list tuning, and exploring model viability, rather than shipping a better EG-1 candidate. [Task 4]
   188	- The list-format diagnosis became much sharper on July 16: the current shipped EG-1 was `Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507` plus rank-16 LoRA, trained mostly on English, with only `259` list outputs and just `3` two-item lists in training. That made two-item list reliability a data-shape problem first; prompt-only tuning raised false lists and meaning damage, so the next credible move is better frozen multilingual data plus trained candidates, not more prompt tweaks. [Task 4]
   189	- The PR #1555 telemetry hardening rule was to tag a dead-mic watch with the arming capture session and only let a different later session resolve it. Without `armedSessionID` / `capturedCaptureSessionID`, a speech-then-silence take could arm and self-complete its own watch, inflating recovery. [Task 5]
   190	- The shipped July 15 state after PR #1555 was: the dead-Bluetooth zero-signal retirement plus measurement landed, a normal real-AirPods dictation proved the no-false-alarm path, `#1520` stayed open for the separate cold-first-press/cold-link problem, and the planning notes needed to move from “5b next” to “6a next.” [Task 5]

   192	## Failures and how to do differently
   193	
   194	- Symptom: a review loop drifts toward abstract council opinions and away from the real defect set. Cause: non-grounded review is treated like evidence. Fix: reopen the real files and let file/line grounded review drive plan changes. [Task 3]
   195	- Symptom: the 5b fix starts growing into message, analytics, or dead-mic-versus-quiet-room inference work. Cause: the plan stops being about the concrete zero-sound receipt. Fix: keep the scope on the plain silence fact, the existing capture-session identity, and zero-signal-source retirement only. [Task 2]
   196	- Symptom: multilingual confidence gets overstated from one headline score or a geography proxy. Cause: the harness is English-heavy and production language fields are blank. Fix: build per-language baselines and exact-runtime tests before retraining or shipping claims. [Task 4]
   197	- Symptom: a privacy scrub or corpus refresh makes the EG-1 branch look wildly different from what actually changed. Cause: private-bundle history or renamed provenance paths contaminate the audit. Fix: compare semantic fields before assuming corpus drift, keep private snapshots ignored, and rebuild the public branch cleanly on current `origin/main` when scrub history damages ancestry. [Task 4]
   198	- Symptom: overnight/autopilot research loses experiment history after compaction. Cause: tests and numbers live only in chat. Fix: keep docs/scripts/log artifacts with the exact metrics and commands tried. [Task 4]
   199	- Symptom: a large overnight EG-1 push burns usage/time but still feels like "little visible product progress." Cause: the lane spread across benchmark invalidation, safety proofs, model scouting, and infrastructure before producing a ranked shipping candidate. Fix: present the chronology plainly, separate trust-building work from actual model-improvement work, and stop pretending the artifact volume equals product progress. [Task 4]
   200	- Symptom: dead-mic telemetry says recovery happened even though only the original bad take completed. Cause: the watch resolves off the same take that armed it. Fix: bind the watch to the arming session ID and require a later session to clear it. [Task 5]
   201	- Symptom: the shipped 5b fix gets blamed for the first cold Bluetooth press not showing a pill. Cause: post-recording dead-mic retirement and press-to-pill cold-start readiness are different problems. Fix: keep `#1520` open as the separate cold-link/cold-first-press lane and do not fold it into the already-shipped 5b scope. [Task 5]

    28	## RULE: eg1-hot-swap-contract
    29	Rule: Shipping EG-1 v2 / EG-2 = upload the new artifact set + license to R2; update `eg1-delivery-manifest.json` with the new revision, per-shard file paths + measured `sizeBytes`/`sha256`, `totalBytes`, and `admission.layout`/`entrypointFile` (this manifest owns hashes/sizes/layout); then update `eg1-manifest.json`'s runtime config to match (`version`, `contextTokens`/`promptTemplateID`/`minAppVersion`/`licenseURL`) — `eg1-manifest.json` no longer carries `sha256`/`sizeBytes` (dead fields removed #1417, see FACT: eg1-artifact-identity). Keep `modelName`/`promptTemplateID` unless the PROMPT changed; bump `minAppVersion` if runtime behavior requires it. Zero code changes unless the prompt template changes (then: new `promptTemplateID` + new `PromptFamily` case + builder; unknown template IDs read RED "app_update_required" in old apps by design).
    30	Why: `EGOneRuntime` refuses manifests whose `modelName != "eg-1"` (fixed Core literal `LLMProvider.egOneModelName`); `refreshInstalledState` purges the previous version's artifact family automatically (extension-scoped).
    31	How to apply: R2 upload runbook below; validate the new host object with HEAD (etag+length), a Range request (expect 206), and a full curl+shasum before updating the manifest.
    32	Ref: #1271 plan §hot-swap; purge added Codex r11.
    33	
    34	## FACT: kill-switch-stops-mutation-not-format-drift (#1386 PR-1, 2026-07-11)
    35	`modelDelivery.egOne.enabled=false` guarantees exactly ONE thing: no EG-1 model bytes are moved, deleted, or fetched. It does NOT guarantee polish keeps working, and for one cohort it structurally cannot.
    36	
    37	A user still on the previously-shipped MONOLITH (`eg-1-v1.gguf`, every release ≤ v2.3.2) holds bytes a sharded build cannot load at all: `EGOneRuntime` boots `manifest.resolvedEntrypointPath` (shard 1), so no directory choice makes a monolith loadable. Turning the switch off therefore leaves that user with raw text (EG-1 is a limb) and their model completely untouched; turning it back on runs the migration and polish returns. Nothing is lost, only deferred.
    38	
    39	**Do NOT "fix" this by restoring the monolithic load path.** That means shipping the old manifest AND the old load path alongside the new ones, permanently — dual-format support carried forever for a rollback lever. Rejected on scope (founder 2026-07-11: don't over-engineer). Codex PR-1 review r14 raised it as DIAGNOSIS-ONLY; the resolution is this documented limitation, not code.
    40	
    41	Corollary for any FUTURE model-format change (EG-2, a new quantization): the kill switch is a byte-mutation stop, not a version rollback. If a format change ever needs a true rollback, that is a deliberate design (ship both manifests), not something the flag gives you for free.
    42	
    43	## FACT: eg1-monolith-retirement-and-owned-home (#1386 PR-1, PR #1500, 2026-07-11)
    44	Shipped delivery shape since PR #1500: EG-1 installs to `~/Library/Application Support/EnviousWispr/Models/eg-1` (owned home; old `PolishModels` is the RETIRED store). At launch `EGOneLegacyUpgradeCoordinator` retires the one shipped monolith (`eg-1-v1.gguf`, 2_889_511_680 bytes, sha256 `3343fc1a...74f6`) by exact size+digest proof: containment gate (resolved-to-resolved, firmlink-safe) → durable `eg1-v1-replacement-owed` marker in `ModelDelivery/` → delete → normal ModelDeliveryController download. Marker clears ONLY on admission or explicit Cancel/Remove (decline counts even kill-switched — contract §5c.10). Post-replacement the RUNTIME decides activation via `activateAfterAutomaticReplacementIfNeeded()` (live `isActiveProvider`; the install-state stream never activates). Telemetry: `eg1.legacy_detected` (+`selected_provider`) / `legacy_retired` / `legacy_retirement_failed(reason: marker_write|delete|unreadable|containment)` / `replacement_completed` / `replacement_declined`; release ship-criterion: every detected distinct_id eventually shows completed/declined/diagnosable-failed. UAT fixture: genuine monolith kept at `~/Developer/EnviousLabs/eg1-uat-fixture/eg-1-v1.gguf` (digest-verified; monolith still hosted at models.enviouslabs.co/eg1/eg-1-v1-q5km.gguf). Contract authority: `docs/model-delivery/model-delivery-contract.md` v1.3 §5c (delete-first ONLY for cryptographically-identified app-owned assets the runtime cannot load — does NOT authorize delete-first for working files, e.g. WhisperKit). Plan+RCA: `docs/feature-requests/issue-1386-2026-07-11-eg1-retire-monolith-rev2.md`, `docs/postmortems/2026-07-11-1386-pr1-defect-cascade.md`.

    46	## FACT: r2-hosting-runbook
    47	Bucket `enviouslabs-models` (DEDICATED — but account-level R2 is shared across projects: [infra/cloudflare.md](file:///Users/m4pro_sv/.claude/knowledge/infra/cloudflare.md) RULE r2-shared-across-projects still applies). Custom domain `models.enviouslabs.co` (zone `370565c81fa22c65cd2322e9c94991f0`, minTLS 1.2, bound 2026-07-02, ~10 min provisioning; interim `error code: 1014` is normal). Cost ~$0.05/mo; egress free.
    48	Upload = mint-use-revoke bucket-scoped token (permission groups `2efd5506f9c8494dacb1fa10a3e7d5b6` write + `6a018a9f2fc74eb6b293b0c548f38b39` read, resource `com.cloudflare.edge.r2.bucket.${ACCT}_default_enviouslabs-models`); S3 creds: access_key=token_id, secret=sha256(token_value); token needs ~10 s propagation; REVOKE after. NEVER print token values.
    49	HF mirror: OFF by founder decision (competitor-lift fear); do not publish without explicit approval.

    51	## FACT: eg1-shard-authoring-runbook (#1417, 2026-07-08)
    52	Splitting EG-1 into CDN-cache-eligible shards (each safely under Cloudflare's 512MB ceiling, target ≤450MB per the Phase-3 warn line) reuses `llama.cpp`'s native `gguf-split` tool and the shared delivery engine's existing `componentSet` layout (already proven by Parakeet's 23-file case) — no new reassembly code, no changes to `ManifestFetchTask`/`CacheAdmission`. One-time steps when cutting a new sharded EG-1 revision:
    53	1. **Split:** run `gguf-split` against the real (unsplit) model weights, choosing `--split-max-tensors` so every output shard lands comfortably under 450MB (shards are NOT byte-range slices — each is an independently-headered valid GGUF file with its own metadata, so per-shard size varies with tensor boundaries, never assume equal division).
    54	2. **Measure, never assume:** compute each shard's real `sizeBytes`/`sha256` from the actual output files — the manifest's `totalBytes` must equal the measured sum, and every `sizeBytes` must be re-verified ≤450MB after the real split (the pre-split total does not divide evenly by shard count).
    55	3. **Upload:** each shard to R2 (`r2-hosting-runbook` above) under a NEW versioned sub-path (e.g. `/eg1/v2-sharded/`) — never reuse the legacy single-file path, so the new Cloudflare Cache Rule (scoped to the new sub-path only) can never accidentally match the old monolithic file, which is over the cache-eligible size ceiling.
    56	4. **Author the manifest:** `admission.layout: "componentSet"`, `admission.entrypointFile` naming the shard `DeliveryManifest.resolvedEntrypointPath`/`EGOneDeliveryAdapter.installedArtifactURL` should boot from (shard 1, by `llama.cpp` convention — it auto-discovers siblings by filename pattern once pointed at shard 1), one `files[]` entry per shard with real measured hash/size.
    57	5. **Validate before shipping:** every `sizeBytes` ≤450,000,000; `totalBytes` == sum of `files[].sizeBytes`; `entrypointFile` resolves to exactly one `resolvedInstallPath` (enforced by `DeliveryManifest.validateStructure()`, throws otherwise); no duplicate `installPath` values (already enforced); every shard file on disk matches its declared `sha256` before upload.
    58	6. **Cloudflare Cache Rule + Transform Rule:** scope to `starts_with(http.request.uri.path, "/eg1/v2-sharded/")` (or whatever the new sub-path is) — same shape as Parakeet's rule (edge TTL 1y, `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable, no-transform`). This is a production infra mutation, executed with explicit founder go, separate from the code/manifest change.
    59	Existing EG-1 installs on the OLD single-file manifest are swept as stale download sidecars by `EGOneDeliveryAdapter.migrateLegacyStoreArtifacts()`'s version-agnostic cleanup and re-download once under the new manifest — the old file stays usable throughout (deleted only after the new N-shard set fully verifies and promotes, via `CacheAdmission`'s existing promote-time orphan cleanup). Full plan: `docs/feature-requests/issue-1417-2026-07-08-eg1-cdn-sharding.md`.
    60	
    61	## FACT: eg1-engine-build
    62	Committed binary `Sources/EnviousWispr/Resources/llama-server` (15 MB static arm64, Metal embedded, system frameworks only). Full pinned recipe + traps in `Sources/EnviousWispr/Resources/llama-server-PROVENANCE.md` (commit `fdb1db87`, ggml-org/llama.cpp). The three traps that WILL recur on rebuild: default build produces minos 26.0 (MUST set `-DCMAKE_OSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=14.0` and verify via `otool -l`); rogue `~/.local/bin/cc` shim breaks cmake detection (use `-DCMAKE_C_COMPILER=/usr/bin/clang`); default links Homebrew OpenSSL (`-DLLAMA_OPENSSL=OFF`). Signing: `build-release-dmg.sh` step [5.5/6] signs the bare Mach-O before the app seal; `build-dev-app.sh` step 7 asserts presence. MIT notice ships via `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` (`scripts/ci/gen-third-party-notices.sh` — `repo:` path scheme, license text vendored at `Resources/llama-server-LICENSE.txt`).
    63	
    64	## FACT: eg1-runtime-config
    65	Launch flags (in `EGOneRuntime.startServerIfInstalled`): `-c 16384 -fa on --cache-type-k q8_0 --cache-type-v q8_0`, app-chosen bind-probed port, 127.0.0.1 only, per-launch `--api-key` bearer token. Measured (M4 Pro, 2026-07-02): RSS 4.1 GB (naive 32768/fp16 config was 7.4 GB — the MacBook-friendly footprint is a FLAG choice, not a model choice; matches `EGOneRuntime.swift` + `llama-server-PROVENANCE.md`), cold start ~8-9 s, warm restart ~1 s, warm inference ~0.2 s.

    67	## FACT: eg1-unload-matrix
    68	All exits verified live 2026-07-02 (kill drills + AX switch drill):
    69	| Event | Behavior |
    70	|---|---|
    71	| Provider switch away | server killed, RAM freed (defers while a recording froze `.egOne`; terminal-state retry) |
    72	| App quit | synchronous kill via `OSAllocatedUnfairLock` mirror (`applicationWillTerminate`) |
    73	| App crash orphan | pkill sweep (anchored regex, exact binary path) — in-spawn + actor-gated `reapOrphansIfIdle` on every no-spawn launch path |
    74	| Crash once | restart-once (~1 s warm) |
    75	| Crash twice | stays down; recovery = settings-open probe or provider re-pick or relaunch |
    76	| Critical memory pressure | killed even while starting |
    77	| Dictation while dead | silent raw paste (~0.5 s; `local_polish_not_ready`) — verified live |

    79	## FACT: eg1-quality-perf-baselines
    80	Quality: 92.4% behavior / 0 critical on hard-340 at exact ship config (native; same artifact via Ollama scored 89.7%); 94.7% on full 1,890 (the number quoted in settings UI copy). Judge: sonnet-5 `behavior_judge.py --system new`. **ACCEPTED NUMBER SPLIT (founder decision 2026-07-05, do NOT re-raise):** in-app settings/release copy keeps **94.7%** (original internal full-1,890 run); the public README + r/macapps post use **93.7%** from the later reproducible `scripts/eval/runs/type-b-1199/eg1_native_shipped_1890/scores/summary.json` (`pass_rate_pct`, same `--system new` judge). This ~1pp split is a deliberate accepted state, not an open reconcile item — do not silently change either number (session-log 2026-07-05). Perf (live, M4 Pro): typical dictations 0.3-0.9 s polish, e2e 0.8-1.3 s Parakeet / 1.9-2.2 s WhisperKit; 1,817 chars in 8.0 s (~230 chars/s throughput). Re-benchmark recipe: `scripts/eval/subset_polish_runner.py --endpoint` against a locally-launched server with the SHIPPING flags (never via Ollama — different runtime, different numbers; see [ollama-operations.md](ollama-operations.md)).
    81	**Shipped-config topic_shift regression (2026-07-05, NOT root-caused):** on the Type B 1,890 exam, `topic_shift` (paragraph breaks) scored 97% via the tuning harness but 78% through the shipped engine (−19pp), driving nearly the whole 94.4→93.7 gap (e.g. TS-026/TS-028 emit zero `\n\n` where the tuning outputs broke between topics). Same Q5_K_M model both runs, so the suspect is the SHIPPING RUNTIME config — most likely the q8_0 KV-cache quantization (the 4.1 GB-footprint choice, FACT: eg1-runtime-config) degrading long-range structuring. Clean test = re-run with fp16 KV cache (`--cache-type-k f16 --cache-type-v f16`) and see if topic_shift recovers. Possible customer-facing product issue (worse paragraph breaks than the tuned model can deliver); founder to decide whether to chase. Benchmark methodology owner: [polish-eval.md](polish-eval.md).
    82	
    83	## FACT: eg1-list-formatting-trigger
    84	EG-1 builds a real **bullet list** from a lead-in phrase followed by a natural run of items with **no spoken ordinals** ("the team needs to confirm backups, feature flags, and the traffic drain" → lead-in `:` then one `- item` per line). Spoken ordinals ("first do X, second do Y, third Z") stay INLINE as sentences — the model reads them as sequential steps, not a list. Verified: `scripts/eval/corpus/list_format_v1.jsonl` + the shipped `type-b-1199/eg1_native_shipped_1890` run passed 74/100 list cases with real bullet output on exactly the lead-in-plus-run pattern; a founder demo dictation using "first/second/third" produced inline prose twice (2026-07-05). Implication for demos/marketing: to SHOW EG-1 building a list, script a lead-in + comma-run, never ordinals. Paragraph structuring + self-correction + filler removal work regardless of phrasing. A true marker list is an EG-1 / cloud behavior, NOT an Apple Intelligence one (AFM list detection is run-to-run inconsistent, confirmed live).
    85	
     1	<!-- tier: 2 -->
     2	# Model Delivery (epic #1348)
     3	
     4	**when:** ANY work touching how model bytes reach a user's machine — Parakeet/WhisperKit/EG-1 downloads, the models.enviouslabs.co mirror, download telemetry, cache validation/repair, first-run model UX, or planning any #1348 phase.
     5	**why:** the #1339 launch-night incident proved the default model path depended on a borrowed downloader (FluidAudio's → Hugging Face) with no failover/verification/cancel; the founder-ratified pivot is one owned, verified cache-admission layer.
     6	**Authority:** `docs/model-delivery/model-delivery-contract.md` (LIVE contract, currently v1.2). This knowledge file is the stable pointer + invariants summary; the contract wins where they diverge. Phases 1-3 shipped; Phase 4 WhisperKit convergence remains parked. Durable final artifacts are posted to epic #1348 as comments (one per artifact). At epic completion, promote the final contract INTO this file.
     7	
     8	## RULE: model-delivery-contract-is-the-authority
     9	Rule: Before designing, reviewing, or amending ANYTHING in the delivery layer, read `docs/model-delivery/model-delivery-contract.md`; a plan that needs to deviate amends the contract FIRST, with rationale, and posts the amendment to epic #1348.
    10	Why: architecture negotiated through PR comments is the failure mode that cost 17 review rounds on EG-1 (#1271) and 7 on the parked PR-2.
    11	Ref: epic #1348; founder directive 2026-07-06 ("document all of this... in-depth plan phase, large/refactor tier").
    12	
    13	## FACT: the-ten-invariants (contract §1, stable)
    14	1. Verified or absent — no unverified bytes in a final cache path; temp download → SHA-256 BEFORE admission → atomic move.
    15	2. One writer — single-flight per ModelIdentity; readers only after admission.
    16	3. Atomic admission — old valid state or new valid state, never a mix.
    17	4. Manifest-pinned — versioned manifest (files+sizes+SHA-256+source templates); NO runtime listing calls (#1339 failure mode #1).
    18	5. Cooperative cancellation — typed result, never process kill; no partial in final cache; temp resumable or discardable.
    19	6. Resumable where possible — URLSession resume data + Range (mirror serves full Range semantics).
    20	7. Source-aware — our copy (models.enviouslabs.co) first, upstream backup second, per-file failover, order overridable.
    21	8. Telemetry-complete — every attempt speaks the shared 14 emitted failure classes plus the reserved `constrained_network_deferred` class (contract §7); Ollama included at lifecycle granularity.
    22	9. Runtime-agnostic — the layer ENDS at a verified cache location; never loads/compiles models.
    23	10. Deletes old supervision code — net launch-path complexity DOWN per migration PR (deletion ledger is a merge criterion).
    24	
    25	Plus (v1.1): manifest trust root — source selection is mutable; expected identity/hash/size is immutable except via trusted app update (bundled pin is the authority; a drifted mirror can never achieve admission). Disk lifecycle — preflight before fetch, stale-version eviction, disk-full is a first-class recoverable failure; constrained/metered-network policy is a deliberate decision, not a default-by-omission.
    26	
    27	## FACT: scope-guardrails (founder + CTO agreed, binding)
    28	Unify ONLY where WE move bytes: Parakeet, WhisperKit, EG-1. Ollama = shared telemetry only (its daemon owns bytes; `OllamaSetupService` commands it). Sparkle = fully out of scope. No god-layer: one fetcher + thin per-backend adapters. The shared `ModelDeliveryController` + `ManifestFetchTask` + `CacheAdmission` engine (`Sources/EnviousWisprModelDelivery/`) generalized the now-retired `EGOneModelStore`'s mechanisms (resume identity, generation tokens, cancel drain-gate, SHA-before-install, disk preflight, stale purge) from one file to manifest sets.

    33	3. **Phase 3 SHIPPED:** EG-1 converged onto the shared engine (`EGOneDeliveryAdapter`); `eg1.download_*` telemetry retired in favor of `model_delivery.*`.

Plan `docs/feature-requests/issue-1386-2026-07-07-modeldelivery-phase4-whisperkit.md`. Ollama telemetry convergence is part of this remaining scope.

The former `feat/1339-reliable-model-source` branch no longer exists (local + remote) — use the #1339 checkpoint comment only for history.

    39	## FACT: global-edge-caching (#1405, Phase 1 shipped 2026-07-07, Phase 2 shipped 2026-07-08)
    40	Far-from-origin first-run downloads stalled on single-region R2 with no edge caching (distance, not volume). **Phase 1 SHIPPED** (no app update — reaches installed users): the worker 302-redirects known downloads to the object's native cache-enabled R2 `/parakeet/…` path; caching is scoped to `/parakeet/` only (`/eg1/` and worker paths excluded). Canonical config + verification: `~/.claude/knowledge/infra/cloudflare.md` FACT: model-delivery-edge-caching and `workers/parakeet-mirror/`. **Phase 2 SHIPPED** (PR #1410, `8316bff`, closes #1371): bounded same-source retry + restored the load watchdog (`LoadProgressWatcher`) to LOAD-only supervision (parked during `downloadOwnedPhases`); the fetcher's own 60s idle timeout is the sole download-stall authority — net deletion, no new timeout number. Canonical retry/backoff/timeout/stall behavior: [download-resilience-standards.md](download-resilience-standards.md).
    41	
    42	## FACT: install-phase-false-wedge-is-NOT-a-download-problem (#1388, fixed 2026-07-09)
    43	Do not re-diagnose "Setup is taking longer than expected... check your VPN" (or `wedge_detected` with `observed_phase="Installing model..."`) as a download problem. Production forensics (14d): **117 of 126 wedge fires happened during the CoreML INSTALL (compile) phase**, which runs with networking disabled — every one a false positive. Root cause: the guard's post-signal silence gate ("gate B", `silence > max(floor, 5x maxGap)`) assumes homogeneous work units, but the compile emits ~52 ticks ~1.5s apart (small models) then goes silent for one ~445MB encoder taking ~20x longer — the ratio self-calibrates on the wrong distribution and the 15s floor becomes the sole trigger. Worse, the fire's teardown never resumed the pending load continuation (`ASRManagerProxy`'s invalidation handler didn't touch it), so **119/126 fires reached no terminal outcome** — the launch warm-up silently hung, the guard slot leaked, and users got warm only via the separate onboarding warm-up. #1388 fixed the completion contract (resume-once, typed causes: death → `serviceUnreachable` and the adapter's one-shot retry; cancel → `ASRLoadCancelledError`, non-transport so the retry can't resurrect it), REMOVED gate B from the model-load guard (gate A — the 120s no-signal-at-all deadline — survives, and the Phase-2 download parking (`downloadOwnedPhases`/`wasInDownloadPhase` + boundary reset) is RETAINED because it protects gate A too, not just gate B — without it download ticks satisfy the pre-first-signal deadline and a service that hangs before its first LOAD signal is undetectable (Codex r2 P1); only `transferSilenceFloorSeconds` was deleted as genuinely gate-B-only), added a user Cancel during install (`EngineWarmupOutcome.cancelled`, never a failure), corrected the copy, and added un-truncated install telemetry (`install_duration_ms`/`install_silence_max_ms` on `coldstart.warmup_completed`). Success-duration data justified removal over re-tuning: onboarding warm-up p99.9 = 2080s, so any non-false-firing deadline would exceed ~35 minutes — a give-up policy, not a detector. Plan: `docs/feature-requests/issue-1388-2026-07-09-install-phase-false-wedge.md` (5 grounded-review rounds). Fault drill: `A11_asr_kill_mid_model_load`.
    44	
    45	**Still open (#1405): Phase 3** = object-size guardrail (>512MB fail / >450MB warn) + cache-HIT/per-country monitoring in `verify-deploy.py` + DE canary — not started, low priority (safety-net/monitoring, not user-facing). Plan: `docs/feature-requests/issue-1405-2026-07-07-model-delivery-global-cdn.md` (Phase 1/2); Phase 2 root-cause pivot plan: `docs/feature-requests/issue-1405-2026-07-08-downloader-owns-stall.md`. Industry-standard retry/backoff/timeout/stall/resume defaults (cited) + how EW maps to them: [download-resilience-standards.md](download-resilience-standards.md) (read before designing ANY retry/timeout value — copy its §1 consensus numbers, do not invent).
    46	
    47	**EG-1 CDN sharding — delivery SHIPPED #1417 (2026-07-08), edge-cache rule still pending:** the monolithic ~2.9GB EG-1 was ~5.6x over the 512MB free-tier cache ceiling. #1417 split it into eight independently-verified GGUF shards (each ≤450MB) using the shared engine's `componentSet` admission (`Sources/EnviousWispr/Resources/eg1-delivery-manifest.json`, baseURL `/eg1/`); shard 1 is the runtime entrypoint and llama.cpp auto-discovers the siblings. EG-1 already inherits Phase 2 retry/resume via `ManifestFetchTask` (self-heals like Parakeet), and each shard is now SIZE-eligible for free-tier caching — but the Cloudflare Cache Rule covering the shard path is a separate, founder-gated infra mutation NOT yet deployed (cloudflare.md still scopes caching to `/parakeet/`; `/eg1/` excluded), so EG-1 gets no edge-cache HIT benefit yet. Still single-source (no mirror/backup), unlike Parakeet's mirror+HF failover. Canonical artifact/hosting/authoring: [eg1-operations.md](eg1-operations.md) FACT: eg1-artifact-identity + FACT: eg1-shard-authoring-runbook. Design rationale/history: [eg1-cdn-sharding.md](eg1-cdn-sharding.md). Briefing: `docs/model-delivery/2026-07-08-cdn-caching-briefing.md`.

codex
## Verdict: PROCEED-WITH-REVISIONS

The core experiment is sound. There is no fatal reason to change bases or rebuild the benchmark system. The plan needs revisions before training, mainly because:

- GovInfo is not automatically public domain.
- VoxPopuli has upstream conditions beyond CC0.
- FLEURS should remain outside training because it is part of the planned public-speech evaluation lane.
- The current held-out sets are development sets, not a clean final shipping exam.
- The plan overstates what the existing generator, leakage scanner, and trainer have already proven.
- Issue #1570 currently says the 800-case comparison comes before new training.

This is a licensing review, not legal advice.

## 1. Coverage — missing or under-specified

### Finding 1 — The premise claims causation too early

The training skew makes data starvation the best hypothesis. It does not prove that the base-model ceiling is irrelevant.

Drop-in replacement for §1’s final sentence:

> Codex’s overnight scan makes training-data starvation the leading testable hypothesis: list examples are scarce, two-item lists are nearly absent, and prompt-only tuning caused regressions. Retrain #1 tests that hypothesis. It does not yet prove that the Qwen3-4B base has no relevant ceiling.

### Finding 2 — The plan conflicts with the live workflow stage

The current [issue #1570](https://github.com/EnviousLabs/EnviousWispr/issues/1570) says the common 800-case comparison must happen before balanced training data is created. The plan silently changes that order.

Add after the Preface:

> ## Workflow-stage authority  
> Issue #1570 currently requires the common 800-case public-speech comparison before new model training. This English retrain may enter Gate 2 only after either:  
> 1. that comparison is complete; or  
> 2. the founder posts an explicit sequencing override and updates issue #1570’s acceptance criteria.  
> Until one of those happens, this plan may complete sourcing, licensing, and corpus design, but it must not start a full training run or make a ship claim.

### Finding 3 — “Real-web-grounded” overstates data quality

OASST is chat/assistant material, not real dictation. Most AMI meetings are role-played design meetings. Common Voice and FLEURS are read speech. These are useful sources, but none directly represents an English power user dictating emails, notes, and lists.

Add to §3.3:

> **Source realism rule:** call this corpus “public-source-grounded,” not “real-dictation-grounded.” OASST supplies chat-style clean text; AMI supplies mostly scenario-based meeting speech; Common Voice and FLEURS supply read speech. Before acceptance, manually audit a stratified sample of at least 100 generated pairs for ASR-reachable input, realistic dictation phrasing, minimal edits, semantic fidelity, and source-style artifacts. Reject and regenerate any stratum with more than 5% material failures.  
>  
> Preserve the target persona explicitly: at least 100 of the 1,000 additions must be email-shaped and at least 100 must be note-shaped. These rows remain subject to the same list/restraint balance and preservation rules.

### Finding 4 — Data acceptance and privacy gates are missing

The plan needs source versions, hashes, provenance, source-level deduplication, PII removal, copyright filtering, and medical/legal safety.

Add to §3.3:

> **Pair acceptance contract:** every accepted row records source name, exact release/revision, source document ID, source URL, source-text hash, license snapshot hash, generation-model ID, generation-prompt hash, and stratum. Acceptance requires: source-document deduplication; PII and secret screening; removal of quotations, code, song lyrics, and embedded third-party material unless separately cleared; exact preservation of entities, numbers, dates, negation, scope, attribution, and obligations; no newly generated medical or legal advice; and no teacher commentary or formatting-command residue in the gold output. Both input and output sides are screened.

### Finding 5 — Training-disjoint is not the same as decision-clean

The existing 75+75, positive-100/restraint-100, and two-item sets have been inspected during prompt and diagnosis work. They are useful development gates, but repeated inspection makes them unsuitable as the only final shipping exam.

Add to §11:

> **Development versus release evidence:** the existing English sets remain the development and regression gates. Before training starts, freeze one small final English release set from unused source documents and families using the existing generator, scorer, and blind semantic-review flow—no new custody system. Do not inspect candidate outputs on that set until the training recipe and shipping seed are fixed. The final set must include lists, matched restraint, core cleanup, and email/note dictation.

### Finding 6 — The metrics are not fully defined

“Measurably,” “core polish,” “meaning damage,” and seed selection need precise meanings. The existing A/B scorer already defines a useful primary gate: net gain ≥8, exact McNemar p<0.05, zero new restraint false lists, and no increased item/scope loss.

Replace §11 with:

> ## 11. Testing / eval — predeclared go/no-go bar  
> Run the exact shipped prompt, chat template, llama.cpp runtime flags, and quantized Q5_K_M artifact. Record prompt hash, GGUF hash, runtime build, and flags.  
>  
> **Primary endpoint:** on the existing 75-case positive-list A/B set, candidate strict wins minus losses must be at least 8 and two-sided exact McNemar p must be below 0.05.  
>  
> **Mandatory safety gates:**  
> 1. Two-item activation improves over the current 11/20 result, with no candidate-only item, scope, entity, number, timing, negation, attribution, or obligation loss.  
> 2. There are zero candidate-only restraint false lists, total false lists do not exceed baseline, and restraint strict score is at least 98/100.  
> 3. Blind semantic review finds zero candidate-only S3/S4 failures and no increase in S2+ failures.  
> 4. Core-polish non-inferiority means the lower bound of the paired 95% bootstrap confidence interval for candidate minus baseline is above -2 percentage points on the frozen core-polish set.  
> 5. The frozen email/note persona slice has no candidate-only S3/S4 damage and no structural regression.  
>  
> Train seeds 1265, 1266, and 1267. Seed 1265 is the predeclared shipping candidate; the other two test robustness and may not be used to cherry-pick a winner. All three must clear the safety gates. Only seed 1265 is opened on the final release set, once.

### Finding 7 — Telemetry and rollback are understated

No text-content telemetry should be introduced, but a model revision still needs safe operational monitoring. Rollback is not merely “repoint a manifest”: the manifests are bundled trust roots, so rollback requires another trusted app/manifest update.

Replace §12:

> ## 12. Blast radius, telemetry, and rollback  
> The artifact change introduces no transcript or list-content telemetry. Existing non-content telemetry must be segmented by model revision and watched for polish completion, local-polish-not-ready, inference failure, timeout, latency, download/admission failure, and user rollback/removal signals.  
>  
> Rollback keeps the current artifact revision immutable and available. If the new revision fails, ship an emergency trusted manifest/app update pointing to the prior shard set and hashes. Never overwrite model objects in place or reuse paths with changed bytes. The delivery kill switch stops byte mutation; it is not a model-version rollback.

That matches the live hot-swap contract: new versioned artifacts plus bundled manifest hashes are required ([eg1-operations.md:28](/Users/m4pro_sv/Developer/EnviousLabs/EnviousWispr/.claude/knowledge/eg1-operations.md:28), [model-delivery.md:13](/Users/m4pro_sv/Developer/EnviousLabs/EnviousWispr/.claude/knowledge/model-delivery.md:13)).

## 2. License web verification

### Round-#1 sources

| Source | Finding |
|---|---|
| OpenAssistant oasst1/oasst2 | **GREEN with notices.** OASST1 contains the Apache 2.0 license; OASST2 declares `apache-2.0`. Apache permits commercial use and redistribution, including derivative works, but the plan’s “need none” statement is wrong. The license says distributed derivatives must include applicable NOTICE attribution, and the license copy/modified-file notices must be preserved. ([OASST1 license](https://huggingface.co/datasets/OpenAssistant/oasst1/blob/fd7dd3fc13915d6a3bba7e4e854b940b651a4032/LICENSE), [OASST2 card](https://huggingface.co/datasets/OpenAssistant/oasst2)) |
| AMI Meeting Corpus | **GREEN for covered components.** AMI says: “All of the signals and transcription, and some of the annotations” are CC BY 4.0. Commercial use, adaptation, and derivative distribution are allowed with attribution, license link, and change notice. Do not assume every annotation is covered. ([AMI corpus](https://groups.inf.ed.ac.uk/ami/corpus/), [CC BY 4.0 terms](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/deed.en)) |
| VoxPopuli | **GREEN only with a corrected scope.** Its table says “VoxPopuli Data CC0,” but its code and pretrained models are CC BY-NC 4.0. The raw European Parliament source also permits commercial dissemination only with source acknowledgement; partial reproductions must cite the complete item’s URL. The EP acknowledgement is an obligation, not a request. Use only the data/transcripts—not VoxPopuli code, models, or separately licensed LM data. ([VoxPopuli license table](https://github.com/facebookresearch/voxpopuli#license), [European Parliament legal notice](https://www.europarl.europa.eu/legal-notice/en/home)) |
| Common Voice CV11–CV16 | **GREEN, with access/privacy conditions.** CV11 declares `cc0-1.0`; CV16.1 also declares CC0 but is gated and requires agreement not to identify speakers. Mozilla says the dataset is CC0 and asks users not to mirror it outside Mozilla Data Collective. Commercial training and model distribution are allowed, but pin the precise release, accept its access terms, strip speaker IDs/demographics, and do not redistribute the raw corpus. ([CV11](https://huggingface.co/datasets/mozilla-foundation/common_voice_11_0), [CV16.1](https://huggingface.co/datasets/mozilla-foundation/common_voice_16_1), [Mozilla terms](https://commonvoice.mozilla.org/en/terms)) |
| FLEURS | **License GREEN; training use should be RED for this project.** The card says all datasets are CC BY and declares `cc-by-4.0`, permitting commercial adaptation and distribution with attribution. However, FLEURS is part of the public-speech evaluation lane. Training on it would contaminate that comparison. Reserve all FLEURS English material for evaluation. ([FLEURS card](https://huggingface.co/datasets/google/fleurs), [CC BY 4.0 terms](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/deed.en)) |
| GovInfo / federal documents | **YELLOW, not blanket GREEN. Blocking correction.** GovInfo says federal documents are “generally” reusable, but warns that publications may contain copyrighted material and recommends consulting the originating agency. Current §105 also permits copyright in certain covered faculty works and allows the government to hold transferred copyrights. Only individually verified federal-employee works may enter the corpus. ([GovInfo policy](https://www.govinfo.gov/about/policies), [current 17 U.S.C. §105](https://uscode.house.gov/view.xhtml?req=%28title%3A17+section%3A105+edition%3Aprelim%29)) |

### Exclusions

- **Enron/AESLC: defer is correct.** The AESLC card has no stated license grant, and public disclosure through FERC is not a reusable copyright license. Founder approval alone cannot clear third-party email copyrights or privacy concerns. ([AESLC card](https://huggingface.co/datasets/Yale-LILY/aeslc/blob/main/README.md))

- **WikiHow: exclusion is correct, but “do not re-investigate” is too confident.** The official terms page was blocked by robots during this review. The current secondary record says pre-March 24, 2025 text was CC BY-NC-SA 3.0 and newer text is proprietary; CC BY-NC-SA expressly prohibits commercial use. Keep it excluded unless an archived official terms snapshot is obtained. ([current licensing record](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WikiHow), [CC BY-NC-SA legal code](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-nc-sa/3.0/legalcode.en))

- **Stack Exchange: exclusion is reasonable, but the date claim is wrong.** Public contributions remain CC BY-SA according to contribution date: 2.5 before April 2011, 3.0 through May 1, 2018, and 4.0 afterward. There is no “July 2024 content license” cutoff. Its current AUP separately prohibits automated collection for ML training without written consent. Commercial reuse is possible under CC BY-SA, but attribution/share-alike and access-method risk make exclusion sensible. ([licensing help](https://stackoverflow.com/help/licensing), [AUP](https://stackoverflow.com/legal/acceptable-use-policy))

- **TED-LIUM: RED is correct.** TED says its content is BY-NC-ND: non-commercial and non-derivative. That is incompatible with commercial model training without separate permission. ([TED licensing guidance](https://help.ted.com/hc/en-us/articles/360004233294-How-do-I-license-TED-or-TEDx-content))

### Exact replacement for §3.2’s license language

> **Round #1 licensing rule:** pin the exact source release/revision, download URL, file hashes, and license snapshot before ingestion. A source-level allowlist controls acceptance; a dataset name alone is not proof.  
>  
> - **OASST1/OASST2 — GREEN with notice:** Apache-2.0. Carry the license and applicable notices in the training-source notice bundle and model card. Filter quotations, code, PII, and suspected third-party text.  
> - **AMI — GREEN for covered material only:** use transcription and only annotations explicitly covered by CC BY 4.0. Carry creator/source attribution, license link, and modification notice.  
> - **VoxPopuli — GREEN with restrictions:** use only VoxPopuli data/transcripts. Do not use its CC BY-NC code, pretrained models, or separately licensed LM data. Carry European Parliament source attribution and the complete source URL.  
> - **Common Voice CV11–CV16 — GREEN with access conditions:** pin the precise release, accept its repository terms, never attempt speaker identification, strip speaker IDs/demographics, and do not redistribute the raw corpus.  
> - **FLEURS — LICENSE-GREEN BUT TRAINING-EXCLUDED:** reserve all FLEURS rows for the public-speech evaluation lane so the benchmark remains disjoint.  
> - **GovInfo — PER-DOCUMENT YELLOW:** accept only material verified as authored by a federal employee within official duties and containing no third-party, contractor, transferred-copyright, covered-faculty, state/local-government, or separately licensed material. Record the originating agency and verification basis per document.  
>  
> **Deferred/excluded:** AESLC/Enron requires actual rights clearance; founder approval alone is insufficient. Stack Exchange remains excluded because CC BY-SA attribution/share-alike duties and current automated-ML access restrictions create unnecessary risk; there is no July-2024 content-license cutoff. WikiHow and TED-LIUM remain excluded for non-commercial/no-derivatives restrictions.

## 3. Q1 grounding

**Proceed.** The approach is technically sound:

- Keep Qwen3-4B.
- Keep the shipped prompt unchanged.
- Add targeted list/restraint data.
- Remove the known leaked families.
- Retrain with the production QLoRA recipe.
- Merge and quantize.
- Judge the actual Q5_K_M artifact through the exact Mac shipping path.

The revisions above change evidence discipline and source selection, not the modeling direction.

## 4. Q2 repo fact-check

### Generator and scorers

- The English generator exists, balances domains/types/counts, and screens prior inputs ([generate_eg1_english_list_benchmark.py:2](/Users/m4pro_sv/Developer/EnviousLabs/EnviousWispr-eg1-overnight-root/scripts/eval/generate_eg1_english_list_benchmark.py:2), [line 33](/Users/m4pro_sv/Developer/EnviousLabs/EnviousWispr-eg1-overnight-root/scripts/eval/generate_eg1_english_list_benchmark.py:33), [line 186](/Users/m4pro_sv/Developer/EnviousLabs/EnviousWispr-eg1-overnight-root/scripts/eval/generate_eg1_english_list_benchmark.py:186)).
- It currently generates synthetic **development** candidates. Its manifest explicitly says `training_eligible: false` ([line 839](/Users/m4pro_sv/Developer/EnviousLabs/EnviousWispr-eg1-overnight-root/scripts/eval/generate_eg1_english_list_benchmark.py:839)). Therefore, extending it for grounded training pairs is new work, not already-proven behavior.
- The sealed A/B scorer exists and implements net gain ≥8, McNemar p<0.05, zero new restraint false lists, and no increased item/scope loss ([score_eg1_english_list_ab.py:230](/Users/m4pro_sv/Developer/EnviousLabs/EnviousWispr-eg1-overnight-root/scripts/eval/score_eg1_english_list_ab.py:230)).
- The two-item scorer enforces exactly two list lines and audited required/forbidden content ([score_two_item_lists.py:2](/Users/m4pro_sv/Developer/EnviousLabs/EnviousWispr-eg1-overnight-root/scripts/eval/score_two_item_lists.py:2)).
- The general structure scorer measures activation, intended item count, and restraint false lists ([score_list_structure.py:66](/Users/m4pro_sv/Developer/EnviousLabs/EnviousWispr-eg1-overnight-root/scripts/eval/score_list_structure.py:66)).
- The novel scorer expressly says it is not semantic or release-quality proof ([score_eg1_english_list_novel.py:2](/Users/m4pro_sv/Developer/EnviousLabs/EnviousWispr-eg1-overnight-root/scripts/eval/score_eg1_english_list_novel.py:2)); the blind-review tooling supplies that separate gate.

### Leakage scanner

It exists and implements exact, token n-gram, character n-gram, and embedding comparison ([scan_eg1_multilingual_development_leakage.py:70](/Users/m4pro_sv/Developer/EnviousLabs/EnviousWispr-eg1-overnight-root/scripts/eval/scan_eg1_multilingual_development_leakage.py:70)). It compares input↔input, output↔output, and both cross-directions ([line 785](/Users/m4pro_sv/Developer/EnviousLabs/EnviousWispr-eg1-overnight-root/scripts/eval/scan_eg1_multilingual_development_leakage.py:785)).

Correction: its production backend currently returns `calibration_required_noncertifying`, not an automatic pass/fail release gate ([line 858](/Users/m4pro_sv/Developer/EnviousLabs/EnviousWispr-eg1-overnight-root/scripts/eval/scan_eg1_multilingual_development_leakage.py:858)).

Replace the leakage claim with:

> Reuse the existing multilingual leakage scanner’s exact, token n-gram, character n-gram, and four-axis embedding comparison. Before corpus generation, add only the minimum schema/role adapter needed for English training rows and calibrate its production thresholds against known positive and negative overlaps. A `calibration_required_noncertifying` receipt is not a passing leakage gate. Screen both input and output sides, plus exact source-document and semantic-family IDs.

### Training script

- No separate proven #1265 production trainer is committed in this checkout.
- `train_sft_v2.jsonl` exists but is gitignored, not committed.
- The committed multilingual trainer is not production-proven by a successful reproduction of the shipped artifact.

Replace §10’s training-script assumption with:

The committed `train_eg1_multilingual_qlora.py` already matches the named #1265 headline settings and older-Qwen QLoRA/response-only structure, but it has not reproduced the shipped Qwen3 artifact.
